## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-14 walkthrough follow-up batch plan (wave 2)

A second 14th wave (the walkthrough continued through the morning). 13 points reconciled
against current code — **Batches 108–112 now shipped too** (full write-up in
[`WALKTHROUGH_FEEDBACK.md`](../WALKTHROUGH_FEEDBACK.md), 2026-07-14 wave 2 section). One is
already fixed and one is partial:

- **Fixed:** the check-in progress now replaces the button in place with no inline brief
  (Batch 110, `CheckInPage.tsx:378`) — that observation predated the fix.
- **Partial:** Batch 110 removed the duplicate *date* above the verdict, but the personable
  status line still doubles it (`DashboardPage.tsx:733`) → Batch 115.

The rest → Batches 113–119:

1. **Holiday still leaks thermal where the scheduler-only gate (105) didn't reach** — the
   brief's thermal review + "pre-cool" action (`morning_analysis.py:1202/1226`) and Sleep's
   Last-night fan chart (`SleepPage.tsx:194`) all still show on a holiday; and the pre-cool
   action links to `/sleep` instead of `/environment` (`:1222`).
2. **Check-in CTAs don't clear** — Home's hero gates on `analysis` not `manualEntry`
   (`DashboardPage.tsx:739`, so Batch 97's async window still nags), and Sleep's "Add today's
   check-in" card shows *because* `hasSleepAccess` is true after check-in (`SleepPage.tsx:286`).
3. **Home doubles the verdict/greeting** (`:733` above `:740`) and stacks "You're all set"
   (`:770`) against a Today card saying the same.
4. **Read-aloud is still robotic** — Batch 111 picked the best *on-device* voice
   (`BriefListenControls.tsx:60`); the natural voice needs the deferred hosted-TTS path.
5. **Calendar chevrons page by month** (`SleepDateCalendar.tsx:40`), not day.
6. **Feedback is only a 3-way rating** (`FeedbackControl.tsx`) — Mark wants more specific.
7. **No follow-up conversation** on a brief — it answers only the single check-in question.

**Sequencing.** 113 (holiday-thermal) and 114 (check-in CTAs) are the most user-visible daily
annoyances — do first. 115 (Home de-dup) is quick. 116 (hosted voice) and 119 (follow-up chat)
are the two heavier, decision-gated pieces (both carry a health-data choice — a third-party
voice API, a conversational surface). 117 (calendar day-nav) + 118 (feedback vocabulary) are
small. Tiers per #19: all Mid. Decision numbers assigned at `/batch-start` (next free **#186**).

This project's CI doesn't build the Dockerfile, so the merge was paused for explicit go-ahead and the real Railway build was watched live: `piper-tts==1.4.2` + `onnxruntime` installed cleanly, both voice-model `curl` downloads succeeded, image built and passed its healthcheck. **Production on merge SHA `ba7b50997fbe23d50c5692bf9c69d80d57e5130f`:** Railway `/api/v1/health` served that SHA; web `/` returned `200`; unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` returned `401` both direct and via Vercel. **Still unverified:** actual synthesized audio quality — the build succeeding proves the model downloaded and Piper is installed, not that a live `POST /api/v1/tts/synthesize` call produces good audio; needs a real check with the Settings toggle on. **Voice/timeout hotfix shipped (DECISIONS #193, PR #141, squash `fe337dc`):** the real synthesize call Craig tried returned a 502 after 46.4s — `medium`-quality Piper synthesis exceeded the 45s timeout on Railway's CPU for a full brief. Voice moved to `en_GB-southern_english_female-low` (faster, gender change incidental to picking a name confidently believed to exist), timeout raised 45→60s. Watched live in the real Railway build: the new voice's `curl` download succeeded, confirming the guessed Hugging Face path was correct. **Production on merge SHA `fe337dcc2c2f5041b0978d7096e41cc08e2a4ae7`:** Railway `/api/v1/health` served that SHA; web `/` returned `200`; unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` returned `401`. **Still open:** whether `low`-tier synthesis now fits in 60s and how the resulting audio actually sounds — needs one more real check with the toggle on. **CSP fix shipped (DECISIONS #195, PR #143, squash `fa20b79`):** the backend was fine (verified valid, non-silent audio via `railway ssh`); the actual silence was `vercel.json`'s CSP missing `media-src`, defaulting to `default-src 'self'` which doesn't cover the `blob:` URLs used for hosted-audio playback. Added `media-src 'self' blob:`. **Voice-quality reversion shipped (DECISIONS #197, bundled into PR #144's squash `9cb43d6` due to a `git checkout` race between two concurrent sessions sharing a working directory — see STATUS.md for the merge-mechanics note):** Craig heard the `low`-quality voice for the first time after the CSP fix and reported it "very robotic". A live `railway ssh` benchmark with realistic full-brief-length text showed `low` (23.27s) wasn't dramatically faster than `medium` (33.83s) — reverted to `en_GB-alan-medium`, timeout raised to 90s. Verified live in production (health SHA `dd1d048` matches `origin/main`, `config.py` confirms `en_GB-alan-medium`). **Still open:** whether the voice now sounds acceptable and completes within 90s for a real brief — needs one more live check with Craig. |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-15 UX review batch plan

Craig's 2026-07-15 full-app UI/UX walkthrough — all 16 routes, verified on-device
against Mark's real production data on a live holiday rest day (audit artifact +
findings D1/P1–P7). Coverage is strong (14/16 user stories fully met, several
unusually well — verdict-first hero, the `copy.ts` plain-English system, one-tap
check-in); the gaps are in optimality, plus one real defect. Issues:

1. **Holiday self-contradiction (defect)** — on a live holiday morning the Brief
   ("Green — Rest Day (Holiday)", thermal omitted) and Sleep ("Holiday away", chart
   dormant, Batch 113) honour the holiday flag, but Home's Last-night sleep section
   still shows the live overnight room glance and the Climate tab shows the live room
   + active fan controls, and Home's hero reads "you're good to go tonight" (train
   framing, not rest). Home's `SleepSnapshotBody` renders `OvernightGlance` with
   `showOvernightGlance` defaulting true (`SleepSnapshotBody.tsx:26/40`);
   `EnvironmentPage.tsx` has no holiday gate; `personalStatusLine` (`copy.ts:55`)
   ignores rest/holiday.
2. **Metrics-vs-baselines lost its status column** — Mark named `Metric · Last night
   · Baseline · Status ✔/⚠` as the table he loves; `MetricComparisonTable.tsx`
   condensed it to 2 columns with the ✔/⚠ only on the age sub-line. The pattern he
   wants already exists on the same Sleep page in `SleepStageAgeTable` (`Stage · Last
   night · Healthy range · ✓/⚠`) and in the brief markdown's 4-column table.
3. **Home stacks too many prompts** — up to five attention blocks (feel recap,
   `FeedbackControl`, `UnviewedBriefCta`, `TodayActions`, `NextActionStrip`) between
   the verdict and the first section (`DashboardPage.tsx:757–789`); the amber "NEXT ·
   Review last night's sleep" button even duplicates the section right below it, and
   the daily rating ask sits under the verdict every morning.
4. **Coach memory is an internal panel, not Mark's** — the store meant to kill his
   handover doc is titled "Coach state", described as "the internal editor",
   admin-gated (showed "unavailable" for Mark on the live pass), and edited as raw
   JSON / plan-block / 13-week map (`CoachStatePage.tsx`). The thesis feature isn't
   Mark-facing.
5. **Sleep ⇄ Climate overlap** — the same 4-stat bedroom block renders on both Sleep's
   Tonight tab (`SleepPage.tsx`) and Climate (`EnvironmentPage.tsx`); the room chart
   lives on Sleep, the controls on Climate, a summary on both — users must learn where
   each fact lives.
6. **Week is an editor in a daily slot** — `WeekAheadPage` (titled "Plan") leads with a
   full editor (per-day add Cycle/Build ride/Weights/Flexibility, move/skip/remove/
   reset/restructure) in a primary tab for a user whose plan "won't change".
7. **Small type for a 57-year-old** — load-bearing readouts at 10–11px (the baseline
   "your normal" sub-line + "for your age" note in `MetricComparisonTable`, the verdict
   eyebrow in `VerdictHero`, the "More detail" label + mono eyebrows in `DashboardPage`).
8. **Presentation slips** — every analysis prompt self-titles "You are Garmin Coach"
   (`morning_analysis.py:83`, `post_workout_analysis.py:61`, `post_flexibility_analysis.py:48`,
   `brief_chat.py:52`) so the brief renders "Garmin Coach — <date>" inside a CheckMark
   shell; nav "Week" opens a page titled "Plan"; the bedroom fan surfaces its raw
   configured name ("Replacement 2 keeper").

Not batched: whether the check-in-gated verdict (no colour on a cold open until
check-in / the 09:30 backstop) matches Mark's "morning answer, always" expectation
(review P7) — a product question to confirm with Mark directly, not a build.

**Sequencing.** 121 (holiday consistency) is the correctness item — do first; the
holiday flag already reaches the client, so it's applying an existing gate to Home's
last-night glance + Climate + the hero line. 122 (status column) is the highest
"Mark will love it" win and reuses the in-house sleep-stage pattern. 123 (Home
de-stack) and 127 (type sizes) are quick frontend passes. 124 (Mark-facing coach
memory) is the largest — a new presentation layer plus a non-admin read — and 126
(right-size Week) both carry a `/batch-start` design decision. 125 (Sleep/Climate)
and 128 (presentation consistency) are small. Tiers per #19: all 🟢 Mid. Decision
numbers assigned at `/batch-start` (next free **#198**).

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-16 walkthrough re-run batch plan

A fresh expert UI/UX walkthrough on synced `origin/main` (after 121-128 shipped),
rendering Home/Brief/Sleep/Climate/Week with realistic data. Two earlier findings
were already fixed by shipped work (Week redesign #152, Sleep⇄Climate overlap #151);
the items below are the gaps that survived. All 🟢 Mid, frontend-only, no migration.
Decision numbers assigned at `/batch-start` (next free **#207**).

- **129** bundles the correctness/polish gaps into one pass: the metrics-table
  direction allow-list is incomplete (a good above-normal reading can render as a
  ⚠ warning), Climate repeats a single fan's status twice and prints
  "Mode unknown · Oscillation unknown", the workout subtitle leaks the raw enum
  ("Bike sweet spot"), tonight's prep actions use a "done"-style green check, and a
  couple of copy strings use developer jargon ("canonical", "rail one layer down").
- **130** carries the flow follow-up: surface a compact metrics-vs-baselines snapshot
  inside the Brief itself (Mark's stated favourite artifact, currently one tap away).
- **131** is design-pending — a longitudinal strength "watching-brief" (should he do
  more/less resistance work over time), his explicit ask that is still only partially
  met; needs a scope decision with Mark before building.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

add both (readiness higher-is-better, respiration higher-is-worse).<br>129.2 Climate de-dup — `BedroomBody.tsx` prints a single fan's live status on both the "Overnight autopilot" row and the "Bedroom fan" card, and shows literal "Mode unknown · Oscillation unknown"; make the autopilot row describe the toggle and hide the mode/oscillation line when unreported.<br>129.3 Copy — drop "canonical" (`EnvironmentPage.tsx`) and "rail one layer down" (`WeekAheadPage.tsx`), and replace the raw enum subtitle ("Bike sweet spot"/"Bike z2") with a shared `workoutTypeLabel` helper used by Home, Week, and Sleep.<br>129.4 Prep bullets — `SleepPrepBody.tsx` marks to-do prep actions with a green check that reads as "done"; use a neutral bullet.<br>129.5 Tests. | The daily reader never sees a good reading flagged as a warning, duplicated/"unknown" fan status, a raw workout enum, developer jargon, or a to-do dressed as done. | Shipped via PR #155, squash `8a58618` (Decision #207): `MetricComparisonTable.tsx` now maps `readiness_score` (higher-is-better) and `average_respiration` (higher-is-worse), so all 8 emitted baseline rows are direction-aware and a good above-normal reading no longer renders as ⚠; `BedroomBody.tsx` describes the autopilot toggle instead of repeating the fan's live status and hides the mode/oscillation line when unreported; a shared `workoutTypeLabel()` (`lib/workoutCategories.ts`) replaces three enum-prettifier copies (Home/Week/Sleep) so the subtitle reads "Sweet spot"/"Zone 2" not "Bike sweet spot"; "canonical" (`EnvironmentPage.tsx`) and "…rail one layer down" (`WeekAheadPage.tsx`) copy dropped; `SleepPrepBody.tsx` prep actions use a neutral bullet. **Frontend-only, no migration/API/schema/verdict change.** Web vitest **293 passed** (new: readiness/respiration direction cases, `workoutCategories.test.ts`, `BedroomBody.test.tsx`, `SleepPrepBody.test.tsx`), tsc + eslint clean, build clean. ## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-16 rest-day activity surfacing batch plan

Traced from a live case — Mark's holiday walk on 16 Jul ("South Lakeland Walking",
70 min, on the last day of an active 12–16 Jul holiday window that had skipped his
Sweet Spot bike). The *treatment* is already correct and stays: the walk is captured
as an `Activity`, read as advisory active-recovery (`post_walk`, ≥30 min / 3 km via
`is_deliberate_walk`), never counts as load, never touches the skipped session, and
rolls into the Trends walking-base volume. What's wrong is *placement*:

- **Home (Batch 132)** — the walk lives only inside the Today card (`DayPlanBody` →
  `PendingWorkoutCheckIns` before its read generates, `WalkReadList` after), which on a
  rest day is collapsed under "More detail": the rest-day primary section is `lastNight`
  (`homeSections.ts:39`) and the `nextAction` ladder has a rung for an unlogged *ride*
  (`firstUnloggedRide`→`log-ride`, `homeActions.ts:112`) but **none for a walk** — even
  though the push nudge ("Your walk is synced — check in and I'll read it") already fired.
  **132** adds the missing walk rung so the walk floats to the top *while it's fresh*
  (decided 2026-07-16: dynamic, not a static re-rank of sleep), then recedes once seen.
- **Week/Plan (Batch 133)** — it doesn't appear at all: the plan-schedule
  (`planScheduleDaySchema`, `schemas.ts:404`) and week-ahead (`weekAheadEnvelopeSchema`,
  `schemas.ts:1238`) payloads are planned-workouts-only, so a day he actually walked
  renders as a blank rest day. **133** surfaces the day's synced activities on the day
  (decided 2026-07-16: shown on Mark's read-first Week glance, kind + duration).

Both prominence calls were made by Craig on 2026-07-16 (recorded in the rows below),
open to Mark's later feedback — same posture as Batch 131. Making the walk *more* visible
does not conflict with rest-day integrity: acknowledging an activity is not the same as
prompting training, and the advisory/no-load treatment is untouched. **132** is
frontend-only; **133** adds a read-only field to two existing endpoints (no new ingestion,
no migration). Decision numbers assigned at `/batch-start` (next free **#212**,
after Batch 134 takes **#213**).

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 132 — Surface the walk on a rest-day Home~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 132.1 Walk rung in the Home action ladder — `nextAction` (`homeActions.ts:90`) resolves a ride's unlogged check-in (`firstUnloggedRide`→`log-ride`, `sectionKey:'today'`) but has no walk equivalent; add a parallel rung (new `NextAction.key`, e.g. `read-walk`) that reads the already-present payload (`pendingPostWorkoutActivities` with `activityKind==='walk'`, and/or a fresh `postWalkAnalyses` entry) so a synced walk becomes the top Home action once the required morning sleep-review step is done, floating and expanding the Today card that holds it — mirroring the ride precedent and the push nudge that already fires — and clears once the walk is read/seen (server `pendingPostWorkoutActivities` empties when the read generates; a per-day 'seen' flag mirrors `hasReviewedSleep`/`hasReviewedBrief` for the generated read). ## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-16 climate resumes on the last holiday day

Traced from the same 12–16 Jul holiday as Batches 132–133. The holiday form labels its
two dates "First day of holiday" / "Last day of holiday" (`HolidayPage.tsx:165`/`:175`),
and Mark travels home on the last day and sleeps in his own bed that night — so the night
of `end_date` is a *home* night, not an away night. Today the bedroom/climate subsystem
gates "away" **end-inclusive** (`active_holiday_window_for_date`, `holiday_pause.py:85`),
so on the last day the Climate tab shows "Holiday away — the room read and fan controls
stay dormant … Climate resumes {endDate}" — a line that already contradicts itself
(`EnvironmentPage.tsx:52`/`:58`) — and the overnight fan autopilot stays a dormant no-op
through the whole 21:30→00:00 pre-sleep slice on the very night he's back, only waking
after midnight once the local date rolls to `end_date+1` (`scheduler.py:1049`, window
`21:30→08:30`, `fan_control.py:46`). So he comes home to a hot room and the fan does
nothing for the first ~2.5 h — exactly the window that matters.

Craig confirmed (2026-07-16) scoping this to the **whole evening / "is he home tonight"
subsystem**, not just the fan: fan control, the bedtime thermal alert, the sleep-protocol
nudge, and the Climate/Tonight UI all resume on `end_date`. The fix is a one-night shift on
the *evening* side only — away nights become `[start, end-1]` (end-exclusive) for those
surfaces. The plan side is deliberately left inclusive: the last day stays a holiday **rest**
(the workout is still `skipped`, `_skip_workouts`), and the morning "last-night" reads —
Sleep's history tab (`SleepPage.tsx:217`) and the brief's thermal review
(`morning_analysis.py`, `insideHolidayWindow`) — correctly stay dormant on `end_date`
morning because they describe the away night just gone. Decision **#213** assigned at
`/batch-start`.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 134 — Climate/bedroom subsystem resumes on the last holiday day~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 134.1 Backend "away tonight" semantics — in `holiday_pause.py` add an **end-exclusive** helper `overnight_away_window_for_date(windows, d)` (with a `holiday_windows_away_overnight` mirror of `holiday_windows_covering_date`, and a service method `get_overnight_away_window_for_date`) returning the active window only for `start_date ≤ d < end_date`: Mark returns home the night of `end_date`, so the bedroom/climate subsystem is away only for the nights `[start, end-1]`. Leave the end-inclusive `active_holiday_window_for_date` / `holiday_windows_covering_date` untouched — the plan-rest and morning "last-night" reads still use them.<br>134.2 Evening/overnight scheduler jobs resume on `end_date` — repoint `run_fan_control` (`scheduler.py:1049`), the thermal half of `run_evening_monitoring_alerts` (`scheduler.py:230`), and `run_evening_sleep_nudge` (`scheduler.py:197`) from `get_active_window_for_date` to the end-exclusive `get_overnight_away_window_for_date`, so on the night of `end_date` the overnight fan autopilot reconciles the live room temp, the bedtime thermal check runs, and the sleep-protocol nudge fires — while the last away night (`end_date-1`) stays dormant. No change to fan thresholds, the wind-down, or the `_record_fan_state` chart tick.<br>134.3 Daily-loop seam + Climate/Tonight surfaces — `daily_loop.py` computes `overnight_away_window` alongside the existing inclusive `active_holiday_window`; `HolidayStateOut` (`routers/daily_loop.py:601`) + shared `dailyLoopHolidayStateSchema` (`schemas.ts:1066`) gain a backend-computed `awayTonight` (= end-exclusive away), mirroring how the holiday seam itself landed (Batch 105/109). Repoint the "tonight"/climate surfaces at `awayTonight`: EnvironmentPage Climate tab (`EnvironmentPage.tsx:41`), Home Tonight + Bedroom cards (`DashboardPage.tsx:696`/`:717`), the protect-sleep suppression (`homeActions.ts:92`), and Sleep's Tonight tab (`SleepPage.tsx:238`). Leave `isActive` (inclusive) driving the surfaces that describe the away night just gone — Sleep's Last-night/history tab (`SleepPage.tsx:217`) and the all-set suppression (`DashboardPage.tsx:758`, already `isRest || isActive`) — and leave the morning brief's inclusive `insideHolidayWindow` thermal-review gate untouched, so nothing keyed to "last night" flips on `end_date` morning.<br>134.4 Tests + DECISIONS #213 — pure-function boundary (`away_overnight` true at `start`, true at `end-1`, false at `end` and after; active-only identity); each scheduler job runs on `end_date` and stays dormant on `end_date-1`; `awayTonight` true `<end` / false on `end` on the payload; Climate tab live on `end_date` while the history tab stays dormant; refresh the now-required `holiday` fixtures the way Batch 109 did. Backend pytest/ruff/mypy + web + shared vitest/tsc/eslint/build + closeout smoke. | When Mark gets home on the last day of a holiday, the bedroom/climate subsystem is live *that night* — overnight fan autopilot, bedtime thermal alert, sleep-protocol nudge, and the Climate/Home-Tonight/Sleep-Tonight surfaces all resume on `end_date` — while the day stays a plan rest and the morning "last-night" reads stay dormant. | Shipped via PR #161, squash `709e72e`: the holiday seam now deliberately splits into inclusive `isActive` for the day / morning / last-night story and end-exclusive `awayTonight` for the evening bedroom-climate subsystem, so the overnight gates and tonight-facing Climate/Home/Sleep surfaces resume on `end_date` while the day itself stays holiday-rest and the morning-after surfaces stay dormant. `holiday.awayTonight` is now a documented shared-schema field distinct from inclusive `isActive`; **no migration**; the propose→approve→push contract, Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, and Red-never-VO2 are untouched. Local verification stayed green (backend focused pytest **80 passed / 29 skipped**, backend `ruff check` clean, scoped backend `mypy` clean, shared vitest **19 passed**, shared `tsc --noEmit` clean, web focused vitest **102 passed**, web `tsc --noEmit` clean, build clean). The first PR run caught one stale expectation (`awayTonight` on `2026-07-14`), fixed in follow-up commit `c1b1870`, after which both branch-push and PR-context CI waves passed all checks plus the Vercel preview. Production verified on Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` SHA `709e72efbe89a331011d8aae0c52a9e31678a1c0`, web `/`, `/environment`, and `/sleep` `200`, and unauthenticated `GET /api/v1/daily-loop` `401` both direct and via Vercel. The live signed-in holiday-return evening eyeball remains deferred to real use. |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-16 tap a workout for detail

Craig noticed the workouts on the Week view aren't clickable. The read-first glance
rows (`GlanceDayRow`, Mark's primary surface) and the admin editor rows (`WorkoutRow`)
are display-only, and there is no workout-detail route anywhere in `App.tsx`, so a tap
has nowhere to go — the plan rows are dead ends. Everything a detail view needs is
already on the plan-schedule payload (`planActionWorkoutSchema`: title, workout type,
planned duration, intensity target, status, source, `structuredWorkout`,
`outdoorDelivery`), so this is **frontend + shared-module only — no backend, no
migration**. The structured bike breakdown and power-profile renderer already exist
inside `StructuredWorkoutSheet` (Batch 88); rather than duplicate them, this batch
extracts the read-only parts into a shared module so the new detail sheet and the
existing editor draw the same ride shape. Decision **#214** assigned at `/batch-start`.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 135 — Tap a workout to open a detail sheet~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 135.1 Shared read model — extract the `structuredWorkout` blob parser, the segment→power-bars expander, and a new read-only per-segment summariser into `apps/web/src/lib/structuredWorkout.ts`, and the power-profile SVG into `apps/web/src/components/PowerProfilePreview.tsx`, both lifted from `StructuredWorkoutSheet.tsx` (which now imports them, keeping only its editor-specific validation/submit). The shared parser is *faithful* — it returns empty segments for an unstructured session rather than the editor's starting template, so a read-only surface can tell "has structure" from "none".<br>135.2 `WorkoutDetailSheet` — a new read-only bottom sheet (`apps/web/src/components/WorkoutDetailSheet.tsx`) on the existing `Sheet` primitive showing title, discipline label + icon, status (Completed / Skipped / To do), planned duration, intensity target, indoor/outdoor + Garmin-delivery state, and coach-planned-vs-you-added source; for a structured bike session it renders the reused power-profile preview + a warm-up / intervals / cool-down breakdown, and other sessions show an honest "No structured breakdown for this session." line. It never edits.<br>135.3 Wire tap-to-open on the Week view (`WeekAheadPage.tsx`) — the read-first glance rows become buttons that open the sheet (Mark's surface), and the admin editor rows get their title/summary block wrapped in a button opening the same sheet, leaving the existing Move / Edit structure / Skip / Remove controls untouched and un-nested. New `detailWorkout` page state; each row is an accessible `<button>` with a per-workout aria-label.<br>135.4 **Decided at `/batch-start`:** (a) scope = planned workouts on **both** the glance and the admin editor; the unplanned-activity chips (Batch 133) stay non-interactive — they carry only kind + duration and have no detail payload. (b) the sheet is **read-only** — editing stays in `StructuredWorkoutSheet` / Move / Skip / Remove; the detail sheet never writes.<br>135.5 Tests (`WorkoutDetailSheet.test.tsx`: structured-bike breakdown + power profile, metadata-only non-bike, completed status; `WeekAheadPage.test.tsx`: glance tap opens the sheet, editor tap opens the sheet without the move picker) + web vitest / tsc / eslint / build gates. | A workout on the Week view is tappable, opening a read-only detail sheet with the full session read — the structured breakdown for a ride, the metadata for the rest — so the plan rows are no longer dead ends. | Tapping any planned workout on the read-first glance opens a bottom detail sheet with title, discipline, status, planned duration, intensity target, source, indoor/outdoor + Garmin-delivery state, and — for a structured bike session — the interval breakdown + power-profile preview reusing the extracted renderer (not a duplicate); the same sheet is reachable from the admin editor rows without breaking their Move / Edit / Skip / Remove controls; the unplanned-activity chips stay non-interactive; frontend + shared-module refactor only, no API/shared-schema/migration change; Green/Amber/Red, #133, #135, Red-never-VO2 untouched; web vitest/tsc/eslint/build green. | Shipped via PR #162, squash `236dacb`: the Week view's display-only workout rows are now tappable into a read-only `WorkoutDetailSheet` on both the read-first glance and the admin editor rows (Move / Edit structure / Skip / Remove untouched), and the Batch 88 structured-ride blob parser + power-profile SVG were extracted into shared `apps/web/src/lib/structuredWorkout.ts` + `apps/web/src/components/PowerProfilePreview.tsx` so the editor and the new sheet draw one ride shape rather than two (the shared parser is faithful — empty segments for an unstructured session — with the editor's warm-up→steady→cool-down template left in the editor). **Frontend + shared-module refactor only, no API/shared-schema/migration**; ## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-16 tap a logged activity for detail

nothing happened. That is by design from Batch 135 — 135.4(a) deliberately scoped the
unplanned-activity chips (Batch 133's `+ Walk · 70 min` badges) as non-interactive — but the
expectation is fair: everything on the Week should be tappable. The Week payload carries only
the light `planActivitySchema` shape (kind, name, `durationMin`, `startUtc`), so Craig chose
(2026-07-16) a **basic-details** sheet over pulling richer activity stats (distance, HR) or the
coach's post-walk read onto the Week, which would need backend payload work. Frontend-only.

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-16 premium UI/UX polish pass

Craig asked for a final world-class UI/UX review of the whole web app to make sure it is
"optimal and the most premium it can be." The audit (code-grounded across `apps/web/src` —
tokens, app shell, primitives, Dashboard/Trends/Sleep, plus cross-cutting greps) found the
foundation already premium (WCAG-verified token ramps, deliberate motion vocabulary, unified
empty/error/offline states, `Input` already dodging the iOS focus-zoom trap, reduced-motion
respected almost everywhere). What remains is the last 5% — a handful of seams where the
polish is uneven, plus one real light-mode defect and one iOS field defect. Batch 137 captures
all of it as **one frontend-only polish pass**; a few items are genuine judgement calls and are
flagged **decide at `/batch-start`** rather than pre-decided. **Not started — planned only.**
Could be split (137.1–137.2 defects shipped first, 137.3–137.5 polish second) if Craig prefers.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 137 — Premium UI/UX polish pass (design review)~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 137.1 **Theme-aware charts (light-mode defect).** `TrendsPage.tsx` (`:209`–`:222`) and `BedroomOvernightChart.tsx` (`colors.*` at `:61`,`:71`,`:82`,`:96`,`:109`–`:168`) feed the **dark-only** `colors` object from `theme/tokens.ts` straight into recharts `stroke`/`fill`/`tick`, so in **light mode** the grid + axes render dark-teal (`#2E4C4E`) on white and axis labels use the dark muted grey. No chart file consults `useTheme`. Derive chart colours from `useTheme().resolved` (or read the live CSS vars via `getComputedStyle`) so grid / axis / line follow the active palette; the `<Tooltip contentStyle>` already uses `var(--*)` and is correct — leave it.<br>137.2 **Form-field correctness (iOS defect + drift).** `textarea.tsx:16` is `text-sm` (14 px) on every viewport → iOS Safari zooms the page on focus; match `input.tsx:27`'s `text-base sm:text-sm` (16 px on mobile, no zoom). `PinInput.tsx:95`–`:100` still uses the pre-Batch-52 `bg-surface border-border` treatment; move it onto the shared `controlFieldClassName` (`--control` fill + `--control-border` + focus ring, exported from `input.tsx`) so the login PIN cells sit at the same elevation as every other field.<br>137.3 **Unify the save affordance.** The purpose-built `SaveButton` primitive (`components/ui/save-button.tsx` — three-state, width-stable, animated check draw-in, reduced-motion aware, already unit-tested) has **zero imports**; the real save CTAs each hand-roll a label swap (`DashboardPage.tsx:1665`/`:1704`/`:1727`/`:2251`, `SettingsPage.tsx:100`/`:241`, `HolidayPage.tsx:149`/`:193`, `WeekAheadPage.tsx:984`, `BlockGeneratorPage.tsx:215`/`:368`, `PinResetPage.tsx:136`, `LoginPage.tsx:101`) and only `CheckInPage.tsx:439` shows a spinner — so pending/saved reads differently on every screen. Adopt `SaveButton` at these sites for one save language app-wide. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** convert every call site, or only the primary write flows (leaving one-shot auth submits like Login / PIN-reset as-is)? **Decided (Craig, 2026-07-17): async saves only.** **Build finding:** in this app nearly every save refetches and unmounts/switches its own button on success (Holiday pause→active card & resume→pause form, the Dashboard/Week confirm panels collapse, Check-in transitions to a generating view), so `SaveButton`'s headline Saved✓ can't show there — it would flash then unmount. So `SaveButton` was adopted only where the full idle→saving→saved lifecycle is genuinely visible — **Settings → Change PIN** — and the primitive gained an optional leading-`icon` prop + a `useSaveButtonState(isPending, isSuccess)` react-query adapter; the long-running (`Check-in`, `Generate block`), collapse-on-success (Dashboard/Week confirm actions) and navigate-away (Login, PIN-reset) buttons keep their existing spinner/label affordance, which is the correct fit. Broadening to a uniform *pending* affordance on the rest is offered as a follow-up.<br>137.4 **Loading + navigation + motion polish.** (a) `.animate-shimmer` (`index.css:376`, with a light/dark-tuned `--shimmer-stripe`) is defined but unused; `skeleton.tsx:12` uses a plain `animate-pulse` opacity blink. **Decide:** wire the shimmer sweep into `Skeleton` (more premium, infra already exists) or delete the dead utility. **Decided: wired the shimmer into `Skeleton`.** (b) No route scroll reset — `App.tsx` has no `ScrollRestoration`; add a `ScrollToTop` that resets to top on PUSH/REPLACE and is a no-op on POP/back (complementing `PageTransition`'s directional slide), so navigating into a route no longer lands mid-page. (c) `TabBar.tsx:53`/`:86`'s active-tab underline animates via framer `layoutId`+spring, which bypasses the CSS `prefers-reduced-motion` block (JS-driven layout animation); gate it with `useReducedMotion()` the way `PageTransition` and `SaveButton` already do.<br>137.5 **Toaster + pinch-zoom (decisions).** (a) `App.tsx:97` `<Toaster position="bottom-right" richColors>` floats toasts into the mobile thumb / bottom-tab-bar zone and uses sonner's own green/red instead of `--success`/`--error`; **decide** a bottom-centre, tab-bar-safe placement + token-aligned colours. **Decided: yes** — a new `AppToaster` component (bottom-centre, offset clear of the tab bar, `theme` tracking the in-app toggle, token left-accent colours, `richColors` dropped). (b) `index.html` viewport sets `maximum-scale=1.0, user-scalable=no`, disabling pinch-zoom (WCAG 1.4.4 concern — real for Mark as an older primary user); with inputs at 16 px (and textareas after 137.2) the focus-zoom rationale is gone. **Decide with Craig** whether to drop `user-scalable=no`. **Decided: dropped it** — pinch-zoom re-enabled.<br>137.6 **Tests + gates.** Chart colours swap with `resolved` theme (light vs dark); textarea font-size class; PinInput field classes; `SaveButton` idle→saving→saved at a converted call site; `ScrollToTop` resets on PUSH and no-ops on POP; TabBar indicator honours reduced motion. Web vitest / tsc / eslint / build green. | A final premium-polish pass from a UI/UX design review: charts that follow light/dark, form fields that don't zoom or drift from the control-field system, one unified save affordance, an agreed skeleton treatment, scroll-to-top on navigation, reduced-motion-safe tab motion, and revisited toaster / pinch-zoom choices. | Charts render palette-correct grid/axis/line in **both** themes; textareas are 16 px on mobile (no iOS focus-zoom) and the PIN cells use the shared control-field fill; the save/pending/saved affordance is identical across the converted call sites via `SaveButton`; skeleton loading uses one agreed treatment; navigating to a new route lands at the top while back preserves feel; the tab indicator respects reduced motion; the toaster placement/colour and `user-scalable` decisions are resolved with Craig. **Frontend-only — no API / shared-schema / migration**; verdict logic + advisory/no-load + Green/Amber/Red + #133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched; web vitest / tsc / eslint / build green. | Shipped via PR #164, squash `9e71731`: a frontend-only design-review polish pass. **137.1** theme-aware recharts (`lib/chartColors.ts` + `useChartColors()`) so `TrendsPage`/`BedroomOvernightChart` follow light/dark rather than freezing to the dark palette on white (the `<Tooltip>` `var()` styling was already correct and stays). **137.2** `Textarea` 16px on mobile (iOS focus-zoom fix), `PinInput` onto the shared `controlFieldClassName`. **137.3** `SaveButton` adopted at **Settings → Change PIN only** — build finding: nearly every save in this app refetches and unmounts/switches its own button on success (Holiday pause→active card, Dashboard/Week confirm panels collapse, Check-in→generating view), so the Saved✓ can't show (it would flash then unmount); the primitive gained an optional leading-`icon` prop + a `useSaveButtonState` react-query adapter, and the long-running / collapse-on-success / navigate-away buttons keep their existing spinner/label affordance (a uniform *pending* affordance on the rest is an offered follow-up). **137.4** `Skeleton` shimmer sweep, new `ScrollToTop` (resets on PUSH, no-ops on POP), `TabBar` active-underline `layoutId` spring gated on `useReducedMotion()`. **137.5** new `AppToaster` (bottom-centre, tab-bar-safe offset, in-app `theme`, token left-accent colours, `richColors` dropped) + `index.html` drops `maximum-scale/user-scalable=no` to re-enable pinch-zoom. **Frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration**; verdict/advisory/Green-Amber-Red/#133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched. New tests: `chartColors` (2), `save-button` (3), `useSaveButtonState` (2), `ScrollToTop` (1), `PinInput` (1), `skeleton` (1) + a Textarea-16px case in `controls.test.tsx`; web `tsc --noEmit` clean, eslint 0 errors (6 known warnings), production build clean (Node 20). Both branch-push and PR-context CI waves passed all 7 jobs — including `Unit tests (web + shared)` — plus the Vercel preview; the local full-suite `WeekAheadPage` cold-start `userEvent` 5000ms timeouts (pre-existing #122/#214 flakiness in an untouched file) did not recur in CI. Production on merge SHA `9e717312aa0205346e656673945335210a87f723`: Railway `/api/v1/health` served that SHA; web `/`, `/trends`, `/settings`, `/environment` returned `200` and the served viewport meta confirms the new build (no `user-scalable=no`); unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` returned `401` both direct and via the Vercel same-origin proxy. The on-device eyeball of the light-mode charts, the shimmer, the repositioned toasts, pinch-zoom, and the Change-PIN Saved✓ remains deferred to real use. |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-17 daily-loop freshness / stale-cache fix

Craig reported the app showing **yesterday's** brief on open. Investigation (no backend bug —
`_local_today` in `daily_loop.py` correctly uses the profile's IANA timezone, not raw UTC) traced
it to two caching layers stacking on a cold/slow-network PWA open: `sw.ts`'s `NetworkFirst` route
for `/api/v1/daily-loop` (`:57`–`:67`) falls back to its up-to-24h Workbox cache after only a
**5 s** timeout with no signal to the page that the response is stale; and `queryClient.ts`'s
`PersistQueryClientProvider` (`maxAge` 24 h, `staleTime` 60 s) paints that same stale response
instantly on launch. Neither layer is wrong to exist (they're the deliberate Batch 62.1
offline/fast-cold-open design) — the gap is that a stale hit is invisible: no banner, no forced
refetch, no manual escape hatch. The daily-loop envelope already carries `subjectDate`
(`packages/shared/src/schemas.ts:259`), so staleness is detectable **frontend-only**, no API/
schema change needed. While tracing this, a second, related bug turned up in the same problem
class: `WeekAheadPage.tsx:144` computes its "is this today?" highlight via
`new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)` — the UTC calendar date — which is wrong for a UK user
during BST (from ~23:00 UTC the local date has already rolled over while the UTC date hasn't),
so the Week view can highlight the wrong day exactly at the boundary this bug lives in. Bundled
into the same batch since both are "the app shows the wrong day" defects in adjacent code.
Craig also asked to fold in a related but separate request: push out the hard 09:30 morning
backstop (Decision #87/#158/#185) that force-generates the brief regardless of whether Mark has
actually stably woken, so a genuine lie-in doesn't get read on stale data — while keeping the
guarantee that he never ends up with zero brief on a day he doesn't engage, just later. This
bumps the batch tier to 🔴 High (scheduler/verdict-timing edge case) since the frontend items
alone were 🟢 Mid. **Not started — planned only.**

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 138 — Daily-loop freshness, stale-cache fix & later morning backstop~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 138.1 **Detect and surface staleness.** In `useDailyLoop`/`DashboardPage.tsx`, compare the served envelope's `data.subjectDate` against the actual local-today (derive from `player.timezone` via the same logic the backend uses, not raw browser locale, so client and server agree at the BST/GMT boundary) and expose an `isStale` flag. When stale, show a dismissible-but-persistent-until-fresh banner ("Showing {date}'s data — tap to refresh") rather than silently displaying an old brief with no indication. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** banner placement/copy and whether it's a top banner vs. inline card on the Home screen.<br>138.2 **Make the refresh actually fresh.** The banner's refresh action must bypass the Workbox cache (e.g. a cache-busting fetch or `cache: 'reload'`) and force a real `queryClient.refetchQueries(['daily-loop'])` — tapping it must not just re-serve the same 24h-old SW cache entry it's warning about. Also bump `sw.ts`'s `api-daily-loop` route `networkTimeoutSeconds` from 5s so a cold/slow mobile connection gets a fairer shot at a live response before falling back. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** the new timeout value (e.g. 8–10s) — trade-off between a snappier fallback and giving slow connections a real chance.<br>138.3 **Fix the UTC-vs-local "today" bug on Week.** `WeekAheadPage.tsx:144`'s `todayIso` must use the same local-today derivation as 138.1 (profile timezone, not `new Date().toISOString()`), so the "today" highlight can't land on the wrong day across the BST/GMT boundary.<br>138.4 **Push the morning backstop later (Craig, 2026-07-17: keep the guaranteed-verdict safety net, just move the deadline out).** Today `wake_detection.py`'s `BACKSTOP = time(9, 30)` force-fires `is_morning_ready` regardless of wake stability once the clock passes it (`past_backstop` short-circuits to `"fire"` even on unfinalized/no sleep data), and the separate `morning_backstop` APScheduler cron calls `run_morning_weather_sync` directly at 09:30 Europe/London as a belt-and-suspenders guarantee (Decision #87, refined by #158/#185) — calibrated to Mark's 363-night backfill (median wake 08:22, p90 ≈ 08:50, latest observed 09:24, `WINDOW_END = time(10, 0)`). Craig wants a later cutoff so a genuine lie-in doesn't get force-read on stale/unfinalized data. Move `BACKSTOP` and the `morning_backstop` cron's `hour`/`minute` later, and extend `WINDOW_END` in step so `wake_check`'s polling window still covers the new backstop time (the window-gate gives up if `now_local.time() > WINDOW_END` before ever reaching the backstop check) — the guarantee-a-verdict-by-X behaviour itself is unchanged, only pushed out. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** the new backstop/window-end times (e.g. `BACKSTOP = 10:30`, `WINDOW_END = 11:00`, keeping the ~30 min buffer between them) — Craig's call on how much later. Append a `DECISIONS.md` entry recording the retune (mirrors how #87 documents the original calibration).<br>138.5 **Tests.** Staleness banner shows when `subjectDate` ≠ local-today and hides once a fresh fetch lands; refresh action triggers a real (non-cached) refetch; `WeekAheadPage` today-highlight uses local-today across a UTC/BST boundary case (e.g. 2026-07-17 23:30 UTC = 2026-07-18 00:30 BST); `wake_detection.py` unit tests updated for the new `BACKSTOP`/`WINDOW_END` constants (existing exhaustive `is_morning_ready` matrix in `test_wake_detection.py` re-parametrized, not weakened); scheduler test confirms `morning_backstop` cron fires at the new time. Backend pytest/ruff/mypy + web vitest/tsc/eslint/build green. | The app never silently shows a stale day's brief without telling the user, gives them a one-tap real refresh, "today" is computed consistently (profile timezone) everywhere on the frontend, and a genuine sleep-in no longer gets force-read on stale data before he's actually stably awake — while still guaranteeing he never ends up with zero brief on a day he doesn't engage. | A cold/slow-network open that lands on cached daily-loop data shows a visible "showing {date}'s data" banner with a working refresh action that bypasses the service-worker cache; `sw.ts`'s daily-loop network timeout is increased from 5s; `WeekAheadPage`'s today-highlight uses the same local-today logic and no longer relies on `new Date().toISOString()`; `wake_detection.py`'s `BACKSTOP` and `WINDOW_END`, and the `morning_backstop` cron, move to a later, Craig-agreed time while still guaranteeing a verdict that day; a `DECISIONS.md` entry records the retune; **no API/shared-schema/migration beyond the constant/cron-time change**; verdict logic + Green/Amber/Red + #133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched; backend pytest/ruff/mypy + web vitest/tsc/eslint/build green. | Shipped via PR #165, squash `f23078b`: the PWA no longer silently opens on yesterday's brief — `DashboardPage` shows a `StaleDataNotice` (sibling to `OfflineNotice`) with a cache-bypassing **Refresh** when the served `subjectDate` isn't local-today, computed by a new `Intl`-based `localTodayIso(profileTz)` in `lib/dailyFlow.ts` (which also fixed `WeekAheadPage`'s UTC-date today-highlight at the BST boundary); `fetchDailyLoop`'s forced refresh passes `cache:'reload'` and the SW `api-daily-loop` timeout went 5s→10s. The morning backstop moved 09:30→11:00 (`wake_detection.WINDOW_END` 10:00→11:30, `morning_backstop` cron 11:00; stability guard/cadence/floors unchanged) so a genuine lie-in isn't force-read on stale data — only lie-in/watch-not-worn days move, still guaranteed a verdict (Decision #217). Decisions settled at `/batch-start` (Craig): 11:00 backstop, top notice + Refresh, 10s timeout. **Frontend + backstop constant/cron-time only; no API/shared-schema/migration**; Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, Red-never-VO2 untouched; `ARCHITECTURE.md` §sync-jobs/morning + `docs/designs/wake-triggered-morning.md` ticked to the new times. New tests: `dailyFlow.test.ts` (5), `DashboardPage.test.tsx` (+3), `WeekAheadPage.test.tsx` (+1), `test_wake_detection.py` (+1 & retuned), `test_scheduler.py` (backstop cron `hour='11'`); backend pytest 634 passed/227 skipped, ruff/`format`/mypy clean (113 files), web tsc/eslint clean, build clean (Node 20). CI's first wave caught a real regression the noisy local full-run masked — `fetchDailyLoop`'s new `init` param made the normal fetch two-arg, breaking `SleepPage`'s single-arg `apiFetch` assertion; fixed by keeping the normal path single-arg (only the forced refresh passes an init, amend `e3869e4`), after which both CI waves passed all 7 jobs + the Vercel preview. Production verified on merge SHA `f23078b723bb9e20960ef0616c5130eff4c9ad41`: Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both served it, web `/` `200`, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` `401` direct and via Vercel. On-device eyeball of the stale banner/refresh and the 11:00 backstop firing deferred to live use. |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-18 check-in edits lost to a warm-resume refetch

Mark reported his **check-in details aren't being saved**. Investigation found **no backend bug** — `DailyLoopService.upsert_manual_entry` (`services/daily_loop.py:232`) and the read-back `_manual_entry` (`:427`) are symmetric on `entry_date` and the write commits correctly; prod data confirms the write path works on days without an interfering refetch. The defect is in `CheckInPage.tsx`: the `useEffect([query.data])` at `:132` mirrors the server's `manualEntry` into local `manualForm` state on **every** change to the loaded data, with no guard for unsaved edits. On Mark's iOS home-screen PWA, `refetchOnWindowFocus` (`lib/queryClient.ts:22`) is enabled **and** deliberately widened to fire on `pageshow`/warm resume (`lib/resumeRefetch.ts`, installed at `App.tsx:77`), so any warm resume mid-check-in (screen unlock, a glance at a notification, an app-switch) refetches `['daily-loop']`; because a fresh envelope differs (new `meta.generatedAtUtc`), React Query's structural sharing yields a **new** `query.data` reference and the seed effect re-runs, resetting the form to the server's still-empty `manualEntry`. He then taps "Get today's brief" and an **empty** entry is saved. **Prod evidence:** his 2026-07-18 morning `manual_entry` was created 09:05:49 with every field null and the brief generated 47 s later (so the PUT fired, empty), versus 11/12 Jul where the row was created then updated ~1–47 min later with his real values (no interfering resume), and 7 Jul's two rows 8 s apart (one empty, one full) showing the same race from the other side. `resumeRefetch.ts:18` documents the exact contract — the widened refetch is "dirty-state-safe" only because editors preserve in-progress edits across it; `CheckInPage` was the one place that didn't. **Frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration.** **Not started at write time — planned then built same session.**

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 139 — Check-in edits survive a warm-resume refetch~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 139.1 **Guard the form-seed against background refetches.** In `CheckInPage.tsx`, add a `dirtyRef` set true on any edit — `setManual` and `toggleChip`, which together cover the subjective-feel buttons, the quick chips, and the feel / notes / BP / supplements / food inputs. The `useEffect([query.data])` that mirrors `data.manualEntry` into `manualForm` returns early when `dirtyRef.current` is set, so a focus / warm-resume refetch can no longer overwrite unsaved edits. A successful save resets `dirtyRef` in `saveMutation.onSuccess`, so the invalidation's refetch re-seeds the form cleanly from the just-stored values. Pristine-form seeding (first load, and an entry that arrives later — e.g. the 11:00 backstop generating while the page is open) is unchanged, since the guard only trips once he has edited.<br>139.2 **Tests.** A mid-edit background refetch of `['daily-loop']` returning a distinct envelope (new `meta.generatedAtUtc`, `manualEntry` still null — the real production structural-sharing condition) preserves the typed subjective score + chip, and the subsequent save PUTs those values, not empties (a regression test verified to fail without 139.1); a pristine form still adopts a server entry that arrives on a later refetch. Web vitest / tsc / eslint green. | His check-in always saves what he actually entered — a warm resume or focus refetch landing mid-check-in can never silently blank the form and persist an empty entry. | Typing into the check-in then a background `['daily-loop']` refetch preserves the entered values; saving after such a refetch sends the typed values in the PUT body, not empties; first-load / backstop seeding of a pristine form still works; **frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration**; verdict logic + Green/Amber/Red + #133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched; web vitest / tsc / eslint green. | Shipped via PR #166, squash `a6dc4ee`: `CheckInPage`'s form-seed `useEffect` (`:132`) now early-returns while a `dirtyRef` is set, so a `refetchOnWindowFocus` / `pageshow` warm-resume refetch landing mid-check-in can no longer blank Mark's unsaved edits and save an empty entry; the flag is set by `setManual` / `toggleChip` (covering the feel buttons, quick chips, and every free-text / BP field) and cleared on a successful save so the post-save invalidation re-seeds cleanly from the stored values. Root cause was frontend-only — `DailyLoopService.upsert_manual_entry` and the read-back `_manual_entry` are symmetric on `entry_date` and commit fine; prod showed his 2026-07-18 morning `manual_entry` written 09:05:49 all-null with the brief 47 s later. **Frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration**; verdict / Green-Amber-Red / #133 / #135 / Red-never-VO2 untouched; no `ARCHITECTURE.md` change (no roadmap/spec/data-model impact). New `CheckInPage.test.tsx` cases: a mid-edit refetch returning a distinct envelope (new `meta.generatedAtUtc`, `manualEntry` null — the real structural-sharing condition) preserves the typed score + chip and the save PUTs them, not empties (verified to fail without the guard), and a pristine form still adopts a later server entry; full `CheckInPage.test.tsx` 10 passed, web `tsc --noEmit` + eslint clean. Both CI waves (branch-push + PR-context) passed all jobs — Build web, ruff, migration check, security audit, mypy, pytest, **Unit tests (web + shared)** — plus the Vercel preview. Production verified on merge SHA `a6dc4ee34626376037cc0d9b361cd09393479a6e`: Railway + Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` both served that SHA, the Vercel production deployment is READY at the merge commit, web `/` and `/check-in` `200`, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` `401` both direct and via the Vercel proxy. On-device eyeball of a real iOS warm-resume mid-check-in deferred to Mark's live use (live-browser blocked on this Mac — [[ui-walkthrough-method]]); the discriminating unit test stands in. Follow-ups noted (not bundled): the empty-payload overwrite in `upsert_manual_entry` (an all-empty PUT still replaces a populated row) and morning-entry uniqueness (the 7 Jul duplicate rows). |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-18 post-workout re-read shows a stale RPE

Mark reported that after keying his post-ride **RPE as 7 by mistake**, changing it to **4**, and tapping
**"Read my workout" again**, the analysis **still read RPE 7**. Investigation found **no save or
generation bug** — `DailyLoopService.upsert_post_ride_checkin` writes rpe=4 and the endpoint calls
`generate_post_activity_read(..., force=True)`, which regenerates through
`PostWorkoutAnalysisService.generate_and_store`; that reads the latest check-in and produces a fresh
**RPE-4** read. The defect is on the **read path**: `generate_and_store` **never supersedes** the prior
analysis — every re-read (this check-in-first path *and* the hourly poll after a `PROMPT_VERSION` bump)
`session.add`s a *new* `Analysis` row, and there is no unique constraint on
`analyses (user_id, activity_id, analysis_type)`. Every other reader resolves "the current read" via
`latest_analysis_for_activity` (newest `generated_at_utc`), but `DailyLoopService._post_activity_analyses`
returned **every** analysis for the day, so a corrected ride's two rows [RPE 4 newer, RPE 7 older] both
reached the envelope. The frontend then rendered the stale one: `DashboardPage.tsx` built `rideByWorkoutId`
with `.set(plannedWorkoutId, ride)` over that newest-first list, so **last-write-wins left the *oldest*
(RPE 7) row** in the map for a planned ride (an unplanned ride showed both as duplicate cards). **Backend
read-path + a small frontend client-side guard; no API/shared-schema/migration; the `analyses` table and
`generate_and_store` insert semantics are untouched (historical rows are deliberately retained — feedback
links to a specific analysis id, and `PROMPT_VERSION` bumps regenerate through a new row).** **Built this
session — planned then built same session.**

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 140 — Post-workout re-read surfaces the latest analysis, not a stale duplicate~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 140.1 **Reduce the daily-loop snapshot to the latest analysis per activity (backend).** In `DailyLoopService._post_activity_analyses` (`services/daily_loop.py`), after the existing single collapsed query (ordered `Activity.start_utc desc, Analysis.generated_at_utc desc`), keep only the *first* row seen per `activity_id` within each analysis type — i.e. the newest read — dropping the older duplicate rows a re-read accumulates. This is the same latest-wins rule `latest_analysis_for_activity` already applies, moved to the one reader that lacked it, and fixes both the planned-ride map bug and the unplanned-ride duplicate cards at the payload boundary. Do **not** delete rows or add a unique constraint — historical analyses are retained on purpose (feedback FK, `PROMPT_VERSION` regeneration, reviews/history).<br>140.2 **Harden the frontend against duplicates (defense-in-depth).** In `DashboardPage.tsx`, dedupe `postWorkouts` to the newest read per `activityId` (`latestRides`, keeping the first occurrence of the newest-first list) *before* the planned/unplanned split, so the `rideByWorkoutId` map and `unplannedRides` can never resurface a stale read even if the payload ever carries a duplicate again — replacing the last-write-wins map that silently picked the oldest.<br>140.3 **Tests.** Backend: two post_workout analyses for one activity (RPE 7 then RPE 4) collapse to only the RPE-4 row in `_post_activity_analyses` (verified to fail without 140.1). Frontend: a planned ride whose payload carries a stale RPE-7 + fresh RPE-4 pair renders only the RPE-4 read behind a single "View analysis" (verified to fail without 140.2). Backend pytest/ruff/mypy + web vitest/tsc/eslint/build green. | A corrected post-workout read always shows the latest analysis — re-reading a ride after fixing its RPE (or any check-in field) surfaces the fresh read, never a stale duplicate, on both the planned-ride Today row and the standalone unplanned-ride section. | Re-reading a ride after correcting RPE 7→4 shows the RPE-4 read, not the RPE-7 one; the daily-loop payload carries at most one post-activity analysis per activity (the newest); no duplicate read cards for an unplanned ride; **backend read-path + frontend guard only, no API/shared-schema/migration**; the `analyses` table, `generate_and_store` insert semantics, verdict + Green/Amber/Red + #133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched; backend pytest/ruff/mypy + web vitest/tsc/eslint/build green. | Built on `claude/dads-feedback-mii8u3` (Decision #219). `_post_activity_analyses` now keeps the newest analysis per activity per type; `DashboardPage` dedupes rides to `latestRides` before the planned/unplanned split. Local gates green: backend pytest **862 passed** (Postgres-backed), ruff + `format --check` clean, mypy clean (113 files); web `tsc --noEmit` clean, `DashboardPage.test.tsx` **57 passed**, eslint clean on touched files, production build clean (Node 22). New `test_post_activity_analyses_keep_latest_per_activity` and the new `DashboardPage.test.tsx` case both verified to fail without their respective fix. **Shipped via PR #167, squash `c0fb581`:** both CI waves (branch-push run #836 + PR-context) passed all 7 jobs — Build web, ruff, migration check, security audit, mypy, pytest, **Unit tests (web + shared)** — plus the Vercel preview. Production verified on merge SHA `c0fb5817bad4b70cfa8a366cd3c7beb9f108dfea`: Railway `/api/v1/health` served `{"status":"ok","sha":"c0fb581…"}`, Vercel same-origin `/api/v1/health` served the same SHA, web `/` `200`, unauthenticated `/api/v1/daily-loop` `401` both direct and via Vercel. Docs closed out on `main` (`docs/phase-batches.md` struck + `Shipped`, `STATUS.md`, `DECISIONS.md` #219). On-device eyeball of a real corrected-RPE re-read deferred to live use; the two discriminating tests stand in. |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-21 brief-generation failure resilience & credit-balance alerting

On 2026-07-21 Mark reported the app **"frozen on writing today's brief."** Root cause was **not** a code
bug — the **Anthropic API account ran out of credits**, so every `POST https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages`
returned **400** `invalid_request_error` ("Your credit balance is too low…"). Brief generation *is* a Claude
call, so `_generate_brief_after_checkin` (`routers/daily_loop.py:160`) caught the `HTTPStatusError` raised by
`generate_anthropic_text` (`services/anthropic_text.py:52`, whose `response.raise_for_status()` also **discards
the error body**), logged `"morning check-in background generation failed"`, and returned — leaving **no
`morning` analysis row** for the day. The frontend has **no failure state**: `CheckInPage.tsx` renders the
`BriefGeneratingCta` "Writing your brief" and polls `['daily-loop']` every `BRIEF_READY_POLL_MS` while
`queuedAtMs` is set, so it waited **hours** for a brief that would never arrive — no error, no timeout, no retry
— and Mark had no signal anything was wrong (he checked in ~09:35 BST, retried ~13:35 BST, still nothing). The
outage was **silent to Craig** too: no alert fired; it surfaced only because Mark reported a frozen app (Craig
then topped up billing and the 2026-07-21 brief was regenerated out of band). Two resilience gaps this batch
closes, both around the *existing* LLM boundary — neither changes generation logic or the verdict: **(1)** a
brief-generation failure must resolve to an honest, retryable state instead of an infinite spinner; **(2)** an
Anthropic billing/credit failure must **alert the admin** so it's caught before a user reports it, with the
failure *reason* captured in the logs (recovering it this time needed a manual out-of-band API call). Tier
🟢 Mid — failure-state UI + a notification hook + a logging fix + tests. The one design fork — **how the client
learns a generation failed** (a client-side timeout/poll-cap vs. a backend-recorded failure signal on the
daily-loop envelope) — is settled at `/batch-start`, and if the backend-signal route is chosen (API/shared-schema,
maybe a persisted marker) it may pull the batch toward 🔴 High. **Decisions settled at `/batch-start` (Craig,
2026-07-21):** 141.1 → **backend failure signal** — a persisted per-`(user, subject_date)` `briefGeneration.status`
(`generating | ready | failed` + reason) on the daily-loop envelope, so the client shows an immediate, accurate,
retryable error (this lifts the batch to **🔴 High** and adds a small Alembic migration); 141.3 → **both** a
structured `error`-level log event *and* an admin push (the push half needs Craig seeded as a prod admin push
recipient; the log half works regardless). Trigger: first billing-classed failure, deduped once/day via the
existing `_already_recorded` pattern. **Building on `feat/batch-141-brief-failure-resilience` (Decision #220).**

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 141 — Brief-generation failure surfaces to the user + credit-balance alerting~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 141.1 **Surface a failed generation to the user (backend signal + frontend).** The check-in "Writing your brief" flow (`CheckInPage.tsx` + `BriefGeneratingCta.tsx`) must resolve to an honest failure state — a "Couldn't finish your brief — tap to try again" card with a working **Retry** — instead of polling `['daily-loop']` forever while `queuedAtMs` is set. Retry re-triggers generation the same way a check-in does (regenerate today), not just re-poll. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** how the client detects failure — (a) a **client-side timeout / poll-cap** (no `morning` analysis after N polls / T seconds ⇒ show failure; simplest, frontend-only, but can't distinguish genuinely-slow from failed) vs. (b) a **backend-recorded failure signal** the daily-loop envelope exposes (e.g. `briefGeneration.status = generating\|ready\|failed`), which the client reads for an immediate, accurate error + retry (honest, but adds API/shared-schema and likely a persisted/transient marker written by `_generate_brief_after_checkin`'s except branch).<br>141.2 **Capture the Anthropic error body for diagnosis (backend).** `generate_anthropic_text` (`services/anthropic_text.py:52`) calls `response.raise_for_status()`, which drops the response body — the "credit balance too low" reason was invisible in `railway logs` and had to be recovered with a manual API call. On a non-2xx, log the parsed Anthropic `error.type` / `error.message` (**never** the `x-api-key`) before raising, so the reason (credit balance / prompt-too-long / model / rate-limit) is in the logs. Raised exception type + call-site behaviour unchanged.<br>141.3 **Alert the admin on a billing/credit failure (backend).** When a brief or post-workout generation fails with the Anthropic credit/billing error (a 4xx `invalid_request_error` whose message indicates balance) — and/or when generation has failed repeatedly — notify Craig (admin) via the existing path (`NudgeAlertService` / web-push) and/or a structured error-log alert, so the outage is caught before a user reports it. Anthropic exposes no balance on the messages API, so the practical trigger is a **billing-classed failure**, not a proactive balance poll; enabling **auto-reload** in the Anthropic console is a complementary ops action for Craig, **not** a code phase. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** alert channel (admin push vs. log/observability only) and trigger (first billing-classed failure vs. an N-failures threshold) to avoid alert spam.<br>141.4 **Tests.** Frontend: a generation that never yields a `morning` analysis (or emits a `failed` signal, per 141.1's decision) resolves to the failure card, and Retry re-triggers generation — not an endless spinner (verified to fail without 141.1). Backend: a 400 credit-balance response is logged with its `error.message` and no key material, and is classified as billing so it raises an admin alert (per 141.3), while a non-billing 4xx does not alert (or alerts only past the threshold). **Deferred — Interval-level workout editing (Mark's 2026-07-23 feedback, point 5).** Editing today is a global duration%/intensity% dial (`apply_manual_override_to_ir`) that scales every step — warm-up, work, recovery, cool-down — uniformly, so he cannot restructure intervals (2×25 → 2×15) or adjust rests independently. A real per-interval editor is **design-first and Mark-led**; **his design notes landed 2026-07-24 and it is now specced as Batch 147 below.** Batch 145 fixes the most painful downstream symptom (mis-graded intervals) independently.

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-24 per-interval workout editor (Mark's 2026-07-23 feedback, point 5)

Mark's deferred interval-editor request (above) arrived with **design notes** on 2026-07-24: a per-interval table
(**No. of intervals**; **work** time / %FTP / cadence; **rest** time / %FTP / cadence), warm-up & cool-down **held
constant irrespective of the workout**, an app-suggested **"Change to"** column he can edit, and an **"Approve and
upload to Zwift"** button — with **Z2 collapsing to 1 interval, rest 0:00** since it's a single continuous effort.
His worked example is scaling/converting the VO2 session (`Warm-up 10 min 55→80%` · `3 min 72%` · `2 min 55%` ·
`5× (2 min @ 120% / 2 min @ 60%)` · `Cool-down 10 min 70→45%`) up/down, or into Sweet Spot or Z2, without touching
the warm-up/cool-down. Design pass reconciled the notes to code this session — findings: (1) today's only edit is
the global two-dial `apply_manual_override_to_ir` (`services/executable_coaching.py:224`), which scales **every**
step uniformly (warm-up/cool-down included) and can't change rep count, so none of his asks are expressible;
(2) the compact source `structured_workout` interval step (`{label, pattern:"5x 2min/2min", target, repeats,
cadenceRpm}`, `services/workout_delivery.py:848`) maps almost 1:1 to his 7-row table, but the string `pattern` +
single `cadenceRpm` **can't carry independent work/rest cadence or a first-class rest %FTP**, and recovery steps are
built with **cadence dropped** (`_expand_pattern`, `services/workout_delivery.py:884`) — so his rest cadence (75→70)
has no path today; (3) `phase` is label-derived (`_phase`, `:911`) so warm-up/cool-down are already identifiable to
hold constant, but the VO2 example's two "primer" steady steps (`3 min 72%`, `2 min 55%`) are phase `interval` and
have **no slot in his table**; (4) cadence already reaches the `.ZWO` (Zwift) file (`build_zwo_xml`, `:311`) though
not the Garmin export (`garmin_workout_export.py:88`, power-only); (5) the **`.ZWO`/intervals.icu/Garmin deliver
rails + propose→approve→push already exist** — the frontend lands rides via intervals.icu (`intervalsEventId`), so
"upload to Zwift" **is** the existing deliver path (ZWO carries cadence, reaches Zwift through intervals.icu).
**Design decision (closest to his notes): edit the source block, not the expanded IR** — add an *additive* structured
interval-block source form carrying first-class work/rest `{time, powerPct, cadenceRpm}`, re-expand → re-derive the
ZWO/intervals/Garmin proposal, and **pass warm-up, cool-down, and any non-block primer steps through untouched**.
`structured_workout` is JSONB so the new form is **additive — no migration**; the edit payload adds shared-schema/API
fields. Tier 🔴 High (new source form + expander branch + source⇄table mapper + suggestion generator + editor UI +
deliver wiring + the Z2 edge case, all around the existing LLM-free delivery boundary). **Not started — planned only.**

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 147 — Per-interval workout editor: edit reps / work / rest, warm-up & cool-down fixed, approve & upload~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 147.1 **Additive structured interval-block source schema.** Extend `_expand_step`/`_expand_pattern` in `services/workout_delivery.py` with a new block form — `{"label", "block": {"repeat", "work": {time, "powerPct", "cadenceRpm"}, "rest": {time, "powerPct", "cadenceRpm"}}}` — expanding to work+rest IR steps with **independent** cadence on both (closing the `cadence=None` recovery drop at `:884` so his rest cadence 75→70 finally has a path). Keep the legacy `pattern`/`repeats` + single `cadenceRpm` form expanding **unchanged** (existing plans/imports). A `rest` of `0:00` (or omitted) emits **no** recovery step and `repeat == 1` emits a single work step — the Z2 collapse. Mirror the new form in the shared Zod schema (additive/optional for pre-147 cache compatibility, per 141's rule). **Decide at `/batch-start`:** field names/units (minutes+seconds vs total seconds; `powerPct` absolute-%FTP vs a band).<br>147.2 **Source⇄table round-trip mapper (pure).** A DB-free mapper reads a planned ride's `structured_workout` and surfaces its editable interval block(s) as Mark's table rows (No. of intervals; work time/%FTP/cadence; rest time/%FTP/cadence), while listing warm-up, cool-down, and any non-block "primer" steady steps as **read-only pass-through** context. Applying an edit writes the block back into a fresh `structured_workout` and a **new `PlannedWorkout` version** (existing `uq_planned_workouts_user_date_version` versioning), leaving the warm-up/cool-down/primer step JSON **byte-identical**. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** whether v1 edits every block or only the primary block (Mark's current sessions are single-block).<br>147.3 **Suggested "Change to" pre-fill.** When the app proposes a change to a scheduled ride, pre-fill the editable column with a suggested alternative Mark can then edit: (a) **scale** the existing block, and (b) **convert to a named type — Sweet Spot or Z2** — from **deterministic, FTP-seeded templates** (not the LLM, for predictable/safe power), with Z2 setting `repeat=1`, rest `0:00`. Reuses the block schema from 147.1; no verdict/threshold logic touched. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** template defaults (e.g. Sweet Spot ~88–94% FTP work / Z2 ~65% FTP) and whether v1 ships scale + both named templates or a subset.<br>147.4 **"Approve & upload to Zwift" wiring + safety.** Route the edited block through the **existing** propose→approve→push proposal flow (edited block → new IR → new ZWO/intervals/Garmin proposal → approve → deliver); button copy in Mark's language ("Approve & upload to Zwift"), mechanism unchanged (lands via intervals.icu; ZWO carries cadence). Preserve **Red-never-VO2** at push (`blocks_red_vo2`, `services/executable_coaching.py:121`) so a hand-edited ≥VO2 block on a Red day is still blocked, and validate inputs to sane bounds. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** input control (steppers vs numeric fields) + exact validation ranges (reps, work/rest duration, work/rest %FTP, cadence rpm).<br>147.5 **Editor UI.** Replace the two-% dial with the per-interval table — Current vs "Change to" columns, warm-up/cool-down shown fixed/greyed, one-tap type presets (keep VO2 / →Sweet Spot / →Z2), and the Approve & upload button; mobile-first and one-tap-friendly, honouring the 139 warm-resume dirty-guard if it shares Check-in state. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** host surface (`DashboardPage` today-card vs the Week tab).<br>147.6 **Tests.** The new block form expands work+rest with independent cadence incl. rest cadence (previously dropped); a 1-rep/0-rest block yields a single work step and still passes `validate_deliverable_bike_workout` (warm-up+cool-down+ramp intact); the mapper round-trips Mark's VO2 session leaving warm-up/cool-down/primer steps byte-identical; a scaled block and a →Z2 conversion produce the expected IR; an edited ≥VO2 block is still blocked on a Red day; a legacy `pattern` plan expands unchanged (regression).
