## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-24 morning brief doesn't "join up" plan vs. swap vs. executed (Mark feedback)

Mark reported (2026-07-24) that the app **"doesn't seem to be joining things up."** He swapped Tuesday's hard VO2 to
Saturday **on the app's own recommendation** (it judged him not ready for VO2 on Tuesday), yet today's morning brief in
more than one place narrates the week as if he **completed VO2 on Tuesday** — e.g. *"…meaning Tuesday's VO2 and
yesterday's sweet-spot block have accumulated but haven't pushed you into a ramp-risk zone."* His diagnosis: to be
trustworthy the coach has to remember **what it recommended, what got changed, and what he actually did**, and ground the
read in the last of those. Design pass reconciled this to code — **root cause:** the morning brief is a single LLM call
whose entire world is the JSON packet (`build_morning_user_prompt`, `services/morning_analysis.py:713`, dumps the packet
verbatim as the prompt), and that packet carries **no factual day-by-day record of the training week**. Its only
history surfaces are today-only planned workouts (`_planned_workouts`, `:556`, `workout_date == subject_date`),
yesterday's aggregate load (`_yesterday_load`, `:598`), weekly-mix **bucket counts** (`services/weekly_mix.py` — not
attached to any weekday), Garmin's own acute:chronic numbers, and — critically — the **static nominal weekly template**
`knowledgeBase.trainingSchedule` (fed at `:381`; content at `services/coaching_state.py:177`, literally
`"Tuesday": "VO2 or higher-intensity bike focus"`). With no factual week to read, the model reconstructs "what's
accumulated" from that nominal routine + the aggregate counts and pins the sessions onto their **template** days — so it
credits "Tuesday's VO2" even though *this* week the VO2 was moved to Saturday and Tuesday was easy. The system prompt
reinforces the template as live truth ("Respect the trainingSchedule rest days", `:122`) and nowhere tells the model it
is a *nominal routine, not this week's record*. **All three of Mark's signals already exist in the DB, just never
assembled for the brief:** (1) **recommended** — `verdict.swapSuggestion` at generation time; (2) **changed** — `swap_day`
(`services/executable_coaching.py:1005`) versions the rows (old `is_active=False`, new re-slotted at the swapped dates)
**and** writes a `workout_moved` audit `Analysis` row carrying a human summary ("Swapped 2026-07-21 and 2026-07-25.") +
the planned-workout linkage (`_record_action_audit`, `:1235`) — one of a full set of change audits
(`workout_moved`/`_skipped`/`_removed`/`_replaced`, `:68`–`76`); (3) **executed** — `Activity` rows
(`models/coaching.py:100`) with real `training_load`, and `PlannedWorkout.status` completed/skipped (`:353`). **Design
decision: assemble a factual "training week so far" — planned → changed → executed, per day — from those existing rows,
feed *that* to the brief, and demote the nominal `trainingSchedule` in the prompt to "the usual routine, not this week's
record."** All reads of existing tables — **backend-only, no migration**; the packet gains a section and the prompt a
rule + a `PROMPT_VERSION` bump. Distinct from the deferred "rearrange the whole week in one tap" restructure UI and the
Batch 147 interval editor — this is about **memory/grounding**, not editing; it complements the executed-vs-planned
thread (Batch 145 grading on executed boundaries, Batch 80 deviation verdict). Tier 🔴 High (new cross-table history
assembler feeding the LLM read + prompt/`PROMPT_VERSION` change). **Shipped via PR #175 / squash `8e249c5`; the as-built policy is recorded in DECISIONS #227.**

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 148 — Ground the morning brief in the real training week (recommended → changed → executed), not the nominal routine~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 148.1 **Assemble the factual "training week so far" (backend, read-only).** A new read-only builder (e.g. a `TrainingWeekService` / helper feeding `morning_analysis.py`) that, over a recent window, produces a **per-day** record joining: the day's **planned** session(s) (type / title / status from the active `PlannedWorkout` versions); **what changed** (moved-in / moved-out / skipped / removed, from the `workout_moved`/`_skipped`/`_removed`/`_replaced` audit `Analysis` rows, which carry a summary + planned-workout linkage); and **what was actually executed** (matched `Activity` with `training_load` + key power/TE, or an explicit "skipped" / "rest day"). So a swapped-away VO2 reads *planned VO2 → moved to Sat* and its Tuesday shows the easy ride he actually did — never a phantom completed VO2. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** (a) window — rolling **last 7 days** vs **calendar-week-to-date** (Mon→today, matching `weekly_mix`'s `week_start`); (b) reconstruct "changed" from the **audit rows** (explicit, carry a summary, can distinguish app-recommended vs Mark-initiated) vs from `PlannedWorkout` **version history** (inactive rows) — prefer audits; (c) whether v1 attributes "**you took the app's suggestion**" (the *recommended* leg of Mark's ask) or ships planned→changed→executed factually first and defers attribution.<br>148.2 **Feed it to the brief and demote the nominal routine (packet + prompt).** Add the assembled record to the morning packet (e.g. `trainingWeekSoFar`) beside the existing `plannedWorkouts`/`yesterdayLoad`; in `SYSTEM_PROMPT` add a grounding rule — narrate "what you've done / what's accumulated this week" **strictly from `trainingWeekSoFar`**, never from `knowledgeBase.trainingSchedule` (which describes the **usual** routine — which day *typically* holds which session — not what happened); never credit a **moved-away** session as executed, and where it helps, acknowledge a session as *moved* (Mark's swap). Bump `PROMPT_VERSION` so stale reads regenerate. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** whether v1 also applies the same demotion/grounding to the **weekly-review** narrative (`services/reviews.py` feeds the identical `trainingSchedule` at `:1023`, so the same nominal-template-as-truth confabulation can surface there) or defers the review surface to a follow-up.<br>148.3 **Tests.** A week where Tuesday's planned VO2 was swapped to Saturday (a `workout_moved` audit + versioned rows) and Tuesday's real `Activity` is an easy Z2 ride renders in `trainingWeekSoFar` as Tue = *planned VO2 → moved to Sat → executed easy Z2 (load X)* and Sat = the VO2, with **no** "Tuesday VO2 completed" anywhere (verified to fail without 148.1); a skipped day shows skipped, not the template's session; a plain unchanged week reads the sessions on the days actually executed; the packet carries `trainingWeekSoFar` and the prompt no longer instructs the model to read history off `trainingSchedule`. Backend pytest/ruff/mypy green. | The morning brief's load / "what you've done this week" narrative is grounded in what Mark **actually did** (and what got **changed** from what was **recommended**) — so a session he swapped away is never narrated as completed, and the coach visibly "joins up" recommended → changed → executed instead of reciting the nominal weekly routine. | The packet carries a factual per-day `trainingWeekSoFar` joining planned → changed (moved/skipped/removed audits) → executed (`Activity` load / status) over the window; the system prompt narrates accumulated load from that record and treats `knowledgeBase.trainingSchedule` as the nominal routine, not this week's history; a swapped-away VO2 never appears as a completed Tuesday VO2 in the read; `PROMPT_VERSION` is bumped; **verdict logic + Green/Amber/Red + #133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched; backend-only, all reads of existing tables, no migration / no shared-schema change**; backend pytest/ruff/mypy green. Surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-24 feedback; complements Batch 145 (grading on executed boundaries) and Batch 80 (deviation verdict); distinct from the deferred whole-week restructure UI and the Batch 147 interval editor. **Shipped via PR #175, squash `8e249c5`: both CI waves and the Vercel preview passed; post-merge `main` CI passed; Railway + Vercel same-origin health served the exact merge SHA; web returned 200; and the morning-loop plus weekly-review routes returned 401 direct and proxied.** |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-24 Week starts on Monday (Mark feedback)

Mark wants the Week tab to read as a stable calendar week beginning on **Monday**, rather
than as seven days beginning whenever he opens it. The current page does not actually show
a calendar week: `WeekAheadPage.fetchSchedule()` requests
`GET /api/v1/plan-actions/schedule?days=14` without `start_date`, the route defaults the
range to profile-local today, and the frontend then labels `schedule.slice(0, 7)` as
**This week** and `slice(7)` as **Coming up**. On Friday 24 July that makes "This week"
Friday 24→Thursday 30 July and "Coming up" Friday 31 July→Thursday 6 August — stable
seven-day chunks, but not weeks.

The backend already exposes the needed seam: the schedule route accepts an explicit
`start_date`, `PlanActionService.schedule()` is range-generic, and the shared response
already reports `startDate`; no endpoint, schema, database or plan-engine change is
needed. The frontend also already has both correct inputs: `localTodayIso(player.timezone)`
for the profile-local calendar day (Batch 138) and Monday arithmetic in
`weekStartForDate()` for whole-week restructure. **Settled with Craig (2026-07-24):**
Monday is the fixed anchor. Keep the existing 14-day payload as **two complete calendar
weeks** — current Monday→Sunday under **This week**, then next Monday→Sunday under
**Coming up** — rather than preserving 14 future days. This brings the visible Week tab
into line with `weekly_mix`, weekly reviews and Batch 148's factual training history, all
of which already define a training week from Monday.

One existing interaction makes a naive query-only change unsafe. The admin-only
**Edit week** organiser and `MoveWorkoutSheet` consume the same fetched dates; a
Monday-anchored window includes elapsed days from earlier in the current week, and today
the sheet disables only the workout's current date. Those historical dates must remain
visible as the honest record Mark asked for, but must not become new write targets:
past-day add/build/move/edit/skip/remove controls are unavailable, and past dates cannot
be selected in the move sheet. The current week's banner-level Reset/Restore/Rearrange
controls stay available even though its header now sits on Monday — those actions are
week-scoped, not attempts to rewrite the elapsed Monday. Today and future dates retain
the existing day controls. This deliberately refines Decision #103's *rolling* 14-day
window while preserving its core promise — a tap-to-move picker can still push a workout
anywhere in the whole next calendar week. Decision number assigned at `/batch-start`
(implemented as **#229** because local docs had already claimed #228). **Shipped
via PR #177 / squash `546070f`; production verified.**

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 149 — Anchor the Week view to Monday~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 149.1 **Profile-local Monday anchor.** Derive the current week's Monday from `localTodayIso(player.timezone)` using date-only/UTC-safe calendar arithmetic (reuse/extract `weekStartForDate`; never raw `new Date().toISOString()` or browser-locale weekday math), include it in the query key, and fetch `/api/v1/plan-actions/schedule?start_date={monday}&days=14`. Keep the API's generic today-default unchanged for other callers.<br>149.2 **Two real calendar weeks.** Keep the existing first-seven/second-seven presentation, but its inputs are now guaranteed to be current Mon→Sun (**This week**) and next Mon→Sun (**Coming up**). Today retains its existing profile-timezone badge; planned-session/done/to-do totals cover the whole Monday-start week, completed planned work from earlier days counts as done, and Batch 133's logged-activity chips remain visible under their existing no-double-count rule (unplanned activities do not silently become planned-session totals). Example regression: on Fri 2026-07-24 the payload starts Mon 2026-07-20, This week ends Sun 2026-07-26, and Coming up is Mon 2026-07-27→Sun 2026-08-02.<br>149.3 **Past-day write guard in the admin organiser.** Elapsed days earlier than profile-local today remain visible and tappable for the existing read-only workout/activity detail sheets, but expose no day/workout add/build/move/edit/skip/remove mutation. The move sheet either omits or disables target dates `< today` with an explicit unavailable state; today, future days, and the source workout's current-day disabling keep their existing behavior. Preserve current-week Reset/Restore/Rearrange on its Monday header because those actions target the containing week, not the elapsed header date; preserve Decision #103's one tap-to-move sheet and next-calendar-week reach; do not widen the API beyond its existing 14-day cap.<br>149.4 **Tests + decision record.** Update `WeekAheadPage.test.tsx` request mocks for the explicit `start_date`; add Friday and Sunday cases proving Monday anchoring/two Mon→Sun groups, a BST/UTC-boundary case proving the anchor follows the profile-local date, planned totals/completed history plus existing activity chips from elapsed current-week days, and an admin case proving past days are readable but their day/workout mutations and move targets are unavailable while today/future controls and current-week banner actions still work. Append DECISIONS #229 at implementation to record the fixed-Monday refinement of #103. Web vitest/tsc/eslint/build green. | Make Week mean a predictable calendar week: every visit begins on Monday, shows the whole current week in context, and previews the whole next week instead of shifting both seven-day groups forward every day. | Week requests a profile-local Monday `start_date` and renders **This week = Mon→Sun** plus **Coming up = next Mon→Sun**; today is still highlighted correctly at BST/GMT boundaries; elapsed days remain in the record, completed planned sessions count toward done, and existing logged activities remain visible, but elapsed dates cannot be mutated or selected as move targets; today/future day controls, current-week banner actions and the one-tap move sheet remain intact through the end of next week; **frontend-only, no API/shared-schema/migration/plan-engine change**; verdict logic + Green/Amber/Red + #133/#135/Red-never-VO2 untouched; web vitest/tsc/eslint/build green. Surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-24 feedback and aligned with Batch 148's Monday-start factual training week. **Shipped via PR #177, squash `546070f`: branch-push, PR-context, and post-merge main CI all passed all 7 jobs; Vercel preview passed; Railway + Vercel same-origin health served the exact merge SHA; web `/` returned 200; and the explicit Monday schedule route returned 401 unauthenticated via Vercel.** |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-24 — Conversational learning batch plan

Craig's 2026-07-24 requirement: the coach should learn **durably** from its
conversations with Mark — distilling standing facts, preferences, terminology,
and recurring themes out of the follow-up chat, check-in notes, post-workout
notes, and corrections, and folding them into the persistent, editable knowledge
base so context compounds instead of each exchange being forgotten. This
completes the product thesis ("hold that knowledge as living, editable state so
he never writes a handover doc again") and goes beyond the two adjacent loops
that deliberately stop short of it: the brief follow-up chat (Batch 119) is
grounded only in one brief's `context_packet` and makes no durable claim, and the
rate-&-correct loop (`recent_corrections` / `RecentCorrection.to_packet()`) feeds
a correction forward into the next generation's packet only, never into standing
state.

**Sequencing (Craig's call).** Option B first, then learning. Today the only
threaded chat is on the **morning** brief (`BriefFollowUpChat` mounted on
`MorningBriefPage`); after a workout there is no chat, only a one-shot check-in
note plus a single answered question. **Batch 150** gives the post-workout read
its own follow-up chat so a second conversational surface exists; **Batch 151**
then learns from **any** chat — so the learning covers both surfaces from day one
rather than shipping morning-only and revisiting. Batch 150 extends an existing
rail (the chat endpoint is already keyed to any `analysis_id`); Batch 151 is the
substantive analysis-engine + knowledge-base + coaching-integrity work.

**Hard guardrail carried by both.** Learning must never become a lever on the
verdict — the 2026-07-10 coaching-integrity audit named sycophancy/gaming the top
acute risk, and a naive "learn from what he says" loop is exactly that failure
mode. Green/Amber/Red (#133/#135), Red-never-VO2, and the data-quality rules stay
data-grounded and unchanged; learned items are durable facts/preferences only,
surfaced as editable KB entries Mark/Craig confirm (extract-and-confirm),
consistent with the Mark-facing Coach-memory read (#202) — never silently
applied. Decision numbers assigned at each `/batch-start`. This plan decomposes
the requirement recorded as DECISIONS #228.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 150 — Post-workout follow-up chat~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 150.1 **Reuse the analysis-agnostic chat endpoint.** `brief_messages` + `GET/POST /api/v1/briefs/{analysis_id}/messages` (`routers/brief_chat.py`) are already keyed to any `analysis_id` (user-scoped 404/403), and a post-workout `analyses` row already stores the `context_packet` + `output_markdown` that `BriefChatService.ask` grounds on — so **no new table/endpoint/migration**. Verify the post-workout packet is sufficient to answer from and that history/turn-cap/`_packet_json` paths are packet-shape-agnostic.<br>150.2 **Generalize the chat prompt across read types.** `brief_chat.SYSTEM_PROMPT` is morning-worded ("a brief you already wrote"); make it speak to "a read you already wrote" (or a per-`analysis_type` variant) while keeping every floor identical — never VO2 on Red, no L/R balance, local times, never narrate a skipped/holiday session as live.<br>150.3 **Scope the adjustment-proposal affordance to the morning only.** `_wants_adjustment` + `_todays_adjustable_workout_id` read the morning packet's `plannedWorkouts`/`restDay` to offer "propose today's ride" — meaningless once the session is done; gate that affordance to morning analyses so a post-workout chat is advisory-only (it already returns None on a non-morning packet — make that explicit + tested). **Decide at `/batch-start`:** advisory-only (recommended) vs. letting a post-workout chat propose *tomorrow's* adjustment.<br>150.4 **Mount the chat on the post-workout surface + tests.** Add `BriefFollowUpChat analysisId={analysis.id}` to the post-workout read disclosure on `DashboardPage.tsx` (the `postWorkoutAnalyses` row, ~:1852); the component already takes only `analysisId`. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** Home disclosure only vs. also any dedicated read view. Tests: a post-workout read threads a follow-up answered from its own packet; guardrails hold; the proposal affordance is absent on a post-workout chat; turn cap + 403/404 ownership hold; the chat renders on the post-workout surface. | Let Mark converse with his coach about a finished session — "why", "what does that mean for tomorrow", "was that too hard" — the same threaded follow-up the morning brief already has, instead of a one-shot check-in note. | Shipped via PR #179, squash `fc4203e` (Decision #230): the existing analysis-keyed `brief_messages` table and `/api/v1/briefs/{analysis_id}/messages` endpoint now support post-workout reads without a new table, endpoint, migration, or shared schema change. The chat prompt and UI copy speak about a generic "read", the prompt includes `Read type`, and the deterministic adjustment-proposal affordance is explicitly gated to `analysis_type == "morning"`, so post-workout chat is advisory-only while the morning brief's existing proposal path remains intact. Home mounts `BriefFollowUpChat` inside the completed-ride read disclosure; v1 adds no dedicated read page. Shared floors hold identically (Red-never-VO2, no L/R balance, local times, no skipped/holiday-as-live); Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, Red-never-VO2, and the morning brief's own chat are untouched. Local gates: backend pytest 665 passed / 236 skipped, backend Ruff/format/mypy clean, targeted web 61 passed, isolated WeekAhead 22 passed, web tsc/eslint/build clean; full web vitest hit the known WeekAhead full-suite timeout flake (350 passed / 2 timed out). Branch-push, PR-context, and post-merge main CI all passed all 7 jobs; Vercel preview passed; Railway + Vercel same-origin health served the exact merge SHA; web `/` + `/brief` returned 200; unauthenticated chat messages returned 401 direct and via Vercel. |
| ~~Batch 151 — Learn durably from any chat~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 151.1 **Distil durable knowledge from conversations.** A deterministic-triggered pass reads recent `brief_messages` across all analysis types (morning + post-workout after Batch 150), plus check-in/post-workout note text and corrections, and calls the shared Anthropic boundary (`anthropic_text.py`) to extract **candidate durable items** — facts / preferences / terminology / recurring themes — each with its evidence (source analysis + date) and a proposed KB destination. Strictly-typed output; the extractor is prompt- **and** code-constrained so it can never emit anything about verdict thresholds, the data-quality rules, or Red-never-VO2.<br>151.2 **Durable-vs-transient taxonomy.** Keep standing facts/preferences/patterns (equipment names, foods that disagree with him, schedule constraints, stated goals, chronic niggles); never persist transient state (today's mood/fatigue/one-off soreness). Encode the taxonomy as an explicit, tested filter — including the sycophancy trap: a conversation full of "I feel great / just tell me I'm fine" yields **no** verdict-affecting learning.<br>151.3 **Extract-and-confirm storage + review.** Candidates land as **pending, editable proposals** Mark/Craig accept/edit/reject — reusing the Mark-facing Coach-memory read (#202) and the existing KB editing rail; accepted items become real `knowledge_base` entries (or structured profile fields) that feed the morning/post-workout packets like the rest of the KB. Nothing auto-applies. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** storage destination (new KB sections vs profile fields vs the experiment/hypothesis tracker for recurring themes) and whether a pending-proposals store needs a migration.<br>151.4 **Trigger, privacy, tests.** **Decide at `/batch-start`:** a batched periodic distillation pass (after each brief, or a daily/weekly job) vs per-turn. Same paid-API / no-third-party stance; the pass runs on our own infra; RLS on any new store. Tests: extraction shape, taxonomy filtering, confirm-before-apply (no silent write), the verdict-guardrail invariants, and that accepted items flow into the next packet. | Turn Mark's conversations into the coach's living memory — the app distils durable facts/preferences/themes from any chat and folds them, on confirmation, into the persistent knowledge base, so it stops forgetting what he tells it. | Shipped via PR #180, squash `071992e` (Decision #231): an explicit Coach-memory action batches the last 30 days of user-authored chat across every analysis type plus check-in/post-workout notes and corrections. Strict typed extraction must cite exact source evidence; code independently rejects unverifiable quotes, transient state, duplicates, sycophantic/verdict pressure, Red/VO2 rules, thresholds, and known data-quality rules. Migration `021` adds user-scoped, RLS-backed `conversation_learning_proposals`; Mark/Craig can edit then accept or reject, and only accepted items version `knowledge_base.section='learned_context'`. Morning and ride/strength/flexibility/walk packets receive confirmed items as `learnedContext` with `classificationImpact='none'`, so Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, Red-never-VO2, thresholds, data-quality rules, and objective metrics remain unreachable from the learning write path. The first branch CI run exposed a mypy 2.3 SQLAlchemy inference issue, fixed in `6173e14`; subsequent branch-push, PR-context, and post-merge main CI passed all 7 jobs, including Postgres lifecycle/packet tests and Alembic upgrade/downgrade, and the Vercel preview passed. Production Railway + Vercel health served exact merge SHA `071992e`; web `/` + `/coach-state` returned 200 and the new learning route returned 401 unauthenticated both direct and proxied. |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-25 — Completed-workout read on the Week view + ERG-always profile (Mark feedback)

Mark's 2026-07-25 feedback, two points folded into one batch. **(1)** Tapping a
completed session on the Week view opens `WorkoutDetailSheet` (Batch 135), which
only ever renders the *originally planned* structure — he wants to look back and
see what he actually did and how he performed. The post-workout read already
persists (one `analyses` row per completed session, keyed to `planned_workout_id`
+ `activity_id`, holding `output_markdown` + `verdict`) and Home surfaces it the
day of, but the Week rail carries no link to it (`PlanWorkoutOut` has no analysis
reference), so it is unreachable later. **(2)** Mark rides **every** Zwift session
in ERG and asked the coach to know this so it never second-guesses his power. He
weighed the one wrinkle — ERG blunts the very short 30/15-style surges, the single
such session he does per week — and chose ERG-on always, because toggling ERG off
mid-session is impractical and can cause trainer/technical issues on his setup, so
the coach must never suggest switching ERG off. Recorded authoritatively in the
`profile` KB so it reaches every packet via `athleteProfile`, rather than left to
the conversation-learning path (which needs a manual distil + accept and would be
dormant here anyway). Calibration decided with Craig 2026-07-25: honest-note, not
a nag.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Lazy fetch on sheet-open, chosen over inlining markdown into the whole-week `/schedule` payload. No migration.<br>152.2 **Render the read in the sheet.** In `apps/web/src/components/WorkoutDetailSheet.tsx`, when `status === 'completed'`, fetch and show the read (verdict pill + `Markdown`) *alongside* the existing planned `Session structure` — Mark wants both "what I did" and "how I performed" — reusing Home's read presentation. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** read-only vs also mounting the `FeedbackControl` + `BriefFollowUpChat` Home already attaches to a completed read.<br>152.3 **Honest states.** Completed-but-no-read-yet, still-generating, and generation-failed each render an explicit one-line state — never a blank sheet or an endless spinner. ## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-25 — Full-app review (four-phase audit) batch plan

Craig asked (2026-07-25) for another review of the app **including the recent
changes** (Batches 147–152: interval editor, training-week grounding, Monday
Week, post-workout chat, conversational learning, completed-read + ERG). Scoped
into **four diagnose-only review batches** so the work is tracked and actioned
like everything else in the ledger.

**Model — diagnose, then remediate.** Each review batch is **diagnose-only**: it
produces a committed report doc and ranks its findings, and **no product code
change lands inside a review batch** (the single exception is the trivial ops act
of seeding `admin_alert_user_id`, called out in 154). Every material finding
becomes its own scoped remediation batch afterward — exactly how the 2026-07-10
`COACHING_INTEGRITY_AUDIT.md` fed the (still-open) F1/F2 remediation. A review
never edits verdict logic, Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, or Red-never-VO2.

**Scope decision (Craig, 2026-07-25 — "for the full app?").** Full-app is the
point for **154/155/156** because the property each judges is *weakest-link or
emergent*: security is only as strong as the least-guarded table/route, and
coaching integrity + UX consistency arise from the whole system, not any one
batch. **153 is deliberately scoped** to the recent batches **plus** the
high-risk engine hotspots — a literal every-file read of ~70k LOC / 247 source
files of shipped, CI-green code is low-yield, so the deep code read targets where
bugs actually hide.

**Ordering.** Recommended **153 → 154 → 155 → 156** (facts/bugs first, then
security, then behaviour, then experience — each leans on the prior), but the
batches are independent and any can be pulled forward (e.g. lead with the
coaching-integrity audit) without renumbering. `/batch-start` assigns the
DECISIONS number when each is actually started.

Keep a content/version fingerprint or retryable sync state rather than trusting pointer equality.<br>158.3 **Observable non-deliverable state (folds CR153-08).** `_deliver_one` swallows an `HTTPException` from FTP-load/IR-build and returns `None` (`:591-598`), indistinguishable from an intentional no-op; persist/emit a structured retryable/non-retryable failure with user/workout/version context and distinguish it from expected non-bike no-ops.<br>158.4 **Tests.** DB-backed failure → persisted state → successful reconciliation retry across interval-edit and generic resync; malformed-IR, missing-FTP, and no-op paths tested separately. | A transient external-update failure can never leave the app claiming the newest workout is live while Zwift holds the old one, and a silently-skipped delivery leaves a durable diagnostic instead of vanishing. | Shipped via PR #187, squash `bf7474b` (Decision #238): `commit=False` is caller-transaction-safe, pointers advance only after cloud success, and persisted delivered IR id/version is the content fingerprint used by reconciliation. ## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-26 — Batch 155 coaching-integrity remediation

Remediation for the open findings in the Batch 155 coaching-integrity refresh (`COACHING_INTEGRITY_AUDIT.md`), which the previous section deferred. These close the chronic-drift gaps the audit says would lift the grade from B+ to A−. **These are the first batches in the wave that deliberately change the deterministic Green/Amber/Red verdict engine** — so every acceptance criterion requires the change to only ever *harden* the light (never soften it) and to keep the light deterministic (computed in Python, not model-set). Stub→batch: R155-A→167, R155-B→168, R155-C→169; **R155-D (anti-sycophancy chat line) is folded into Batch 165** (same `brief_chat.py` prompt). 167/168 are 🔴 High (verdict core, paired — could be run as one batch if preferred); 169 is 🟢 Mid. The older 2026-07-10 verdict findings are now batched too: F3 (MED-HIGH — sleep credit can carry a night across the Green line), F4 (MED — no cumulative escalation to Red), and F5 (MED — optimistic missing-data policy) are **clubbed into Batch 170** (all three reshape the same `_morning_verdict` ladder and interact); F6 (MED — chronic surveillance is advisory-only) is **Batch 171** (🟢 Mid unless it takes the verdict-cap option). F7's decay half is covered by 169. The verdict-engine safety rule (only ever harden the light, keep it deterministic) applies to 170, and to 171 if it caps the verdict. Decision numbers assigned at `/batch-start`; rows stay `Planned`.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 167 — Close F2: training load can cap the verdict (R155-A)~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 167.1 **Give `_morning_verdict` a load input.** The deterministic verdict takes no ACWR/ramp/recovery-time parameter (`services/morning_analysis.py:1450-1461`); ACWR/chronic-load/balance are prose-only to the model (`:69-72`), a hard prior day only appends a note when the day is already non-Green (`:1547-1550`), and the one place load enters the ladder *relaxes* it — a Low Garmin readiness with clean recovery + any load present reads `load_driven` and escapes auto-Amber (`:1503-1508,1522`). Pass a real load signal (computed ACWR and/or Garmin recovery-time) into the verdict.<br>167.2 **Add a hard load cap (never a relax).** When ACWR ≥ ~1.5 (or recovery-time over a threshold), cap the day at **Amber** independent of how good last night looked; keep the acute Red gates. This only *hardens* the light. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** exact thresholds + source (computed ACWR vs Garmin recovery-time vs both), and whether to remove or subordinate the `load_driven` relax path.<br>167.3 **Motivating real case + regression.** 07-24 fired Green on readiness LOW + yesterday HARD via `readinessInterpretation="load_driven"`; after this change that morning caps at Amber — add a deterministic test on that shape.<br>167.4 **DECISIONS + tests** proving the cap fires on high ACWR/recovery-time, never lowers caution, and leaves the acute Red/Amber gates unchanged. | Let accumulating training load *cap* the daily light at Amber (not merely relax it), closing the audit's F2 gap so a fast ramp with clean overnight signals can no longer green-light overreaching. | Shipped via PR #197 / squash `596b2b9` (Decision #248): `_morning_verdict` consumes computed ACWR and Garmin recovery time; ACWR ≥1.50 or recovery >24h applies a one-way Amber ceiling; `load_driven` is subordinate; the structured cap packet and prompt v17 preserve deterministic ownership; 07-24 and only-hardens regressions cover the real case. No migration, shared-schema, plan, delivery, auth, or hosting change. Three CI waves passed; Vercel preview/production and Railway production were verified at the exact SHA; web, readiness, and protected daily-loop smokes passed. Closes 🔴 **F2 / R155-A** (`COACHING_INTEGRITY_AUDIT.md`). |
| ~~Batch 168 — Close F1: absolute baseline anchor + drift alarm (R155-B)~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 168.1 **Anchor personal floors with an absolute floor.** The soft-sleep→Green override floors readiness at Mark's own rolling median (`morning_analysis.py:1315-1321`, `:1696`), "in band" keys off personal quartiles (`personal_baselines.py:49-62`), the window is 84 days (`metric_baselines.py:36`), and no absolute anchor exists — so a multi-week fatigue slide lets the floors sink and old-Amber becomes new-Green (Probe 4: readiness 52 → Green against a drifted median of 50). Add an absolute floor the personal floor can't sink below (e.g. never treat readiness < 50 as "at median", regardless of history).<br>168.2 **Alarm on the baseline trend itself.** Flag when the 84-day median is *declining* so a slow slide is visible instead of absorbed as the new normal. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** whether the trend signal caps the verdict (like F2) or is a strong surfaced warning; exact absolute-floor constants.<br>168.3 **DECISIONS + tests** — a drifted-baseline scenario no longer grants the override below the absolute floor, healthy-baseline behaviour is unchanged, and the trend alarm fires on a sustained decline. | Stop "normal" self-recalibrating — anchor the verdict's personal floors so a slow multi-week fatigue slide can't quietly become the new Green, closing the audit's F1 gap. | **Shipped via PR #198 / squash `d6fa01c` (Decision #249):** the effective soft-sleep readiness floor is `max(personal 84-day median, 60)`, so readiness 52 against a drifted median of 50 remains Amber while readiness 66 on a healthy baseline remains Green. Two calendar 42-day halves inside the trailing 84-day window, with at least 21 samples each, trigger a structured warning when the recent median declines by at least five points; prompt v18 must surface it, but the alarm cannot set or soften the light. Deterministic drift, sampling-boundary, healthy-baseline, and warning-only regressions pass. Backend + docs only; no migration, shared-schema, plan, delivery, auth, or hosting change. Branch-push, PR-context, and implementation-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Vercel preview/production reached Ready; exact-SHA Railway health, API readiness, web `/`, and protected daily-loop smokes passed. Closes 🔴 **F1 / R155-B** (`COACHING_INTEGRITY_AUDIT.md`). |
| ~~Batch 169 — Bound & decay accumulating memory: learned_context + corrections (R155-C)~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 169.1 **Cap and/or age `learned_context` (F9).** `learned_context_packet` returns *all* confirmed items with no volume cap and no decay (`services/learned_context.py:13-20`), so over long accumulation it could grow the prompt and subtly warm tone (human-curated, cannot touch the light). Add a volume cap and/or aging so confirmed memory can't grow unboundedly.<br>169.2 **Decay old corrections (F7).** Corrections are fed to the model as ground truth (last 5) with no decay (`morning_analysis.py:80-84`); age/decay them so a repeated correction can't indefinitely steer narrative tone, and never let a correction restate an objective metric as better than measured.<br>169.3 **Tests** — the packet respects the cap/age policy; stale corrections decay; `_morning_verdict` remains unable to read `learned_context` (unchanged invariant re-asserted). | Bound and age the coach's accumulating memory — learned context and corrections — so it can't grow the prompt or warm tone over time, closing F9 and F7's decay half. | **Shipped via PR #199 / squash `353c61c` (Decision #250):** `learnedContext` now emits at most the latest 12 eligible confirmed items; dated items older than 365 days decay out; undated legacy items sort oldest and survive only when the cap has room. Fed-forward corrections keep the existing five-row cap but now decay after 45 days, and morning/post-workout prompts v19/v11 forbid using corrections to restate objective metrics, completed workouts, or deterministic verdicts as better than measured. `_morning_verdict` still has no learned-memory parameter. Backend + docs only; no migration, shared-schema, plan, delivery, auth, or hosting change. Branch-push, PR-context, and implementation-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Vercel preview/production reached Ready; exact-SHA Railway health, web `/`, and protected Coach-memory learning/daily-loop smokes passed. Closes **F9 / R155-C** and **F7 decay** (`COACHING_INTEGRITY_AUDIT.md`). |
| ~~Batch 170 — Verdict-ladder hardening: sleep-credit ceiling, cumulative escalation, missing-data policy (F3 + F4 + F5)~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 170.1 **Sleep-credit ceiling (F3, MED-HIGH).** `age_adjusted_sleep_score` only ever raises the raw Garmin score (credit ≥ 0, capped +12; `sleep_scoring.py:45-47,159`) and feeds the only hard gates — Red < 60, Amber < 74, Green ≥ 74 (`morning_analysis.py:1180-1206`); +12 can jump a full band and once a credited night reaches ≥74 the soft-sleep-caution path is skipped. Real data 07-08: raw 53 (POOR) → age-adj 65 kept a would-be-Red night at Amber. Let age-credit lift *within* a band but not alone carry a night *across* the Green threshold — require a corroborating objective signal (or a minimum raw score) to convert a credited night to Green.<br>170.2 **Cumulative escalation to Red (F4, MED).** The ladder is first-match with no stacking (`morning_analysis.py:1180-1206`): Poor readiness alone caps at Amber and moderate-bad signals never compound (Probe 5: age-adj 62 + Poor readiness + subjective 3 + hard-yesterday + 1400-min recovery → Amber). Add a stacking rule: Poor readiness co-occurring with any second negative (soft sleep, low subjective, hard-yesterday, elevated RHR) escalates Amber → Red.<br>170.3 **Missing-data policy (F5, MED).** `None` is treated as passing for subjective (`:1152`) and HRV/readiness (`:1322-1331`) while missing RHR fails safe, so the null move (feel awful, log nothing) keeps a Green honesty would lose (no-HRV → Green; blank subjective → Green; honest subjective 3 → Amber). Require *positive* HRV evidence to convert a soft night to Green (absent HRV neutralises the override, like absent RHR), and treat a stale/absent subjective as neutral, not positive.<br>170.4 **Combined tests + DECISIONS.** Because all three reshape the same ladder, test their interactions as one matrix (credited-night-not-Green, stacking→Red, null-move-no-longer-Green) plus the existing audit Probes; DECISIONS entry; prove every change only *hardens* the light and the acute gates + #133/#135 data-quality rules hold. | Make the deterministic ladder resistant to the slow/second-order moves — a credited night crossing the Green line, a pile-up of moderate-bad signals never reaching Red, and the "log nothing" null move — closing audit findings F3/F4/F5. | **Shipped via PR #200 / squash `6ad70a0` (Decision #251):** a raw-below-74 night credited across the Green threshold now requires the complete measured #133 recovery bundle plus a current subjective score ≥5; Poor readiness plus soft sleep, current subjective <5, a hard prior day, or proved-elevated RHR escalates to Red; and absent HRV/readiness/check-in or an absent RHR baseline stays neutral and cannot unlock the discretionary Green exception or Low-readiness relaxation. Age credit still lifts within Red/Amber; acute Red/Amber, #133/#135, the Batch 167 load cap, and Red-never-VO2 remain intact. Prompt v20 exposes structured `sleepCreditCeiling` / `cumulativeEscalation` evidence without controlling the deterministic result, and the combined matrix proves every changed outcome is equally or more cautious. Backend + docs only; no migration, shared-schema, plan, delivery, auth, or hosting change. The PR check rollup and implementation-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; Vercel preview/production reached Ready; exact-SHA Railway + Vercel health, web `/`, and protected daily-loop smokes passed. Closes **F3/F4/F5** (`COACHING_INTEGRITY_AUDIT.md`). |
| ~~Batch 171 — Chronic overreaching drives an action, not just advice (F6)~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 171.1 **Wire sustained chronic decline into a real action.** `chronic_patterns` watches HRV/readiness/RHR/sleep over 28 days but is explicitly read-only ("no verdict or delivery-rule change", `chronic_patterns.py:1-6`) and only *suggests* ("do not chase load", `:713-716`); `block_progression` already computes a ≥2-Red-morning signal. Turn a sustained recovery-marker decline (or that ≥2-Red signal) into an actual action instead of a dismissible card. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** an automatic **deload proposal** via the existing restructure/delivery rail (lighter, no verdict change → stays 🟢 Mid) vs a **temporary daily-verdict cap** (touches the verdict core → bump to 🔴 High and apply the only-harden/deterministic rule).<br>171.2 **Guardrails.** Don't let a motivated user dismiss the signal indefinitely; avoid false deloads (require genuine sustained decline, not a single bad day); reuse the existing propose→approve→push contract if using the deload path.<br>171.3 **Tests** — a sustained decline (or ≥2-Red) triggers the chosen action; a single bad day does not; the existing chronic advisory copy still renders. | Let the app *act* on a sustained overreaching signature it can already see — an auto deload proposal or a temporary verdict cap — instead of only showing an advisory card a motivated athlete can ignore, closing audit finding F6. | **Shipped via PR #201 / squash `d6cd890` (Decision #252):** an already-qualified personal recovery marker missed on ≥70% of observed nights, or at least two Red mornings in seven days, emits a deterministic `verdict.chronicAction` and persistent approval-gated deload proposals for active indoor-bike workouts through day six. The established transform cuts duration 25%, drops one zone, and removes HIT/VO2; sparse/isolated evidence is neutral, completed sessions are excluded, acute/acted adjustments win, and stable audit tags make retries idempotent. The daily verdict remains unchanged (`verdictImpact="none"`), prompt v21 is explanatory only, and the existing chronic advisory packet/copy is regression-protected. Backend + docs only; no migration, shared schema, new endpoint, frontend, provider, auth, or hosting change. ## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-27 — Installed home-screen PWA can't activate (Batch 160 iOS regression)

Reported 2026-07-27: after a successful Safari activation, the iOS **Add to Home Screen** app still shows "Set up this device". Batch 160 removed the in-app login path, and iOS isolates a standalone home-screen app's storage from Safari, so the device token minted through the Safari activation link never reaches the installed app — and `AccessPage` offers no way to authenticate from inside the app. See DECISIONS #242.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 172 — Installed-PWA activation entry (Batch 160 iOS home-screen regression)~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 172.1 **In-app activation entry (the iOS fix).** `AccessPage` (`apps/web/src/pages/AccessPage.tsx`) shows only static "ask Craig for a link" copy, so the installed standalone PWA can't authenticate — iOS isolates its `localStorage` from the Safari tab the link opened in. Add a text field that accepts a full `…/activate?code=XXX` URL **or** a bare code, extracts the code, and calls the existing `activateDevice(code)` from `useAuth()` (the same path `ActivatePage` uses), storing the device token in the standalone app's own storage; on success navigate to `/`.<br>172.2 **Single-use reconciliation + setup copy.** Activation codes are single-use and opening the Safari link consumes them, so instruct iOS users to add to Home Screen, open the app, then paste the link/code (not tap it in Safari first). Keep the `/activate?code=` link flow unchanged for Safari/desktop. Show a retryable inline error on an invalid/expired/used code (reuse `ActivatePage`'s message style).<br>172.3 **Tests** — `AccessPage` renders the input; a bare code and a pasted full `/activate?code=` URL both extract the same code; submitting a valid code calls `activateDevice` and routes to the dashboard; an invalid code surfaces the error and stays on the setup screen; the existing link-activation path is untouched. | Restore "add to home screen" setup on iOS: let the installed standalone app authenticate itself with a pasted activation code/link, closing the Batch 160 regression that left the home-screen app with no sign-in path. | The device-setup screen exposes an activation field that accepts a bare code or a full `/activate?code=` URL; entering a valid code activates the device in the installed app's own storage and lands on the dashboard; an invalid/expired/used code shows a retryable error without leaving the screen; the Safari/desktop link flow still works; web unit tests cover input render, URL + bare-code extraction, success routing, and failure. **Frontend-only, no migration, no backend change.** New regression from Batch 160 (DS154-01), found 2026-07-27. Decision #242 assigned at `/batch-start`. |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-29 — Mark feedback 2026-07-25/28/29: adjustment coherence + packet honesty

Feedback wave from Mark across 2026-07-25, 07-28, and 07-29 (screenshots in `~/Downloads/Dad Fitness/`), reconciled against the code and confirmed against prod on 2026-07-29. Five findings, all overtraining-guardrail-adjacent, grouped into three batches. **Batch 173 (coherence)** closes the two linked adjustment defects: the morning Amber/Red adjustment is generated too late to accept — the check-in path gates it behind `ride_still_pending` while plan rides are pre-delivered to Zwift days ahead, so only the ungated 11:00 backstop produces it (prod-confirmed: the 07-29 `amber_regeneration` proposal was created at 11:00, ~85 min after the 09:35 verdict) — and four code paths scale an Amber ride to four different intensities for the *same* session (KB rule keeps 67%, narrative keeps 67%, editor "Scale down" preset gives 60%, delivery transform gives 54%). **Batch 174 (packet honesty)** fills two gaps where the packet omits real data and the narrative fills it with a template or a partial signal: the post-workout read carries only today's planned row so it parrots the prose `weeklyRhythm` for "tomorrow", and the morning read's "yesterday" is exercise-only so a high-cost non-exercise day is invisible. **Batch 175 (chat policy)** relaxes the brief-chat fence so the coach answers general training-science questions instead of refusing. Where a change touches the deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder (174.2's optional verdict-hardening), the verdict-engine safety rule applies: only ever *harden* the light, keep it deterministic. Decision numbers assigned at `/batch-start`; rows stay `Planned`.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 173 — One coherent morning adjustment: generate it at check-in, scale it one way~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 173.1 **Offer the Amber/Red adjustment at check-in, not only at the 11:00 backstop.** `regenerate_after_morning_checkin` calls `regenerate_for_verdict` (`executable_coaching.py:513-525`) but gates it behind `ride_still_pending` — False whenever the deliverable bike ride already has an approved/pushed proposal. Plan rides are pushed to Zwift days ahead (07-29's ride pushed since 07-08), so the check-in almost always sees an already-pushed ride and skips the adjustment; the ungated 11:00 backstop (`run_morning_weather_sync` → `regenerate_for_verdict`, `scheduler.py:459`) then creates it ~85 min later (prod-confirmed: proposal `bf964ccc`, `amber_regeneration`, 10:00 UTC vs the 08:35 UTC verdict). Distinguish a ride **pre-delivered by plan import/schedule** from one **Mark deliberately approved or edited today** (e.g. by proposal `origin`/approver), so a pre-delivered ride still gets a *proposed* (never auto-pushed) morning adjustment surfaced front-and-centre, while Decision #29's real intent — never silently overwrite a ride Mark acted on *today* — is preserved. Align the backstop and check-in paths to the same rule (the backstop currently ignores the gate). **Decide at `/batch-start`:** the exact "acted-on-today" predicate, and whether the backstop keeps proposing on already-pushed rides.<br>173.2 **One verdict-scaling rule, and stop dropping a Zone-2 ride below Zone 2.** Four paths scale an Amber ride independently: the KB rule "cut duration 20-30% and remove HIT" (`coaching_state.py:186`), the `_eased_ride_detail` narrative guidance "drop a zone" (`morning_analysis.py:1412-1414`), the delivery transform `adjust_ir_for_verdict` (×0.75 duration, cap 98%, −13pt zone-drop → 54% on a 67% ride; `executable_coaching.py:83-88,209-214`), and the manual editor "Scale down" preset `scale_block` (×0.75 duration, ×0.9 power → 60%; `interval_workout_editor.py:137-151`). Unify on one deterministic scaling function that the editor preset, the delivery transform, and the narrative guidance all describe identically. For an already-Zone-2 endurance ride, cut duration only — do **not** drop intensity below the Zone-2 floor (the KB rule and the narrative are right; "drop a zone" + ×0.9 are wrong for Z2, which is why Mark hand-reset the editor's 60% back to 67% on 07-29). **Decide at `/batch-start`:** the canonical constants and how "already at/below Z2 → cut duration only" is detected.<br>173.3 **Surface the deterministic adjustment numbers in the packet.** Brief-chat couldn't explain the 60% because no packet held it ("the packet doesn't give me visibility into how the app calculated its 60% suggestion"). Emit the verdict-adjustment (duration/intensity deltas + resulting targets) into the morning analysis packet so the narrative and the chat (Batch 175) quote the same figures instead of guessing. Explanatory only — never sets the light or the numbers.<br>173.4 **Tests + DECISIONS.** A check-in Amber/Red morning on a pre-delivered, un-acted-on ride produces a *proposed* adjustment (front-and-centre `delivery.changed`) at check-in, not only at the backstop; a ride Mark approved/edited today is still not silently re-adjusted; the editor preset, delivery transform, and narrative guidance produce identical numbers for the same ride; a Zone-2 ride is duration-cut only; the packet carries the adjustment figures; every path stays deterministic. | Make the morning Amber/Red adjustment appear when the verdict does — and describe it with one set of numbers everywhere (home card, editor, narrative, chat) — instead of a late backstop proposal that disagrees with three other scaling rules. | On an Amber/Red check-in with a pre-delivered ride still un-acted-on today, a *proposed* (never auto-pushed) coach adjustment is created at check-in and flips the Home card into its approve-and-upload state; a ride Mark approved or edited today is never silently re-adjusted (Decision #29 intent preserved); the check-in and 11:00-backstop paths apply the same re-adjust rule. The editor "Scale down" preset, the `adjust_ir_for_verdict` transform, and the `_eased_ride_detail` guidance return identical duration/intensity for a given ride from one shared scaling function; an already-Zone-2 endurance ride is cut in duration only, never pushed below the Zone-2 floor. The morning packet carries the structured adjustment (deltas + resulting targets), explanatory-only and unable to set the verdict or numbers. Deterministic tests cover check-in-vs-backstop timing, the acted-on-today guard, cross-path number equality, the Z2-duration-only rule, and packet exposure. Backend-only; no migration or shared-schema change; delivery stays propose→approve→push (Decision #29). Prod-confirmed root cause 2026-07-29 (proposal `bf964ccc` at 11:00 vs verdict at 09:35). **Shipped via PR #202 / squash `04efcb3` (Decision #253):** built as one batch (not split). New leaf `services/verdict_scaling.py` shares `ease_amber_power_pct`/`ENDURANCE_CEILING_PCT=75` across the delivery transform, editor "Scale down" preset, and narrative (an already-Zone-2 ride is duration-cut only, held at 67%; harder intervals drop a zone floored at the ceiling, capped at threshold); the `_deliberately_acted` guard moved into `regenerate_for_verdict`, so a ride pre-delivered by push-on-plan-set gets a *proposed* (never auto-pushed) adjustment at check-in while a ride Mark approved/edited today is untouched, and the check-in and 11:00 backstop share the rule; `verdict.verdictAdjustment` surfaces the deterministic figures (explanatory-only, prompt v22). Backend + docs only; no migration/shared-schema/frontend change. Branch-push + PR-context CI passed all seven jobs; production verified on merge SHA `04efcb3` (Railway direct + Vercel same-origin health, web `/` 200, daily-loop 401 direct/proxied). |
| ~~Batch 174 — Analysis packets carry the real schedule + non-exercise load, not templates~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 174.1 **Post-workout read sees the real upcoming sessions, not the `weeklyRhythm` template.** `post_workout_analysis._planned_workouts` filters `workout_date == subject_date` (`post_workout_analysis.py:656-676`), so the packet's `plannedWorkouts` holds only the completed day; the prompt still carries `include_tomorrow_impact` (`:447`) and the prose `weeklyRhythm` (`coaching_state.py:172-178`) — so the model parrots the template for "tomorrow" (07-25: said "Sunday is light strength or mobility" while the plan had Sunday = Long Z2 120 min, and when challenged could only guess a "swap"). Fetch the next 1–2 days' active planned workouts and add them to the packet as `upcomingWorkouts`; update the output rule so the model treats actual upcoming sessions as ground truth and names them (e.g. "tomorrow's Long Z2, 120 min"), using `weeklyRhythm` only as fallback when no real row exists.<br>174.2 **Morning "yesterday" includes non-exercise load, not just training load.** `_yesterday_load` queries only `Activity` rows (`morning_analysis._yesterday_load` + `_yesterday_load_packet`) → `totalTrainingLoad`/`hardestActivity` only; `_training_load_signal` (`:1014`) feeds the verdict only ACWR + recovery-time; yesterday's `DailyMetric` (`stress_avg`, `body_battery_drained`, `body_battery_end`) is never loaded. So the narrative reduced "yesterday" to "training load 5.2" and a high-cost non-exercise day is invisible (Mark's 07-28 concern). Load yesterday's `DailyMetric` and add all-day stress + Body-Battery drain to the yesterday packet; update the prompt so the read stops equating "yesterday" with training load alone. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** narrative-only (surfaced context) vs also *hardening* the deterministic verdict (very high all-day stress / deep Body-Battery drain adds caution). If it touches the ladder it must **only ever harden** the light and stay deterministic (verdict-engine safety rule); HRV/Body-Battery already partially reflect non-exercise stress, so narrative-only is the conservative default.<br>174.3 **Tests + DECISIONS.** Post-workout: a packet whose next day is a planned Long Z2 120 min carries that row in `upcomingWorkouts` and the prompt ranks it above `weeklyRhythm` (deterministic packet test). Morning: yesterday's stress/Body-Battery-drain is loaded and present even on a zero-exercise day; if verdict-hardening is chosen, a deterministic test proves it only ever hardens. | Stop the reads inventing "tomorrow" from a prose template and reducing "yesterday" to exercise load — feed them the actual upcoming sessions and yesterday's whole-day cost. | The post-workout packet includes the next 1–2 days' active planned workouts as `upcomingWorkouts` and the prompt names the real upcoming session over the `weeklyRhythm` template (falling back to the template only when no row exists); a regression proves tomorrow's real row is present and preferred. The morning packet loads yesterday's `DailyMetric` and carries all-day stress + Body-Battery drain in the "yesterday" context; the prompt no longer equates "yesterday" with training load alone; a high-stress zero-exercise yesterday is represented. If 174.2 hardens the verdict, deterministic tests prove it only ever hardens and stays computed in Python. 174.1 is additive context (independently shippable, ~🟢 Mid); 174.2 carries the verdict decision. Backend-only; no migration or shared-schema change. **Shipped via PR #203 / squash `b051b7c` (Decision #254):** all active planned rows on subject day +1/+2 now populate `upcomingWorkouts`, and prompt v12 makes them authoritative over the prose rhythm; morning prompt v23 receives yesterday's `wholeDayCost` even with zero exercise, while `statusScope="exercise_only"` and `classificationImpact="none"` preserve the narrative-only boundary. The deterministic verdict/load inputs are unchanged. After fixture-order repair `0260bfc`, branch, PR-context, and implementation-merge `main` CI passed all seven jobs; production verified exact-SHA Railway + Vercel health, DB readiness, web `/` 200, and protected daily-loop 401 direct/proxied. |
| ~~Batch 175 — Brief-chat answers general training-science, not just the packet~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 175.1 **Permit general, labelled sport-science answers.** `brief_chat` fences the model to "Answer only from the packet and the read… If the packet does not hold what is needed to answer, say so plainly" (`brief_chat.py:61-63`), which made it refuse Mark's answerable "what %FTP could I drop the VO₂ intervals to and keep similar benefit?" (07-28). Relax the fence for **general, non-personalized endurance-science** (minimum-effective VO₂ intensity, intensity/duration trade-offs, why a zone matters): let the model answer from established exercise physiology, **clearly labelled as general principle**. Keep the packet-only rule for anything about **Mark's own metrics/plan/history** — those may not be invented.<br>175.2 **Keep plan changes in propose/confirm; quote the app's own numbers.** Any actual prescription change still routes through the existing propose/confirm affordance (it already does — preserve it). When Batch 173.3 surfaces the deterministic adjustment in the packet, the chat quotes those figures when asked "why 60%/54%?" instead of punting. State the boundary explicitly: general science = allowed + labelled; personal prescription = packet-only or propose/confirm.<br>175.3 **Guardrails + tests.** The relaxation must not invent Mark-specific numbers or override the deterministic verdict/floors — the existing floors stay (`brief_chat.py:66`: never recommend VO2 on a Red day, etc.). Tests: a general min-effective-%FTP question returns a substantive, labelled answer (not a refusal); a question about Mark's specific readiness answers only from the packet; a plan-change request yields propose/confirm, not a direct change; the Red-day-VO2 floor holds. **Decided at `/batch-start`:** use a broad established-endurance-science lane rather than a curated whitelist, with exact label `General principle:`. | Let the coach answer legitimate training-science questions ("how far can I dial this back and keep the benefit?") from general physiology, clearly labelled, instead of refusing because it isn't in the packet. | **Shipped via PR #204 / squash `4aeb5c1` (Decision #255):** brief-chat prompt v4 permits general non-personalized endurance-science answers labelled `General principle:`, while Mark-specific metrics/plan/history/readiness/prescription stay packet-bound and actual workout changes remain confirm-before-apply/propose-confirm. Red-never-VO2 and all deterministic floors are unchanged. Added DB-backed guardrail tests for general science, packet-only personal answers, propose/confirm, and Red-day VO2, plus a pure prompt-policy test for local no-Postgres verification. Backend prompt-policy + tests/docs only; no migration, backend-service rewrite, frontend, shared-schema, provider, auth, hosting, verdict, or plan/delivery change. ## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-29 — Mark feedback: live body metrics (weight → W/kg) + dynamic VO2 max

Separate feedback thread from Mark on 2026-07-29 (a question thread over a ride read, distinct from the 07-25/28/29 adjustment-coherence wave above), reconciled against the code and confirmed that the underlying Garmin data is already synced. Two findings with one root cause: **the live Garmin body metrics the coach needs are captured on the daily `DailyMetric` row every sync, but the coaching packets either omit them or are shadowed by a stale static copy.** Weight is fetched (`garmin_sync.py:203`) but kept only on an exact same-day weigh-in (`_extract_weight_kg`, `:758-769`) — so it is null on most days and reaches only the morning packet, never the post-workout/chat packet where power-to-weight matters — and **watts-per-kg is computed nowhere** (API + web greps, 0 hits), which is why the read admitted it could not give a W/kg figure. VO2 max is synced live (`get_max_metrics`, `:204`; currently 55) and already feeds the age-norm (`morning_analysis.py:1328`), yet the `profile` KB hardcodes `vo2max: 54` (`coaching_state.py:31`) and that stale copy is injected as `athleteProfile` into every read (`post_workout_analysis.py:426`), so the coach narrates a fixed 54. **Batch 176** carries weight forward and computes W/kg into the ride read (and, via `brief_chat.py:300`, the chat over it); **Batch 177** overlays the live daily VO2 onto the packet so real changes flow into the coaching. Both share one mechanism — resolve an *effective* body metric at packet-build (live daily value, profile as fallback, carry an as-of date) and overlay it onto the packet — and could factor a shared helper; because KB seeding fills only missing sections (`coaching_state.py:562`), neither can be fixed by editing the seed, so both live in the packet-build layer. Both are explanatory-only: nothing here touches the deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder (verdict-engine safety rule). Decision numbers assigned at `/batch-start`; rows stay `Planned`.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 176 — Bodyweight from Garmin → watts-per-kg in the ride read (and chat)~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 176.1 **Carry the most-recent weigh-in forward.** `_extract_weight_kg` (`garmin_sync.py:758-769`) returns a value only when a weigh-in is dated exactly the calendar day, with no carry-forward, so `weight_kg` (`coaching.py:58`) is null on every day Mark does not step on the scale (most days). `get_weigh_ins` already fetches a 7-day range (`garmin_sync.py:203`) — resolve the most-recent weigh-in within the window and carry it forward, retaining its as-of date so a stale weight is never shown as today's. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** lookback window length; whether to persist effective weight + as-of date on the daily row or resolve at packet-build.<br>176.2 **Weight + W/kg into the post-workout packet.** The packet (`post_workout_analysis.py:422-433`) carries `ftpWatts` (280, via `_ftp_watts` `:922-928`) but no bodyweight, and W/kg is computed nowhere (API + web greps, 0 hits) — the reason the read said it could not give a W/kg figure.
