## Post-roadmap — 2026-07-30 — Mark feedback: the chat and the app feel "almost disconnected"

Mark reported on 2026-07-30 that the LLM conversations and the app itself feel *almost disconnected*, citing a reply that referred to something not being in the **packet**. Reconciled against the code: the report is accurate and **structural, not a wording defect**. Brief-chat's entire world is one stored `context_packet` plus the read's own markdown (`brief_chat.py:297-302`) in a single Anthropic call with no retrieval — `generate_anthropic_text` (`anthropic_text.py:150-159`) takes only system/user/prior-messages and has **no tool-use support**. Three consequences: (1) the chat system prompt uses the internal noun "packet" eight times and `:61-63` instructs the model to say plainly when the packet lacks something, so our schema vocabulary reaches Mark verbatim — the exact leak he saw; (2) everything else the app computes is invisible to a conversation — Trends, Reviews, Week Ahead, Sleep, Environment are separate routers with their own packet types (`deep_review`, `seasonal_trend`), so a question whose answer is rendered one tab away gets an honest refusal; (3) the packet is frozen at generation, so anything after the brief (a completed ride, an edited plan) cannot be discussed on that read. Underneath sits a schema assertion that a conversation cannot exist without a document: `BriefMessage.analysis_id` is `NOT NULL` with an FK (`coaching.py:565`), and the Sleep page has no `Analysis` of its own — it borrows `morningAnalysis` (`SleepPage.tsx:123`), as do the breathwork/strength/walking briefs, which are not `analyses` rows at all. So "chat after the sleep brief" has nothing to attach to today. **Batch 178** feeds the conversation live app state and stops it narrating its own plumbing; **Batch 179** unifies the per-read chats into one coach reachable from anywhere. **Sequencing is load-bearing: 178 must land before 179** — a general chat surface built on today's empty context would refuse *more* than the current per-read chat, shipping Mark's complaint at a larger scale. Note also that Batch 175 (Decision #255) already patched one instance of this by relaxing the fence for general sport science; the residual is structural, and the fix is **feeding the model real data, not loosening the fence further** — the packet-bound rule is what stops the LLM inventing Mark's metrics or talking around the deterministic verdict, and that stays. Both batches are explanatory/surface work: nothing here touches the deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder (verdict-engine safety rule), and any plan change stays on the existing propose/confirm rail (Decision #29). Decision numbers assigned at `/batch-start`; rows stay `Planned`.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 178 — Chat answers from live app state, and stops narrating its own plumbing~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 178.1 **Stop leaking the internal vocabulary.** `brief_chat.SYSTEM_PROMPT` (`brief_chat.py:56-85`) says "packet" eight times and `:61-63` tells the model to say so plainly when the packet cannot answer — so Mark is told things are not "in the packet". Rewrite the prompt so the internal noun never reaches the user and the genuinely-unknown case reads as a coach's sentence ("I don't have your sleep history in front of me here") rather than a schema complaint. The honesty requirement is unchanged — only the register. Add a test asserting the plumbing vocabulary (`packet`, `context packet`, `JSON`) is absent from answers, including on the not-known path.<br>178.2 **Assemble chat context at ask-time, not read-time.** `ask()` freezes on `analysis.context_packet` stored at generation (`brief_chat.py:297-302`), so a ride completed after the morning brief is invisible to that brief's chat. Build the conversation's context when the question is asked. Reuse `analysis_currentness.py` (Batch 159) for the staleness rules rather than inventing a second notion of "current". **Decide at `/batch-start`:** rebuild the read packet wholesale vs keep the stored packet as the read's own record and layer a fresh delta over it.<br>178.3 **Widen the context to the rest of the app.** The deterministic builders already exist and are reusable as-is — `build_training_week_packet` (`training_week.py:206`) for the week ahead, `compute_trend_windows` plus `window_json` (`trends.py:289,471`) and `compute_year_on_year` (`:360`) for trends, `compute_review_rollup` (`reviews.py:355`) for the latest review conclusions, plus recent activities and sleep history. Compose a compact app-state block so the questions Mark actually asks ("has my HRV been trending down?") are answerable from data the app has already computed. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** deterministic pre-assembly (cheap, no client change) vs a real tool-use retrieval loop, which would require extending `generate_anthropic_text` (`anthropic_text.py:150-159`) with tool support — recommend pre-assembly for v1 and tool-use only if the block proves too coarse. Also decide the token budget and what gets truncated first.<br>178.4 **Tests + DECISIONS.** A trend question answers from the real series instead of refusing; a question about an activity completed after the read's generation is answered rather than being invisible; the not-known path produces a coach's sentence with no internal vocabulary; the Batch 175 general-science lane and its `General principle:` label still work; Mark-specific figures are still never invented; Red-never-VO2 and every deterministic floor hold; a plan-change request still yields propose/confirm and no direct mutation. | Make the conversation answer from what the app already knows — the week, trends, reviews, recent sessions, current state — instead of from one frozen document, and stop it telling Mark about "the packet". This is the substance behind his "almost disconnected", and it is worth doing on its own even if Batch 179 never ships. | Chat context is assembled at ask-time and includes app state beyond the single read (week ahead, trend series, latest review conclusions, recent activities, sleep history), reusing the existing deterministic builders rather than duplicating them; a question whose answer is already rendered elsewhere in the app is answered rather than refused; an event occurring after the read's generation is visible to that read's chat; no answer contains internal schema vocabulary on any path, including the not-known path; Mark-specific metrics/plan/history are still never invented and the Batch 175 labelled general-science lane is preserved; the deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder, Red-never-VO2 and all floors are unchanged, and plan changes remain propose/confirm (Decision #29). Backend-only; no migration (context assembly, not storage). Backend pytest/ruff/mypy green. Surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-30 feedback. **Shipped via PR #207 / squash `5d5abee`** (Decision #258): new `services/chat_context.py` assembles the conversation's context at ask-time — the stored packet stays the read's own record and an `appState` block is layered alongside it carrying a `sinceThisRead` delta, today's plan, the week ahead, the monthly trend series + year-on-year, the latest review conclusions, recent sessions and sleep history — reusing `TrainingWeekService.build_window` (new windowed entry point, explicit subject-date anchor) and a new public `TrendsService.windows`, with stored review rows rather than a recomputed rollup. Deterministic pre-assembly, not tool-use; 30k-character budget with every trim named in `omittedForLength`. Prompt → `brief-chat-v5-2026-07-30` keeps the internal nouns to the single sentence that forbids them. Branch-push, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI all passed seven jobs (1043 pytest against Postgres); one branch push failed pytest first on a same-date fixture needing distinct plan versions, fixed in `11b69c8`. **Production deploy needed the documented break-glass path:** a Railway platform incident (2026-07-30 16:32 UTC) paused Hobby-plan deploys and dropped the merge webhook, so `railway redeploy --from-source` shipped `main` HEAD once the incident cleared. Production verified on `5d5abee`: Railway direct + Vercel same-origin health both returned the SHA, `health/ready` db ok, web `/` 200, and protected brief-chat POST + daily-loop 401 direct and proxied. |
| ~~Batch 179 — One coach conversation, reachable from anywhere~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | **Depends on Batch 178 — do not start before it ships.** 179.1 **Let a conversation exist without a document.** `BriefMessage.analysis_id` is `NOT NULL` with an FK to `analyses` (`coaching.py:565`), which is why chat can only hang off a read and why the Sleep page — which has no `Analysis` of its own, borrowing `morningAnalysis` (`SleepPage.tsx:123`) — cannot host one without inventing a sleep analysis row purely as a conversation anchor. Make the anchor optional. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** nullable `analysis_id` (smaller migration, keeps one table) vs a proper `conversations` plus `conversation_messages` pair (cleaner threading, larger change). Either way this is the batch's one migration.<br>179.2 **Do not silently break the learning loop.** `conversation_learning` inner-joins `BriefMessage` to `Analysis` in both its source query (`conversation_learning.py:433-434`) and its single-record lookup (`:717-718`). Making the anchor nullable without touching these means every unanchored message vanishes from Batch 151's learning pipeline with no error — the best conversations would stop being learning sources. Convert to outer joins and add a regression test asserting an unanchored user message is picked up as a learning candidate.<br>179.3 **Key the capability gate off today's state, not the document type.** `_analysis_allows_adjustment_proposal` (`brief_chat.py:182-186`) tests `analysis_type == "morning"` as a proxy for "there is a live adjustable workout"; the real question is already answered deterministically by `_todays_adjustable_workout_id` (`:160-179`). Re-key the gate to today's actual plan state so the propose affordance appears exactly when a live adjustable workout exists, from any entry point, and never on a retrospective one. The deterministic keyword check on Mark's own words stays, the model still triggers nothing, and the propose→approve→push gate (Decision #29) is untouched.<br>179.4 **One entry point, context seeded by origin.** Replace the three read-scoped mounts (`MorningBriefPage.tsx:108`, `DashboardPage.tsx:1856`, `WorkoutDetailSheet.tsx:231`) with a single coach surface reachable app-wide, including from Sleep and the breathwork/strength/walking briefs that have no analysis row. Opening it from a page seeds that context ("we're talking about last night's sleep") without fencing the conversation to it. Rethink `MAX_USER_TURNS_PER_ANALYSIS` and `MAX_HISTORY_TURNS_IN_PROMPT` (`brief_chat.py:50-51`) as per-thread rather than per-document, so a conversation is not lost when the read rolls over. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** thread model (one rolling thread vs threads with optional read anchors), history retention, and whether the existing per-read history is migrated or left addressable in place.<br>179.5 **Hoist the guardrails into one policy surface.** The verdict floors, anti-sycophancy rules and honesty rules currently live per-read and drift apart; with one conversation they should be one shared module, applied wherever the coach speaks. Tests + DECISIONS: the floors hold identically from every entry point.<br>179.6 **Tests + DECISIONS.** A conversation can start with no analysis anchor and persists across reads; unanchored messages reach the learning pipeline; the propose affordance appears for a live adjustable workout from any entry point and never on a retrospective read; chat works from Sleep with no analysis row invented; existing per-read history remains readable. | Turn N amnesiac per-document chats into one coach Mark can talk to from anywhere, because the discontinuity is the cause of "almost disconnected" — today the schema asserts a conversation cannot exist without a document, which is why there is no way to just ask the coach something. | The chat is reachable app-wide including from surfaces with no `Analysis` row (Sleep, breathwork/strength/walking briefs), with context seeded by origin but not fenced to it; a conversation can exist with no analysis anchor and survives a read rolling over; unanchored messages are still learning sources (outer join, regression-tested) so Batch 151 is not silently degraded; the propose affordance is gated on today's real plan state rather than `analysis_type`, appears from any entry point when a live adjustable workout exists, never on a retrospective read, and Decision #29's propose→approve→push gate is unchanged; verdict floors and anti-sycophancy rules live in one shared policy surface and hold identically from every entry point; existing per-read history stays readable. One migration (anchor optionality, shape decided at `/batch-start`); frontend surface change; no provider/auth/hosting change and no verdict change. Backend pytest/ruff/mypy plus web test/build green. Surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-30 feedback. **Shipped via PR #208 / squash `94f10b8`** (Decision #259). **Kickoff decisions:** *179.1* one rolling thread with a nullable anchor rather than a `conversations`/`conversation_messages` pair — the rows already were the thread, so migration `026` drops the `NOT NULL`, adds `origin_kind`/`origin_date` for the surfaces with no read plus `ix_brief_messages_user_created`, and existing per-read history stays readable **in place**; RLS needed no work (019 already enabled it). *179.3* the acceptance criteria's ambiguous "never on a retrospective read" resolved as **purely today's live plan state** — a completed or skipped ride already yields no affordance, while blocking a genuine "make today easier" asked from yesterday's read would recreate the fencing this batch removes; `ChatContext.adjustable_workout_id` answers it from live rows (active, unclosed, structured, bike, not inside a holiday window), superseding Batch 178's subject-date liveness set, which was removed rather than left as a second wrong answer. *179.4* the three inline read chats **stay** and still list only their own read's turns; both surfaces render one extracted `CoachConversation` into the same thread, reached app-wide from `CoachLauncher` + `GET|POST /api/v1/coach/messages`, with a controlled origin vocabulary that degrades unknown values so client text can never enter the prompt. The cap moved from per-read (10) to per-local-day (20). *179.5* `services/coach_policy.py` is a **registry**, not a rewrite of five prompts: the conversation composes from it and the deterministic reads are audited against it (`missing_floors`), because the walk/strength/mobility reads legitimately state neither Red-never-VO2 nor the power-balance floor and making them recite inapplicable rules would bump every prompt version. Prompt → `coach-chat-v6-2026-07-31`. **179.2 was the silent-regression risk:** `conversation_learning` inner-joined `BriefMessage` to `Analysis` in both its source query and its evidence lookup, so a nullable anchor alone would have dropped every unanchored message out of Batch 151's pipeline with no error at all; both are now outer joins, regression-tested. Branch-push, PR-context and post-merge `main` CI all passed seven jobs (1059 pytest against Postgres); the first branch push failed pytest alone on the same fixture class that failed Batch 178's first push — two plan rows on one `workout_date` are siblings needing ascending versions. Production verified on `94f10b8`: exact-SHA Railway + Vercel health, `health/ready` db ok, web `/` 200, and 401 direct/proxied on the new coach surface, the per-read surface and daily-loop. Migration verified live via read-only `railway run` — head `026`, anchor nullable, both origin columns and the new index present, all 64 existing messages preserved. |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-08-01 — Mark feedback 2026-07-30/08-01: the day the app reads is not the day Mark lived

Feedback wave from Mark on 2026-07-30 and 2026-08-01 (screenshots `WhatsApp Image 2026-07-30 at 09.33.25/26/27` in `~/Downloads/Dad Fitness/`), reconciled against the code and **confirmed against prod on 2026-08-01**. Mark reported the morning brief quoting *"the all-day stress average was a very low 13"* for a day his Garmin showed as **24**, and inferred we were reading his *sleep* stress. He is right about the symptom, and the fix is not the one his diagnosis implies: **we are not reading the wrong field, we are reading the right field too early and never again.** Garmin's stress and Body-Battery aggregates are computed over a local-midnight-to-now window (`stress.startTimestampLocal`/`endTimestampLocal`; canonical sample `~/garmin-spike/out/stress.json`), and the scheduler syncs only `_profile_today(profile)` (`scheduler.py:277,283,874`) — so the morning run freezes each `DailyMetric` at ~09:00 over a window that is ~85% sleep (2026-07-29: 8h04m asleep inside a 9h39m window), and nothing ever re-reads it. Prod proof from the stored `raw_payload`: every row written by `garmin_history_backfill.py:188` ends at midnight with plausible whole-day values (18 Jun — stress 29, Body Battery drained 75, ended 18), while **every live-synced row since ~25 June ends 08:00–10:24** and carries a waking snapshot (31 Jul — stress 12, drained 1, ended 92; Mark's completed day was **28 / 70 / 16**). Our "daytime stress" therefore tracks his sleep stress to within 0–3 points on almost every live day, which is precisely why his sleep-stress inference fitted the evidence. The blast radius runs well past the sentence he quoted: the same truncation corrupts `body_battery_drained`/`body_battery_end` (both narrated by Batch 174's `wholeDayCost`, `morning_analysis.py:1627-1647`) and the `daytime_stress_avg` chronic driver (`insights.py:822` → `chronic_patterns.py`, `rem_interventions.py`, `sleep_projection.py`, `experiment_evaluation.py`), and it introduces a **step discontinuity at 24/25 June** between correct backfilled history and truncated live rows, so any trend or chronic comparison spanning that boundary sees a fabricated late-June collapse in stress. The deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder is **not** affected — `_morning_verdict` (`morning_analysis.py:1867`) consumes neither field — so Mark's "false green light" worry does not hold literally, though the narrative did draw false reassurance from it ("a genuinely low-cost day") and chronic detection genuinely is degraded. Three batches: **Batch 180** finishes the day and repairs the history; **Batch 181** stops the coach asserting a stale packet is *correct* when Mark contradicts it from his own device; **Batch 182** revisits what evidence justifies a week-long deload. **Batch 182 was blocked on a design answer from Mark; he answered on 2026-08-01 and it is now partially unblocked** — the other two are defects with an obvious correct answer, 182 is a judgement about how much evidence should buy a week-long intervention. Rather than name a threshold he asked the app to derive it (*"I would hope the app could effectively tell me from it's knowledge"*), so 404 days of his history were analysed against his reply; findings, caveats and reproduction SQL are in **`docs/reviews/deload-escalation-evidence.md`**, and the headline is that his recovery markers are *worst* after rest and that a Red driven by planned training debt is trivially separable from a Red driven by a systemic crash. 180 and 181 are independent and may ship in either order, though 180 first is preferred so 181's tests are not written against known-bad data. Nothing here touches the deterministic ladder (verdict-engine safety rule) and plan changes stay on the propose/confirm rail (Decision #29). Decision numbers assigned at `/batch-start`; rows stay `Planned`.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 180 — Daily rows finish the day, not the morning~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 180.1 **Re-sync the previous day once it has closed.** `_sync_garmin_daily` fetches and stores only `_profile_today(profile)` (`scheduler.py:277-288`), so a `DailyMetric` is written once, mid-morning, and never revisited; `parse_daily_metric_fields` (`garmin_sync.py:426-483`) then reads whole-day aggregates — `stress_avg` from `avgStressLevel or averageStressLevel` (`:461`) and `body_battery_charged/drained/end` (`:462-469`) — out of a window that has barely started. Re-sync D-1 alongside D in the morning run so yesterday is re-read after it has actually finished. `sync_daily` already upserts on `(user_id, calendar_date)` (`garmin_sync.py:331-343`), so this corrects the row in place with no migration and no new endpoint. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** D-1 only vs a short rolling re-sync window (D-1..D-3, which also self-heals a missed run); whether the extra Garmin fetch rides inside the existing `_retry_sync` backoff and its 429 budget; and whether a same-day evening pass is worth it or D-1 alone is sufficient.<br>180.2 **Never present a truncated window as a finished day.** The payload states its own coverage (`startTimestampLocal`/`endTimestampLocal`), so partial-day is *detectable*, not a guess. Carry the window (or a derived completeness flag/as-of time) onto the daily row or resolve it at packet-build, and make `_yesterday_load_packet`'s `wholeDayCost` (`morning_analysis.py:1627-1647`, `allDayStressAvg` at `:1631`) either omit the figures or state them as incomplete when the window does not cover the day. This is the durable guard: even with 180.1 shipped, a missed or failed overnight sync must degrade to honest silence rather than to a confident wrong number. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** persisted column vs packet-build resolution; the completeness predicate (window end ≥ local midnight vs a coverage fraction); and whether the same guard is applied to the `daytime_stress_avg` chronic driver (`insights.py:822`) so a partial day is excluded from baselines rather than counted low.<br>180.3 **Repair the stored history.** Roughly five weeks of rows (~25 June → present, ~38 days) hold a waking snapshot; the earlier backfilled rows are correct, so the series currently contains a fabricated step change at the boundary. Re-run the existing backfill path (`garmin_history_backfill.py:188` → the same `sync_daily` upsert) over the affected range to overwrite them with completed-day values. **Decided 2026-08-01: the repair rides in this batch**, not staged separately — the fix and the history it invalidates land together, so Mark is never reading a corrected brief against uncorrected trends, and the fabricated late-June step is not left standing behind a working sync. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** the exact start date (verify the boundary from `endTimestampLocal` rather than assuming 25 June); and whether personal baselines / chronic flags need explicit recomputation afterwards or regenerate naturally.<br>180.4 **Tests + DECISIONS.** A payload whose window ends mid-morning is not presented as a whole day (packet omits or flags it); a re-sync of a past date overwrites the earlier snapshot in place via the existing upsert; a completed-day payload yields the whole-day figures; the 31 July regression case specifically (stored 12 / 1 / 92 → 28 / 70 / 16) is covered from a fixture; the deterministic verdict is unchanged either way. | Stop writing each day's Body Battery and stress from a window that is ~85% sleep and never re-reading it — finish the day, refuse to narrate an unfinished one as finished, and repair the five weeks already stored wrong. | The morning run re-syncs the previous day (window decided at `/batch-start`) so `DailyMetric` holds completed-day aggregates rather than a ~09:00 snapshot; the upsert corrects rows in place with no migration. A daily payload whose window does not cover the calendar day can be detected from `startTimestampLocal`/`endTimestampLocal`, and `wholeDayCost` omits or explicitly qualifies stress and Body-Battery figures in that case rather than stating them as the day's totals — so a missed overnight sync degrades to silence, never to a confident wrong number. The ~38 affected rows since the backfill/live boundary are repaired from Garmin, removing the step discontinuity that currently makes late June look like a sharp drop in stress; whether baselines/chronic flags are recomputed is decided at `/batch-start`. Deterministic tests cover the partial-window guard, in-place re-sync, the completed-day path, and the 31 July regression case. The Green/Amber/Red ladder is untouched (`_morning_verdict` consumes neither field). Backend-only; no shared-schema break; optional daily-row column decided at `/batch-start`. Backend pytest/ruff/mypy green. Surfaced by Mark's 2026-07-30 and 2026-08-01 feedback; root cause prod-confirmed 2026-08-01. |
| ~~Batch 181 — The coach reports what it was given, and never insists it is true~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 181.1 **Distinguish "what I was handed" from "what is true".** Mark challenged the figures with the correct ones off his own watch and, in his words, *"its answer said this was correct data from the packet it got."* The chat is packet-bound by design (Batch 175 / Decision #255) and had no way to see Garmin — that fence is right and stays — but being unable to verify a number is not grounds for asserting it is *correct*. Add the rule to the shared policy registry `services/coach_policy.py` (`FLOORS`, `:80-111`; Batch 179 / Decision #259) so it composes into the coach conversation and is audited into the deterministic reads: figures are reported as what the app recorded, never asserted as ground truth about Mark's body.<br>181.2 **When Mark contradicts a figure from his own device, his reading wins.** The correct response to "you said 12, it was actually 28" is to accept the discrepancy, say plainly that the app's stored figure disagrees with his device and that his device is the better evidence, and treat it as a data problem to surface — not to defend the packet. This must not become blanket capitulation: the coach still holds its ground on *coaching judgement* (the Red-never-VO2 floor, the deterministic verdict, propose/confirm), and the existing anti-sycophancy rules stay. The boundary is **observed data vs coaching decisions** — Mark is the authority on the former, the deterministic engine on the latter. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** whether a contradiction is merely acknowledged in-conversation, or also recorded (Batch 151/179 conversational learning already captures these turns — a user correcting a metric is arguably the highest-signal learning event we get) and surfaced to admin as a data-quality flag.<br>181.3 **Tests + DECISIONS.** A packet figure the user disputes produces acknowledgement, not insistence, and no claim that the stored value is correct; the coach still refuses to move a Red-day VO2 session or the deterministic verdict when pushed (anti-sycophancy intact); the Batch 175 `General principle:` lane and Batch 178's no-internal-vocabulary rule still hold; Mark-specific figures are still never invented. | Stop the coach telling Mark he is wrong about his own data when he is right — report figures as what the app recorded rather than as fact, and when his device contradicts them, treat his reading as the better evidence. | The coach never asserts that a stored figure is *correct*, only that it is what the app recorded, and says so in a coach's register with no internal vocabulary (Batch 178 rule preserved); when the user contradicts a figure from their own device, the answer acknowledges the discrepancy and defers to their reading rather than defending the stored value. The distinction between **observed data** (user is the authority) and **coaching judgement** (deterministic engine is the authority) is explicit, so the change cannot be read as licence to capitulate on the verdict, the floors, or propose/confirm — regression tests prove Red-never-VO2 and the deterministic verdict still hold under pressure. The rule lives in `coach_policy.py` so it applies wherever the coach speaks rather than in one prompt. Whether a disputed figure is also recorded as a data-quality signal is decided at `/batch-start`. Backend prompt-policy + tests/docs only; no migration, frontend, provider, auth, hosting, or verdict change. Backend pytest/ruff/mypy green. | ~~Batch 182 — A deload that understands why the Red happened~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | **Unblocked 2026-08-04 — and the shape changed.** Mark answered the outstanding question and the answer *removed* the duration cap this batch was going to carry: **eight of the nine low-training stretches were holidays**, so the day-4 inflection in `docs/reviews/deload-escalation-evidence.md` is mostly a travel effect, not a rest effect. His own reading is the disciplined one — *"don't think right to say 100% of impact was holiday and expect not training would at least have been a factor… impossible to establish what % … which means it is more something to monitor going forward."* He also corrected two readings of his position: rearranging a week is **a tool he actively uses and wants** (*"a key tool I currently use so I'm totally bought into it"* — swap Tuesday's VO2 for a Z2 when below par, preserving the weekly mix), and the assumption that he would train at all costs is wrong — when Copilot dropped recovery weeks from his 13-week block **he was the one who challenged it and argued for keeping them** (corroborated in `plan_blocks`: W04–W08 are five consecutive BUILD weeks). Evidence doc amended 2026-08-04.<br>182.1 **The red-cluster path is acute evidence wearing chronic clothing.** `_chronic_action_signal` (`chronic_patterns.py:636-691`) has two independent triggers and they are wildly asymmetric. The sustained-marker path requires a recovery marker (`readiness_score`, `hrv_7_day_avg_ms`, `resting_heart_rate_bpm`; `:39-41`) to miss its personal band on **≥70% of ≥10 samples** (`CHRONIC_ACTION_MISS_RATIO = 0.7`, `MIN_METRIC_SAMPLES = 10`, `:32-34`) inside a **≥21-night** window (`MIN_OBSERVED_NIGHTS = 21`, `:31`). The red-cluster path requires **2 Red mornings in 7 days** (`CHRONIC_ACTION_RED_THRESHOLD = 2`, `CHRONIC_ACTION_RED_WINDOW_DAYS = 7`, `:35-36`; `:678`) — no sample floor, no requirement that anything else look bad — and yields the same seven-day deload. Prod 2026-08-01: Red, Red, Green, Amber, Green, Green, Green, Green, Green, Amber over ten mornings, so two adjacent Reds against six Greens fired a full week's easing. Mark's structural objection: his plan puts three sessions Tue–Thu with two of them hard, so **every Friday has a standing chance of being Red by design** — meaning one further poor night is all it ever takes. The Red calls themselves are sound (`_morning_verdict` runs off sleep/readiness/HRV and never consumed the fields Batch 180 fixed); this is an escalation-policy defect. Also re-word the capability text, which says *"a sustained run of poor recovery"* while the red path clears on two mornings.<br>182.2 **Qualify a Red by why it happened, using two signals we already store.** *Physiology:* **Fri 31 Jul** was readiness 20 POOR on `recovery_time_min` **2584** (43 h of debt from a Tue/Wed/Thu block of 214/66/198) while HRV was 52 BALANCED and RHR 43, his lowest of the fortnight — an expected, planned consequence of training on healthy systemic markers. **Sat 1 Aug** was HRV 52 → 35, RHR 43 → 48, sleep stress 12 → 28 — a genuine acute hit. *Mark's own words:* **he explained both Reds in the check-in before either brief ran, and the escalation read neither.** Saturday's `manual_entries` row at 08:44 carried `subjective_score` 3, feel *"Have a bit of a hangover today"*, notes *"Was out last night drinking… 13 uk units… poor sleep and feel the effects this morning"* — and the morning analysis generated at **08:45:49, one minute later**. Friday's carried *"presumably due to a harder day's training yesterday and cumulative 3 day training load"*. Qualify a Red before it counts toward `red_count` (`:678`) from `recovery_time_min` / `acute_load` / HRV / RHR against `metric_baselines`, **plus the check-in `feel` and `notes` text for the day**. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** the physiological predicate and its lookback; whether check-in text is interpreted by the model or matched deterministically (the verdict path must stay deterministic, so a model reading can qualify escalation only); whether an explained Red is excluded or down-weighted.<br>182.3 **Know where he is in the plan before proposing to change it.** The 1 Aug proposal was not merely severe, it was **redundant** — `plan_blocks` already held **PN2 W03 RECOVERY, 2026-08-03 → 08-09**, a scheduled recovery week starting two days later, and the escalation read none of it. Mark: *"if we are moving into a '1' lighter week anyway, then plan already allowing for this and it doesn't need to change anything."* The plan is a 2121 (two build weeks, one lighter) and manages fatigue by design. Read `plan_blocks.block_type` / `start_date` / `end_date` and suppress or soften a chronic proposal when a recovery, taper or consolidation week is already scheduled inside the horizon it would cover. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** suppress entirely vs propose only the part the plan does not already cover; the horizon.<br>182.4 **Propose a rearrange, not a reduction.** The app's only chronic lever is a *deload* (75% duration, one zone drop, no HIT — Batch 171). What Mark actually does, and wants, is **rearrange**: swap a hard session for an easier one within the week so the weekly mix survives and each session type lands on a day his feel and metrics support. These are different interventions and he is bought into the one we do not offer. For a short cluster, propose a swap (defer the hard session, pull an easier one forward) and reserve the deload for the sustained-marker path. The restructure engine and endpoints already exist from the 2026-07-07 wave. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** reuse the existing restructure preview→apply path vs a narrower swap proposal; whether it stays on the propose→approve→push rail (it should — Decision #29).<br>182.5 **Record why a quiet period happened.** Nothing in the schema says *why* training stopped — the gap that made the whole 404-day analysis unfalsifiable and cost a round-trip to Mark to resolve. His conclusion was *"something to monitor going forward"*, which is exactly right and small: capture a reason for a low-training stretch (holiday, illness, travel, deliberate rest) so the question is answerable from data next time instead of from memory. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** whether this is inferred from existing plan/holiday state plus check-in text, or a light explicit capture; whether it ships here or splits out.<br>182.6 **Tests + DECISIONS.** Two adjacent Reds in an otherwise-Green week do not by themselves propose a seven-day deload; a Red carrying heavy recovery debt with intact HRV/RHR does not count toward escalation while a markers-crashed Red does; a check-in naming a cause is honoured; a chronic proposal is suppressed when a recovery week is already scheduled; a short cluster yields a swap proposal rather than a week-long reduction; the sustained-marker path still fires unchanged; nothing here can soften today's deterministic Green/Amber/Red (verdict-engine safety rule); proposals stay approval-gated and idempotent (Batch 171 / Decision #252). | Stop two explained bad mornings buying a week-long reduction — read why the Red happened, check whether the plan already handles it, and offer the rearrange Mark actually uses instead of a deload he does not want. | A Red is qualified before it counts toward chronic escalation, using stored physiology (recovery debt vs HRV/RHR against baseline) and the check-in text Mark already writes — both 31 Jul (training debt, markers intact) and 1 Aug (explicitly reported drinking) are recognised as explained and neither feeds a seven-day proposal. A chronic proposal is suppressed or narrowed when `plan_blocks` already schedules a recovery/taper/consolidation week across the horizon it would cover, so the 1 Aug case (RECOVERY week starting 3 Aug) produces nothing. For a short cluster the app proposes a **rearrange** — deferring a hard session and pulling an easier one forward, weekly mix intact — with the deload reserved for the sustained-marker path (≥70% miss over ≥10 samples in a ≥21-night window), which is unchanged. The capability wording matches the bar that actually applies. Reasons for low-training stretches become recordable so the rest-versus-recovery question is answerable from data in future. Nothing alters the deterministic daily verdict; proposals stay approval-gated, idempotent and on the propose→approve→push rail (Decision #29). Backend-first; any restructure surface reuses the existing engine. Backend pytest/ruff/mypy green. ## Post-roadmap — 2026-08-04 — Mark feedback: proactive guidance, not just day-by-day

Mark asked on 2026-08-04 for the coach to guide him **proactively** on training and sleep, rather than only day-by-day or when he asks a question. Reconciled against the code, the gap is not that the app lacks a longer horizon — it is that **everything with a horizon longer than one day is pull-only, and the one thing the app says unprompted every day is a fixed string.** Three findings. **(1)** The evening nudge is the highest-frequency proactive touchpoint in the app and the least intelligent thing in it: `build_evening_nudge_plan` (`nudge_alerts.py:132`) returns the same hardcoded body every night — *"20:00 breathing, pre-cool the room toward 17C, finish snack by 21:30, seal the bedroom near 22:00, bed 23:15"* — regardless of what Mark did that day or what the room and the forecast are about to do, while `project_sleep` (`sleep_projection.py:72`) already derives a tone (`routine`/`protect`/`watch`), a headline, evidence and up to two `prep_actions` from exactly that data and serves them only into the pull-only daily-loop payload (`daily_loop.py:1180` → `:1445`). **(2)** `ReviewService` is constructed in exactly two places, both in `routers/reviews.py` (`:173`, `:194`) — **no scheduler job ever creates one** — so the weekly and monthly reviews, the most substantial multi-day artefacts the app builds, exist only if Mark opens Reviews and taps "Generate review" (`ReviewsPage.tsx:247`). **(3)** The same holds for every other multi-day computation: the chronic 28-day patterns and their action signal (`chronic_patterns.py:1015`), experiment evaluations (`experiment_evaluation.py:464`), weekly-mix `at_risk` accounting (`weekly_mix.py:183`), the rotating REM lever library (`rem_interventions.py:187`) — all computed, none ever mentioned unless he opens the screen that renders it. And nothing anywhere is *forward*-looking: the Week tab is a schedule, not guidance about what the coming week asks of him.

**The delivery rail is the coach thread, and it needs no migration.** Batch 179 (Decision #259) already made `BriefMessage.analysis_id` nullable, added `origin_kind`/`origin_date` for surfaces with no read, and `role` already takes `user`/`assistant` (`coaching.py:546,576-584`) — so a coach-*initiated* message is simply an assistant row in the one rolling thread. That is better than four new notification types: proactivity gets a single home, Mark can reply to it in place, and because 179.2 converted the learning joins to outer joins those turns still feed Batch 151's conversational learning. New origins go into the controlled `ORIGIN_KINDS` vocabulary (`chat_context.py:119`), never as free text — that vocabulary exists precisely so app-supplied strings cannot reach the prompt.

**Two guardrails bind all four batches.** *Proactive is not the same as more notifications:* the static 20:00 nudge is the cautionary case — identical words every night is how a channel stops being read — so every unprompted message must earn its place by saying something Mark does not already know, and Batch 187 carries an explicit budget. *Batch 182's discipline carries forward:* Decision #263 established that acute evidence is qualified before it buys an intervention, and nothing here may notify off evidence 182 would have excluded — a proactive "you may be overreaching" fired on two explained Reds is worse than silence. **Sequencing:** 184 is independent and can ship first; **185 must precede 187**, which delivers on the coach-message rail 185 builds; 186 is best folded into 185's Sunday message rather than becoming a second push; and 185.4 builds directly on the chat components **Batch 183 shipped**. Nothing here touches the deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder (verdict-engine safety rule) and any plan change stays on the propose/confirm rail (Decision #29). Decision numbers assigned at `/batch-start`; rows stay `Planned`.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 184 — Tonight's nudge is about tonight~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 184.1 **Lift the projection's input assembly out of the router.** `project_sleep` (`sleep_projection.py:72`) is pure and reusable, but everything it consumes is assembled router-locally: `_activity_training_signals` (`daily_loop.py:1144`), the measured sleep drivers from `InsightsService.cached_drivers` (`:1342`), the snapshot's `sleep_protocol` and overnight weather, and the fresh bedroom temperature (`:1346`). A scheduler job cannot reach any of it without duplicating that assembly — which is why the nudge sends a constant. Move the assembly into a service that both the router and the nudge call, so the evening push, the daily-loop payload and the Sleep page can never disagree about tonight. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** a new leaf service vs extending an existing one; whether the nudge reuses `cached_drivers` or tolerates a colder read; how it degrades when the bedroom reading is stale (`environment_freshness.py` already answers that question).<br>184.2 **Send tonight's projection, and stay quiet when there is nothing to add.** Replace the hardcoded body in `build_evening_nudge_plan` (`nudge_alerts.py:132`) with the projection's `headline` plus its one or two `prep_actions` (`sleep_projection.py:136`), carrying the tone as the push severity. The fixed protocol stays as the honest fallback for `status="fallback"` — fewer than `MIN_DRIVER_SAMPLES` (8) measured drivers, or no training signals at all (`:7`, `:151`) — which is correct behaviour, not a defect. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** whether a `routine` night sends at all. A nightly *"tonight looks like a standard protocol night"* re-creates precisely the ignorable-channel problem this batch exists to fix; the recommendation is that `protect`/`watch` always send, while `routine` and fallback nights send the protocol at most once a week.<br>184.3 **Tests + DECISIONS.** A `protect` night names the real driver (late or hard session, warm room, warm overnight low) rather than reciting the protocol; a fallback night degrades to the protocol text; a `routine` night obeys whichever quiet rule is chosen; per-day idempotency through `_send_once`'s `sleep-protocol-{date}` tag (`nudge_alerts.py:703`) is unchanged; the 20:00 local fire window (`EVENING_NUDGE_TIME`, `EVENING_NUDGE_WINDOW_MIN` = 20, `:40-41`) and the holiday-away skip (`scheduler.py:196-206`) still hold; the push and the Sleep page describe the same projection. | Make the app's most frequent unprompted message say something true about tonight — it already computes exactly that and currently shows it only to someone who opens the app. | The 20:00 nudge carries tonight's projected sleep risk — a headline plus up to two prep actions derived from today's actual training, the measured sleep drivers, the live bedroom temperature and the overnight forecast — with the fixed protocol retained as the fallback whenever the projection is unpersonalized, so the nudge never invents a driver it has not measured. The projection is assembled once in a service, so the evening push, the daily-loop payload and the Sleep page cannot disagree about the same night. A `routine` night does not repeat the same words nightly (exact quiet rule decided at `/batch-start`). Per-day idempotency, the 20:00 window and the holiday-away skip are unchanged; the deterministic verdict is untouched. Backend-only; no migration or shared-schema change. Backend pytest/ruff/mypy green. | ~~Batch 185 — The weekly review arrives instead of waiting to be asked for~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 185.1 **Schedule the review.** `ReviewService` is constructed only from the router (`reviews.py:173,194`), so the weekly review is generated only on a tap. `run` is already idempotent per period — it returns the stored review untouched unless `prompt_version` moved or `force` is set (`:630-647`) — and already runs its Anthropic call inside `workload_slot`, so it is safe to fire unattended. Add the job and register it in the single-job runner (`run_scheduled.py:46-54`), which is how wall-clock work actually fires in prod: the in-process APScheduler is unreliable in the Railway container (`docs/runbooks/scheduled-jobs-cron.md`). Note that `resolve_period_window` (`reviews.py:129`) defines weekly as the ISO week *containing* `as_of`, so the run date selects the week — a Sunday-evening run reviews the week just ending, whereas a Monday-morning run would review a new and empty one. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** the exact local time; whether the monthly review is also scheduled at month end or stays manual; the holiday-away skip; and the behaviour when the Anthropic call fails (Batch 141's failure card + admin alert is the existing pattern to reuse, not reinvent).<br>185.2 **Deliver it into the coach thread, not as a document to go and fetch.** Write an assistant-role `BriefMessage` (`coaching.py:546`) carrying the review's conclusions, anchored by `analysis_id` to the stored review row, with a new entry in `ORIGIN_KINDS` (`chat_context.py:119`) naming the surface. No migration is required — Batch 179 already made the anchor nullable and `role` already takes `assistant` (`:576-584`). Mark's reply then lands in the same thread with the review as its anchor, and Batch 178's ask-time assembly gives that conversation the rest of the app's state for free. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** whether the message is the review's own opening lines or a separately generated short summary.<br>185.3 **One push, carrying the conclusion.** Reuse `_send_once` (`nudge_alerts.py:703`) so the weekly push is idempotent per (profile, subject_date) exactly like every other notification. The body must be the single most important conclusion of the week — never "your weekly review is ready", which is the failure mode this whole plan exists to avoid. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** the deep-link target (`/reviews` vs the coach thread).<br>185.4 **Make an unread coach message visible.** `CoachLauncher` (`CoachLauncher.tsx:42`) fetches the thread but has no notion of an unseen turn — with the coach now speaking first, the thread is only a delivery surface if Mark can see that something is waiting in it. Batch 183 has already shipped the per-message dates on these same components, so this extends them rather than competing with them. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** a client-side last-seen marker (no migration) vs a persisted read marker (a migration).<br>185.5 **Tests + DECISIONS.** The scheduled run generates the ISO week just ended rather than the week starting; re-running the same week neither regenerates nor re-notifies; an assistant-authored message appears in the thread and Mark's reply anchors to the review; the push body carries a conclusion rather than an announcement; the new origin resolves through `normalize_origin_kind` (`chat_context.py:137`) while unknown values still degrade to `general`; coach-initiated turns still reach the Batch 151 learning pipeline through 179.2's outer joins. | Turn the app's most substantial multi-day artefact from something Mark has to remember to generate into something that arrives and starts a conversation. | The weekly review is generated on a schedule (cadence and local time decided at `/batch-start`) through the existing idempotent `ReviewService.run`, registered in the single-job runner so it fires reliably in prod, and covering the ISO week that just ended rather than the one starting. It is delivered as an assistant-authored turn in the app-wide coach thread — anchored to the stored review, with its origin added to the controlled vocabulary rather than passed as free text — so Mark can reply in place and Batch 178's ask-time context answers the follow-up. Exactly one push accompanies it, idempotent per day through the existing `_send_once` rail, and its body states the week's most important conclusion rather than announcing that a document exists. An unseen coach turn is visible on the launcher (mechanism decided at `/batch-start`). Coach-initiated turns remain learning sources; the deterministic ladder, the floors and propose/confirm are untouched. Backend-first plus the launcher change; migration only if a persisted read marker is chosen. Production exact-SHA Railway/Vercel health, DB readiness, web and protected weekly-review route smokes passed. Surfaced by Mark's 2026-08-04 request for proactive guidance. |
| ~~Batch 187 — The coach speaks when something changes~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | **Depends on Batch 185 — it delivers on the coach-message rail 185 builds.** 187.1 **Detect transitions, not levels.** Scheduled messages are only half of proactive; the other half is noticing. The app already computes state that genuinely *changes* — the chronic suggestion status (`insufficient_history` / `clear` / `active`) and its action signal (`chronic_patterns.py:1015`), experiment evaluations reaching an outcome (`experiment_evaluation.py:464`), a weekly-mix bucket going `at_risk` while there is still time to fix it (`weekly_mix.py:183`) — but a level is re-derived every day, so notifying on one would produce a daily message about a standing condition. Fire only on a *transition*. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** a persisted last-notified state (a migration) vs deriving the previous state from the stored `analyses` rows that already record each evaluation.<br>187.2 **Route through Batch 185's rail, under a hard budget.** Coach-initiated messages land in the one thread rather than spawning new notification types. Cap unprompted state-change messages at roughly one per week: if two transitions fire together the more important one wins and the other waits or is dropped, because a burst is how a channel is taught to be ignored. **Decide at `/batch-start`:** the ranking between transition kinds, the budget window, and whether a suppressed transition is queued or discarded.<br>187.3 **Inherit Batch 182's qualification rather than re-deriving it.** Decision #263 established that acute evidence must be qualified before it buys an intervention — the deterministic cause classifier reads the check-in text Mark already writes, and a Red carrying recovery debt with healthy HRV and RHR is excluded from cluster escalation. Nothing in this batch may notify on evidence 182 would have excluded, and a transition that 182's plan-awareness would suppress (a recovery, taper or consolidation block already scheduled in the horizon) must not produce a message either. This is the batch's main safety surface: an unprompted "you may be overreaching" is a much stronger claim than a card on a screen he chose to open.<br>187.4 **Tests + DECISIONS.** A standing `active` pattern produces one message on the transition and nothing on subsequent days; a `clear` → `active` flip notifies while `active` → `active` does not; two transitions in one window yield one message, ranked; a transition whose underlying Reds 182 would have excluded produces nothing; a transition covered by an already-scheduled recovery block produces nothing; the message lands as an assistant turn Mark can reply to; the deterministic verdict, the floors and propose/confirm are untouched. | Make the coach notice things — a pattern flipping, an experiment concluding, a mix quietly going at risk — instead of computing them and waiting to be asked, without turning that into a daily stream of noise. | Unprompted messages fire on a *transition* in already-computed state (chronic suggestion status and action signal, experiment outcome, weekly-mix `at_risk`) and never on a standing level, so a persistent condition is mentioned once rather than daily; the previous-state mechanism is decided at `/batch-start` and adds a migration only if a persisted marker is chosen. All of it routes through Batch 185's coach-thread rail under an explicit budget of about one unprompted message per week, with a defined ranking when transitions collide. No message fires on evidence Batch 182 would have excluded from escalation (Decision #263's cause classifier and recovery-debt-with-intact-markers rule) or on a horizon where a recovery/taper/consolidation block is already scheduled. Each message is an assistant turn Mark can reply to and which still feeds the Batch 151 learning pipeline. The deterministic Green/Amber/Red ladder, the safety floors and the propose→approve→push rail are unchanged. Backend-only. Backend pytest/ruff/mypy green. Surfaced by Mark's 2026-08-04 request for proactive guidance. |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-08-05 — Full-app review wave 2 (five-lane audit) batch plan

Craig asked (2026-08-05) for a **fresh review of the app**. The previous wave
(Batches 153–156, 2026-07-25/26) is genuinely closed: its 21 findings were
remediated by 157–166 and the coaching-integrity findings by 167–171. Since that
wave's ship SHA `7e77169`: **79 commits, 227 files changed, +24,083 / −3,362,
migrations 022→026, 15 new backend modules, Batches 157–187** — more change than
the codebase held *at* the last audit.

**Why now, and why the lanes are re-cut.** The centre of gravity moved. At the
last review the app was a document generator (morning brief, post-workout read,
reviews). It now **initiates conversation and answers from live state**: Batch
178 (ask-time app state, 30k budget with named trims), 179 (one rolling
conversation, migration `026`, `services/coach_policy.py` as the floors module),
183 (local dates), and 184/185/187 — the 20:00 sleep nudge, the Sunday review
push, and unprompted state-change turns. That is a risk class nothing has looked
at: the coach saying something wrong, saying too much, or quietly contradicting
the deterministic verdict. Hence the new **Lane B** (188), which did not exist in
wave 1.

**Model — diagnose, then remediate** (unchanged from wave 1, Craig 2026-08-05).
Each review batch is **diagnose-only**: it produces a committed report doc in
`docs/reviews/` and ranks its findings; **no product code change lands inside a
review batch**. Every material finding becomes its own scoped remediation batch
afterward, triaged into a roadmap for Craig's approval before any code starts —
exactly as `reviews/BATCH_153-156_REMEDIATION_ROADMAP.md` sequenced R1–R9. A
review never edits verdict logic, Green/Amber/Red, #133/#135, or Red-never-VO2.

**Scope decision (Craig, 2026-08-05).** All five lanes; diagnose-only.

**Ordering.** Recommended **188 → 189 → 190 → 191 → 192** — the two repo-only
lanes first (they need nothing but `main`), then ops/security, then behaviour and
experience (which need a production data pull and the live app). The batches are
independent and any can be pulled forward without renumbering. `/batch-start`
assigns the DECISIONS number when each is actually started.

**Deliberate scoping.** 190/191/192 are **full-app** because the property each
judges is weakest-link or emergent — security is only as strong as the
least-guarded route, and coaching integrity and UX consistency arise from the
whole system. 189 is **diff-anchored** at `7e77169..HEAD` plus the engine
hotspots: an every-file read of 47k backend + 28k web LOC of shipped, CI-green
code is low-yield. 188 is full-app across the *prompt surface* specifically.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The lane's answer is that the whole-app property does not hold and three defects compound on one surface — the coach: `cn()` discards `fixed` in favour of the `relative` Batch 185 added in `368ef69`, so the launcher computes `position: relative; left: -16px` and sits off-viewport at the end of page flow (live in the deployed bundle, desktop included); the thread opens 27,992 px from its newest turn on an already-truncated 60-of-82-message window; and a failed thread fetch renders as "Nothing here yet", making 82 messages read as deleted. Batch 189's `CR189-01` is confirmed by execution — a `state_change` turn lights nothing — and 75 light-mode AA failures across 13/15 routes re-open `UX156-03` past Batch 163, whose guard stays green because it checks tokens in a CSS file rather than rendered pages. **Merged without a green CI run:** GitHub Actions was in a critical outage (githubstatus.com "Incident with Actions", 15:22 UTC) and all seven jobs were cancelled while queued; Craig authorised the merge on the grounds that the diff is docs-only. Compensating evidence: the Vercel preview built this tree, and local web `tsc` plus 382 vitest tests across 62 files passed. Production verified on the exact merge SHA `1f7507e` (Railway direct + Vercel same-origin health), database readiness `ok`, web `/` 200, protected coach-thread and daily-loop routes 401 direct and proxied, and the batch-specific smoke confirmed the freshly-rebuilt frontend chunk (`Layout-Dwq4Qqxf.js`) carries the launcher call byte-identical to what the review measured — proving the review changed nothing and that `UX192-01` is still live. Decision #273.** |

## Post-roadmap — 2026-08-06 — Batch 188–192 remediation roadmap (13 batches)

Craig approved the triage in [`docs/reviews/BATCH_188-192_REMEDIATION_ROADMAP.md`](reviews/BATCH_188-192_REMEDIATION_ROADMAP.md), which sequences the wave-2 audit's **67 findings** (12 High, 33 Medium, 22 Low; `CC188-*`, `CR189-*`, `DS190-*`, `CI191-*`, `UX192-*`) into 14 roadmap-local slots (`W0`–`W13`). Per the roadmap's own numbering discipline, `W0` is **not a batch** — it is a read-only production SQL query (CR189-06: does migration `022`'s coin-flip pairing hold a mislinked read or a resurrected skip in Mark's real history?) that can run alongside any other slot without a `/batch-start`. `W1`–`W13` are authored below as Batches 193–205, in roadmap order. Tier reflects the roadmap's suggested coding-agent model (🔴 High = Opus 5, 🟢 Mid = Sonnet 5/Haiku 4.5), not finding severity — several Medium-max slots (199/201/205's siblings W7/W9) are Opus-tier because the guard itself is the safety-critical product. DECISIONS numbers are assigned individually at each batch's own `/batch-start`, not here, per the numbering-collision lesson in [[reference_decisions_numbering_and_shared_dir_race]].

**Two standing zero-code decisions expire this weekend independently of any batch:** the 08-08/08-09 chronic-deload proposals (evidence withdrawn by CI191-03, addressed inside Batch 194) and DS190-01's always-on-vs-externalise-jobs call (gates half of Batch 195). Both are Craig's to make, not implementation.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 193 — The coach actually reaches Mark~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 193.1 **UX192-01.** `CoachLauncher.tsx:126` passes `'fixed … relative'` to `cn()`; tailwind-merge keeps the last position utility and discards `fixed`, so the launcher renders in normal flow off-viewport. Drop the redundant `relative` — a `fixed` element is already its own containing block for the unread-dot anchor Batch 185 added it for.<br>193.2 **UX192-02.** The thread opens at `scrollTop: 0` of a 28,380px pane; scroll to the newest turn on open and after each reply, and decide what the 60-message ceiling means against a real 82-message history with no "load older".<br>193.3 **UX192-03.** A failed thread fetch renders as "Nothing here yet" (no error branch in `CoachLauncher.tsx:78,149-153`); give `CoachConversation` an explicit status input with three distinct presentations and a retry.<br>193.4 **CR189-01 / UX192-04.** The unread predicate hard-codes `originKind === 'weekly_review'` so a `state_change` turn lights nothing; widen to role-and-recency (or an explicit complete allowlist) behind a shared constant in `packages/shared`, and bring `coachOriginKindSchema` + `ORIGIN_PROMPTS` up to `ORIGIN_KINDS` (backend 15 kinds vs. shared/client 14).<br>193.5 **UX192-06.** Append the user's turn optimistically and add a thinking affordance for the multi-second Anthropic call.<br>193.6 **Verification gate (UX192-10).** Before closing, force one weekly review into a disposable context and confirm turn written → dot lit → launcher visible → sheet opens on the new turn → week-ahead prose readable — this chain has never been exercised in front of a user. | Fix the only findings currently broken in production, all on one surface, before the first-ever Sunday weekly review (2026-08-09) lands on every one of them — this also retro-unblocks Batches 184–187's entire proactive-coaching output, currently undeliverable. | Assert the rendered DOM, not the class string: `getComputedStyle(button).position === 'fixed'` and in-viewport on a long page; rendered copy verified per status, not per component; a test that fails when the backend gains an origin kind the client does not know; the UX192-10 gate executed and confirmed before closeout. No migration. **Shipped 2026-08-15 via PR #226 / squash `104dd24`: dropped the redundant `relative` class (verified live via `getComputedStyle` — `position: fixed`, in-viewport, not the class string); the rolling thread scrolls to `scrollHeight` on open and after every reply (the 60-message window was already the most recent 60 — the bug was hiding its newest end, not the window); `CoachConversation` takes an explicit `loading`/`error`/`ready` status with retry; `coachOriginKindSchema`/`ORIGIN_PROMPTS` gain `state_change` (client now matches the backend's 15 `ORIGIN_KINDS`) and the unread predicate widens to a shared `PROACTIVE_COACH_ORIGIN_KINDS` constant, guarded by a new backend parity test; the composer shows an optimistic bubble + thinking indicator while pending. The UX192-10 chain was executed live in a real browser via a disposable, reverted dev-server mock (no prod contact): turn written → dot lit → launcher visible/fixed → sheet opens on the turn → prose readable → read-state persisted. An unrelated CI blocker (a newly-published high-severity `nanoid` advisory, transitive dev-tooling only) was fixed at closeout as a fourth reviewed exception in `scripts/audit_js_advisories.py`, approved by Craig. Local gates 840 passed / 362 expected PostgreSQL skips, Ruff check+format clean, mypy clean across 140 source files; branch, PR-context and post-merge CI all passed seven jobs with **1202 passed, 0 skipped** — every locally-skipped Postgres test really ran, including the twelve new ones, plus the real `upgrade head`/`downgrade base` round trip over `028`. Production verified on the exact merge SHA: Railway + Vercel health both `08e6859be8086a633b03893e8fe5634af7c011dc`, readiness `db: ok`, web `/` 200, protected `/api/v1/daily-loop` 401 direct and proxied. Batch smoke against the live database: `alembic_version` `028`, 475 rows (419 settled + 56 morning, 419 distinct days), old unique constraint gone, new unique + check constraints present, `phase` server default `NULL`, zero morning rows missing `recorded_at_utc` or carrying an empty `raw_payload` — and **2026-07-30, the audit's own headline case, now holds both observations**: `morning` MODERATE/64 with a 943-minute clock recorded 07:15, `settled` POOR/19 with 3233 minutes recorded 20:36. |

**One gotcha carried from the roadmap into every slot above.** Three separate wave-2 findings (UX192-01, UX192-05, CR189-11) shipped past green tests because the assertion never touched the thing it claimed to protect — a class string instead of a computed style, a token in a CSS file instead of a rendered node, a declared tuple instead of the SQL that ran. Where a slot's acceptance criterion is a property, assert the property, not its declaration.


## Post-roadmap — 2026-08-16 — The five deferred wave-2 items, specced (Batches 206–210)

With Batch 205 shipped the wave-2 roadmap's thirteen scheduled slots are complete and the
ledger had no unshipped rows. What remained were the five items the approved triage in
[`docs/reviews/BATCH_188-192_REMEDIATION_ROADMAP.md`](reviews/BATCH_188-192_REMEDIATION_ROADMAP.md)
put under *Deferred / accept-and-close*. Craig asked (2026-08-16) for all five to be
specced. **Specced is not scheduled:** the triage deferred three of them on value grounds
at a 1–2-user scale and that call still stands, so each row below carries the revisit
trigger the roadmap gave it. Authoring them now means that when a trigger fires the work is
ready rather than needing a fresh dig through three review documents.

**Two rows changed materially between the review and this authoring, because deployed state
was verified rather than transcribed** (the Batch 190 lesson):

* **Batch 206 is no longer expensive.** UX192-09 was graded Low and deferred on the
  assumption that slimming `/api/v1/daily-loop` meant splitting the endpoint. It does not:
  `DailyMetricOut.rawPayload` and `SleepOut.rawPayload` ship the whole Garmin blob to the
  browser on every call — measured in production at ~14 kB and ~22 kB stored per row,
  before JSON expansion — and **`rawPayload` has zero non-test readers anywhere in
  `apps/web/src`**. It is parsed by the shared zod schema and then never used. That makes
  the first phase a deletion, not an architecture change.
* **Batch 209's numbers moved.** DS190-05 reported 28/28 coach tables under RLS; live it is
  now **29/29** (`job_runs` arrived with Batch 195 / migration `027`). `relforcerowsecurity`
  is still **0 of 29** and every table is still owned by `postgres`, the Railway connection
  role, so the finding itself is unchanged — only its denominator.

DECISIONS numbers are **not** pre-assigned; each is allocated at its own `/batch-start`, per
the collision lesson recorded in `DECISIONS.md`. Tier reflects the suggested coding-agent
model, not finding severity.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 206 — The daily loop stops shipping what nothing reads~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 206.1 **UX192-09, first phase — delete the dead weight.** `DailyMetricOut.rawPayload` (`routers/daily_loop.py:965`) and `SleepOut.rawPayload` (`:997`) serialise the entire stored Garmin blob into every `/api/v1/daily-loop` response. Verified 2026-08-16: production rows average ~14 kB (`daily_metrics`) and ~22 kB (`sleep`) stored, and `grep -rn rawPayload apps/web/src` returns **zero** non-test matches — the field is validated by `packages/shared/src/schemas.ts` and never read. Drop it from both response models and from the shared schema, keeping the server-side readers (`routers/bedroom.py:248` `extract_hypnogram`, `morning_analysis` `_training_and_activity_fields`/`_extract_fitness_age`, `daily_metric_coverage`) which read the column directly and are unaffected.<br>206.2 **Re-measure before going further.** The review measured 5.4 s / 275,530 bytes against production. Re-measure after 206.1 and record the new figure in the batch row. **Decide at `/batch-start` on the strength of that number:** stop here, or continue into a hero-first split of the endpoint.<br>206.3 **Only if 206.2 says continue** — split the first-paint payload (verdict, today's actions, plan) from the long tail (the four post-analysis lists, briefs, projections) so Home and `/brief` paint before the rest arrives. Batch 62's `localStorage` persistence stays.<br>206.4 Tests: shared-schema round trip without `rawPayload`; a daily-loop response-shape assertion that fails if either blob is reintroduced; existing bedroom/hypnogram and morning-packet coverage stays green. | The morning path stops paying, on every cold load, for two blobs the browser never opens — and any further splitting is decided against a measured number rather than an assumption. | The morning path stops paying, on every cold load, for two blobs the browser never opens. **206.2 MEASURED AND DECIDED — stop at 206.1; 206.3 is not needed.** Measured against production on 2026-08-16: the two blobs serialise to **264,729 bytes on average** over the last 14 days (metric ~59 kB, sleep ~206 kB; peak 281 kB combined) against the review's measured 275,530-byte payload — **96.1% of it**. The earlier `pg_column_size` figures (~14 kB / ~22 kB) are TOAST-*compressed* storage; JSON text is what goes on the wire, and **there is no compression middleware anywhere in the API** (no `GZipMiddleware`, nothing in `vercel.json`), so the response really was served uncompressed. Removing the two fields takes `/api/v1/daily-loop` from ~275 kB to roughly **11 kB**, which resolves UX192-09 without touching the endpoint's shape — a hero-first split would now be optimising an 11 kB payload. Shipped as backend + shared-schema only: `DailyMetricOut.rawPayload` and `SleepOut.rawPayload` deleted from `routers/daily_loop.py` and from `dailyMetricSchema`/`sleepSchema` in `packages/shared/src/schemas.ts`. The columns are untouched and every server-side reader still works (`routers/bedroom.py` `extract_hypnogram`, `morning_analysis._training_and_activity_fields` / `_extract_fitness_age`, `daily_metric_coverage`) — they read the row directly. **Rollout is safe in both directions:** zod strips unknown keys, so a new client against an old server ignores the field, and the old client's schema had `.default({})`, so a cached bundle against the new server does not fail. Guards added because the regression is silent: two backend assertions that the field is absent from both response models (paired with a positive assertion that the neighbouring readings Home and the sleep page do render are still present), and a shared-schema test proving the parsed object has no `rawPayload` key even when the input contains one — zod's silent stripping means a regressed server would otherwise raise no error anywhere. **Noted, not taken:** `SleepOut.factorsJson` is also unread by the web but is ~1.1 kB and plausibly displayable later; and the absence of response compression is a real cross-cutting win left for its own batch. A deployed-bundle grep was tried first and rejected as an unsound smoke: the shared schema lives in a lazily-imported route chunk not linked from `index.html`, so the check found nothing even against the *pre-deploy* build and could never have distinguished before from after. |

Membership is **one-way**: the launcher sets `analysisId` only when replying to a weekly review (`CoachLauncher.tsx:93-101`) and `BriefChatService.ask` resolves an anchor only from an explicit `analysis_id`, never inferring one from origin (`services/brief_chat.py:381-397`), so a launcher question asked while standing on `/brief` is stored with `analysis_id = NULL` and can never appear in that brief's inline chat — though inline questions appear in both. The options rejected, recorded so they are not re-litigated: **(a) anchor by route** — same button behaving differently per screen; **(b) explain the relationship** — signposts the wrong-box trap instead of removing it. (a) **Anchor by route** — a launcher question asked on a route showing a read attaches to that read; membership becomes symmetric, at the cost of the same button behaving differently depending on where Mark stands. (b) **Explain the relationship** — leave membership alone, have the inline view state that it shows only this read's turns and link to the full thread; honest and least magic, but Mark must understand a distinction he never asked for. Ask whatever's on your mind."; the inline view says nothing). Note Batch 193 fixed the *error* state (UX192-03); the empty-state divergence is separate and still live.<br>207.4 Tests at the chosen shape: anchor resolution for a launcher question asked on an anchored route; heading consistency; both empty states. | Mark can tell whether he is in one conversation or several, and a question asked in the "wrong" box does not vanish from the read it was obviously about. | Mark can tell whether he is in one conversation or several, and a question asked in the "wrong" box does not vanish from the read it was obviously about. **Shipped: shape (c).** The three inline views and `BriefFollowUpChat` are deleted; the floating launcher is the only coach surface, so the two-headings problem and the one-way membership both dissolve rather than being signposted. **The stated cost was avoided, not paid:** new `contexts/CoachAnchorContext` lets a screen showing a read register it and the launcher anchor that screen's questions to it, so asking "why?" on the brief still reaches the coach with that brief attached — one box, one thread, context intact. An explicit weekly-review reply still wins over the page anchor. **Registrations are a stack, not a single value, and that was a real defect its own test caught:** a workout sheet opening over an already-anchored page cleared the anchor on close while the page's effect never re-ran to restore it, leaving a visible read unanchored; reading the top of a stack means closing the sheet uncovers the page again. Home anchors only while its analysis is expanded. Frontend-only; no backend, schema, migration, prompt, verdict, plan, auth or hosting change.
