The fix is moving `coach` to its own Supabase project, preferably its own organisation so billing is not shared. **Decide at `/batch-start`: do 208.1 only, or commit to the migration.**<br>208.3 **Only if 208.2 commits to the move** — rehearse export/import against a disposable target first, reusing Batch 196's `backup-drill` restore machinery and its same-host refusal guard; then cut over with credential rotation, `DATABASE_URL` session-mode port 5432 (never 6543), and exact-SHA health verification on both surfaces. Treat it as a rehearsed cutover with a rollback plan, not a config edit. | A `coach` security warning is never buried in an unrelated app's backlog, and the shared-project residual is either accepted deliberately or removed. | 208.1 ships standalone and is worth doing regardless. 208.3 only proceeds on an explicit decision, with a rehearsed restore and verified post-cutover health. **Trigger for 208.3:** another shared-quota incident, a second real user, or any move to paid infrastructure. |
| Batch 209 — RLS that constrains the application, not just the client | 🔴 High | Planned | 209.1 **DS190-05 — close the server-side bypass.** Verified live 2026-08-16: **29/29** coach tables have RLS enabled (28/28 at the review; `job_runs` arrived with migration `027`), **0 of 29** have `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, and all 29 are owned by `postgres` — the role FastAPI connects as. Client roles hold no coach grants, so the *external* boundary genuinely passes; what RLS does not currently do is constrain the application itself, so a missing `user_id` predicate in any FastAPI query can cross the two private users while RLS reads as "enabled".<br>209.2 **Two shapes, decided at `/batch-start`.** (a) **Least-privilege application login** — a non-owner role with explicit grants, exercised against every write path including the scheduler, the backup job and the migration runner. (b) **`FORCE RLS` plus a deliberate server policy and request-scoped identity** — narrower blast radius but needs a session-variable identity plumbed through `AsyncSessionLocal` and every background job that has no request.<br>209.3 Whichever shape: a migration, a connection-role rehearsal against a disposable database, documented exceptions for the scheduler/backup/migration paths, and a rollback plan. **Do not flip `FORCE` on production without all four.** Reuse `scripts/check_rls_posture.py` + `RLS_POSTURE_DATABASE_URL` for deployed-state verification, and Batch 204's recording-mock migration test technique for the SQL-level assertion — a `RLS_TABLES` constant is not proof, and CI's plain `postgres:16` has no `auth` schema so the enable-guards no-op there by design.<br>209.4 Tests: every write path under the constrained role; each documented exception exercised; a negative control proving a query missing its `user_id` predicate now fails rather than crossing users. | RLS protects against an application authorization defect, not only against PostgREST/client-role exposure. | Every write path passes under the constrained role, each exception is documented and exercised, and a deliberately unfiltered query is proved to fail. Migration + rehearsal + rollback plan all present. **Trigger:** a genuine second user, or any move to a least-privilege application login. At one user this remains the same deferral wave 1 made for DS154-06's second half. |
| Batch 210 — No pooled connection idles across a model call | 🟢 Mid | Planned | 210.1 **CR189-12 — shorten the lock's reach.** `reviews.py:709` takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` and the Anthropic call at `:725` runs inside the same transaction (confirmed still true 2026-08-16); `generation_requests.py:192` does the same through its `yield`. The connection, its transaction and the lock are all held for a 60-second-timeout external HTTP call — on the Supabase pooler in session mode, a pooled connection sitting idle-in-transaction.<br>210.2 Restructure so the lock covers the *claim* and the *write* but not the network call: take the lock, claim the work, commit; make the model call outside any transaction; reopen a transaction to record the result, re-checking the claim so a concurrent worker cannot double-write. Batch 198's `state-change` writer already demonstrates the claim-then-work shape and Batch 204/195's `generation_requests` lease is the existing durable-identity mechanism to build on — this is a restructure of existing pieces, not a new one.<br>210.3 Tests: a real two-session PostgreSQL regression proving the second worker observes the claim rather than duplicating the paid call, and that a crash between claim and record leaves a re-claimable state rather than a stuck lease. | A slow model call stops holding a pooled connection open, without weakening the guarantee that one review is generated once. | Two concurrent workers produce exactly one generation; no transaction spans the HTTP call; the crash-between-phases case is re-claimable. **Trigger:** connection-pool pressure on the Supabase pooler, or a second user. Recorded by the review as the correct trade at one user — this row exists so the fix is ready, not because it is due. |

**Why these five sat in Deferred.** The roadmap's disposition legend distinguishes *Do now*
from *Defer — real but low value at current 1–2-user scale; revisit on a trigger*. Nothing
below invalidates that reasoning; Batches 207 and 208.1 are the two that were never really
about scale (a product decision and a notification route), and Batch 206 turned out cheaper
than its grade assumed once deployed state was measured. 209 and 210 remain genuinely
trigger-gated and should not be scheduled ahead of user-facing work.

## Post-roadmap — 2026-08-17 — Coaching-integrity audit refresh (Batch 211)

Craig asked for an audit refresh once Batches 194 and 205 had shipped the two
findings Batch 191 said were blocking an A−. Diagnose-only, following the Batch
155/191 pattern: a full report in `docs/reviews/`, a Mark-safe scorecard beside
it, and a summary block appended to the running framework doc.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 211 — Coaching-integrity audit refresh~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | 211.1 Re-run the framework's two lenses against the current engine, applying Batch 191's own kickoff rule — a remediation counts as closed only when observed working on **real data**.<br>211.2 Answer the live question: are CI191-01 (Batch 194) and CI191-02 (Batch 205) closed, and does the grade move off B+?<br>211.3 Prong A — drive the real functions with real recorded inputs; Prong B — read-only SQL over the `coach` schema.<br>211.4 Report + Mark-safe scorecard + framework summary block. | Establish, on evidence rather than on the presence of code, whether the two Highs are closed — and say plainly what the observation window can and cannot support. | **Grade moved to A− (from B+)** — the first refresh where it moved. **CI191-02 closed and *observed***: the single wake sync since Batch 205 shipped (2026-08-17) wrote today's `morning` row and re-synced closed days into `settled` rows without touching a wake row; 08-15 holds `69/MODERATE/1min` at wake against `26/LOW/2849min` settled, a 43-point readiness and 2,848-minute gap on a day the coaching called moderate. **CI191-01 closed and *proved***: driving the real `_qualify_red_morning` with the actual 2026-08-07 Red (`alcohol`, HRV 43 vs floor 45, RHR 47 vs ceiling 45) — which the old rule excused — now returns `acute_cause_with_systemic_strain` and counts; all four of Batch 194.2's bounds are independently demonstrable (physiology contradicts, endogenous tags never excuse, decay after a few days, cap of one). Not yet fired live because no Red has occurred since 2026-08-08, which predates the fix — **that reservation is the only thing between A− and A**. **CI191-08 closed**: all five deterministic protections are now in `coach_policy.FLOORS` (10 floors, each with a failing negative control). The spoken layer has started speaking (`state_change` 0 → 1, newest weekly review 2026-06-29 → 2026-08-16, user turns 41 → 92). Verdict distribution reversed 48% → 71% → 75% → **59% Green** (19/5/8 over 32 reads), the hardening working as intended. **One new finding, `CI211-01` (Low):** sixteen `workout_delivery_proposals` sit at `proposed` and every one is for a workout date already past (27 Jun → 13 Aug), none ever pushed, with no expiry path — hygiene rather than a live defect, since the daily loop looks proposals up by `planned_workout_id` and they cannot surface against a current session. **Observation window stated as a first-class caveat:** Batch 194 had two mornings and Batch 205 exactly **one**, against Batch 191's twelve, so every claim is labelled *observed*, *proved* or *implemented*. Diagnose-only — no product code, prompt, threshold, verdict, plan, proposal, migration, config or production row changed. Production verified on the exact merge SHA `e54f60f51bfc5144b30960c41c618dfff7edc8f4` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, readiness `db: ok`, web `/` 200, protected `/api/v1/daily-loop` 401 proxied. **The diagnose-only boundary is evidenced rather than asserted:** `git diff --name-only origin/main...` returned **zero** non-`.md` files before the merge, so there is no functional surface for a behavioural smoke to test — the health/readiness/route checks confirm the deploy is intact and unchanged, which is the whole claim for a review batch. |


## Post-roadmap — 2026-08-19 — Mark feedback 2026-08-08/13/14/15 + the Claude-integration review (Batches 212–220)

Craig relayed six points of Mark's feedback (2026-08-08, 08-13, 08-14 ×1, 08-15 ×2 — sent
before Batches 206–211 shipped but reconciled here against current `main`), then asked
whether the way the app talks to the Claude API is optimal. Both threads are authored
together because the review changed the priority order of the feedback: **five of the six
points are ordinary defects in deterministic Python and cost nothing in Claude spend.** Only
the sixth (the room-temperature question) needs a capability the app does not have.

**Every claim below was verified against production, not transcribed from the reports.**
Three of the six root causes turned out to be different from what the in-app coach told Mark
when he challenged it:

* **The "illness context" is the word *cold*.** `_CHECK_IN_CAUSE_PATTERNS["illness"]`
  (`chronic_patterns.py:69`) contains `\b(?:head|chest\s+)?cold\b` — the optional group means
  the bare word matches. Driving the real dict against Mark's actual 08-14 note ("*more awake
  from this time as felt cold from drafts*") and his 08-15 note ("*backfired as too cold*")
  returns `['illness']` for **both**. He was describing his bedroom, and the classifier read
  it as sickness. This collides head-on with point 3: the more he reports on room
  temperature, the more the app thinks he is ill.
* **The VO2 "2-session target" is a stale row, not a hardcoded number.** `target =
  len(in_bucket)` (`weekly_mix.py:206`) counts rows, and the query filters only `is_active`
  (`:292`). Production for 2026-08-11 holds **three** `bike_vo2` rows for one session: v1
  `skipped`/`is_active=true`, v2 `planned`/`is_active=false`, v3 `completed`/`is_active=true`.
  The supersede path deactivated v2 but left the skipped v1 active, so `in_bucket` is 2 and
  `done` is 1 — exactly the string Mark saw. His own note records the origin: "*I accidentally
  skipped this day in app. I reinstated a vo2 workout.*"
* **The Red-day cut Mark experienced never went through `verdict_scaling`.** The 08-08 rows
  are v1 45 min (`plan_no2_import`), v2 **37 min**, v3 45 min completed — and **all three have
  no `origin` and no `adjustment`** in `structured_workout`, so `adjust_ir_for_verdict` did
  not author any of them. The v2 work block went 35 min → 1575 s (26.25 min) = **×0.75, the
  *Amber* scale**, applied via `source=interval_editor`, with the fixed 5+5 warm-up/cool-down
  diluting it to an 18% overall cut. So the brief's "50%" and the delivered ride's 37 minutes
  came from different code paths — the same fragmentation Batch 173.2 fixed for Amber, still
  live for Red.

The remaining two were confirmed as reported. The **sprint misread is observed in the stored
packet**, not inferred: every interval carries `boundarySource: planned_durations` (the
fallback — both alignment paths declined), a 12-second sprint holds **6 samples** (~1 sample /
2 s, against `TRACE_ALIGNMENT_BIN_SEC = 5`), there is no `maxPowerWatts` field anywhere in
`_build_interval` even though `maxHeartRateBpm` exists, `_adherence` has **no** duration or
sample guard while `_fade`/`_hr_drift_pct` correctly refuse below 180 s (`:570`), and
`prescribedErgMode` is **null** despite the key existing — which is why Mark had to write
"*ride was done in erg mode throughout*" in his own note. The cheapest tell is internal: the
recovery valleys recorded **63.5–68.6% FTP against the sprints' 54.1–54.6%**, so the work
intervals read *below* the rests they sit between. And **Body Battery drain** is a one-line
gap: `body_battery_drained` exists on `DailyMetric` (`coaching.py:88`) and is populated
**109/109** settled days since 1 May (mean **63.8**, range 31–89), but is absent from the
eight baseline keys in `metric_baselines.sample_values` (`:94-112`). Mark called his drain of
60 "normal" and he was right — the app simply never told him.

**What the Claude-integration review found, and why it demoted itself.** The boundary is a
hand-rolled `httpx.post` (`anthropic_text.py:150`) sending four keys — `model`, `max_tokens`,
`system`, `messages`. No `anthropic` SDK in the project; `cache_control` appears **zero**
times in the codebase; no tools, `thinking`, `output_config`, structured outputs or
streaming. Model is `claude-sonnet-4-6` at `max_tokens: 4096`. Measured in production, the
morning packet is **46 kB avg / 57 kB max ≈ 13,000 input tokens** (knowledge base ~24% of
it), and chat re-sends its whole prefix on every turn across an average **5.3 messages per
thread** (197 messages / 37 threads).

The finding that reordered everything: **Mark's entire sleep-and-recovery history — all 485
nights, every analytical column plus his own free-text notes — is 44 kB ≈ 12,700 tokens,
smaller than a single morning packet.** The app spends more tokens each morning describing
one day than it would cost to send every night Mark has ever recorded, and it is using ~1.3%
of Sonnet's 1M context window. The constraint was never context, cost or latency; the history
was simply never sent. That makes Batch 220 — no tools, no SDK change, no agent loop, just a
different packet at a different cadence — the best value in the whole review at **~$0.18 a
run via the Batch API**, against a current total spend of roughly $8–15/month.

**Prior art that must not be re-specced.** Batches 178/179 (2026-07-30) already answered the
earlier "*almost disconnected*" report: 178 shipped an ask-time `app_state`
(`brief_chat.py:530`) so the chat is no longer a frozen packet, and 207 later collapsed the
per-page chats into one coach. What remains from that complaint is narrower and is what
Batches 217–218 address: Claude still cannot say **how** a conclusion was reached, and it
re-pays for its own prefix every turn.

**Tools and the agent loop are deliberately not authored here** — see *Future enhancements*
below for the reasoning and the trigger. DECISIONS numbers are **not** pre-assigned; each is
allocated at its own `/batch-start`, per the collision lesson in `DECISIONS.md`. Tier
reflects the suggested coding-agent model, not severity.

| Batch | Tier | Status | Phases | Goal | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~Batch 212 — The check-in classifier stops diagnosing illness from the weather~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 212.1 **Mark's point (2026-08-14).** The morning brief carried a line about illness context being logged and pending review, with zero verdict impact. He never reported illness, it appeared nowhere else, and when he asked the coach directly it could not account for it. Root cause proved: `\b(?:head\|chest\s+)?cold\b` in `_CHECK_IN_CAUSE_PATTERNS["illness"]` (`chronic_patterns.py:69`) matches the bare word.<br>212.2 Narrow the pattern so a bedroom-temperature report is never an illness signal — require an illness-bearing qualifier (`head cold`, `chest cold`, `streaming cold`, `come down with`) and drop bare `cold`. Audit the other four cause families in the same dict for the same class of over-match (`\bill(?:ness)?\b`, `\bsick\b`, `travel`'s `\bholiday\b`) rather than fixing only the reported one.<br>212.3 **Stop the brief narrating a zero-impact internal signal at all.** The line was self-defeating by its own admission ("zero impact on today's verdict … pending human review"). Either suppress structural/pending-proposal plumbing from user-facing copy, or give it enough provenance to be explainable — Batch 217 supplies the mechanism; this phase decides which side of that line the tag sits on.<br>212.4 Tests: Mark's **verbatim** 08-14 and 08-15 notes must classify as no acute cause (both currently return `['illness']`); a set of genuine illness phrasings must still match; a negative control per cause family. | A note about a cold room never becomes a medical record, and any structural signal that does reach the brief can explain itself when challenged. | The two real notes classify clean, true-illness phrasings still match, and every cause family carries a negative control. The brief no longer emits a signal it cannot account for. **This is the highest-trust-per-line fix in the wave:** it is the one failure where the coach told Mark something false about his health and then could not say why. **Shipped via PR #242 / squash `9f72e4f`.** All sixteen PR checks and post-merge `main` CI green (**1224 passed, 0 skipped** against a 1204 baseline; locally 862 / 362 expected PostgreSQL skips). **Two findings during the build raised the severity beyond the spec:** the bogus tags *accumulate* (one row 08-14, a second 08-15, both live on 08-16 inside the 28-day window), and `illness` reaches `_qualify_red_morning`'s acute branch, so a Red with intact physiology could have been excused — bounded by Batch 194, but never merely cosmetic. Auditing all five families found a second over-match (`drinking`/`drank` tagged "drinking plenty of water"). `PROMPT_VERSION` v30 → `morning-analysis-v31-2026-08-19`, which is the regeneration cache key rather than a label. Production verified on the exact merge SHA `9f72e4fe9a02f228dceb805cc791946883bdf691` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, web `/` 200, protected daily-loop 401 proxied. **The batch smoke is a read-only introspection of the running container** (`railway ssh --service api`), which is the only honest test for a change with no unauthenticated surface: the *deployed* classifier returns `()` for both of Mark's verbatim notes, still returns `('illness',)` for "streaming head cold" and "caught a cold", returns `()` for "drinking plenty of water" and `('alcohol',)` for "drinking wine", reports `morning-analysis-v31-2026-08-19`, and confirms both the new gate and the surviving never-soften rule in the live prompt. Decision #290. |
| ~~Batch 213 — The weekly mix counts sessions, not rows~~ | 🟢 Mid | Shipped | 213.1 **Mark's point (2026-08-15):** "*Not sure where you're getting this from 'VO2 has 1 of a 2-session target done' — There is only ever 1 vo2 session in my weekly mix.*" He is right; the second row is a ghost.<br>213.2 **Fix the data-integrity half.** The 08-11 chain left v1 `skipped` with `is_active=true` while v2/v3 superseded it. Find the supersede path that deactivates a replaced `planned` row but not a replaced `skipped` one, and close it. Repair the live 08-11 row read-modify-write (`railway run`, the Batch 152 pattern) — **not** by hand-editing production without an audit trail.<br>213.3 **Fix the accounting half independently**, because correct data should not be the only thing standing between Mark and a wrong denominator. `summarize_weekly_mix` must count *sessions*, not rows: dedupe by (date, bucket) or by version chain, and decide explicitly whether a `skipped` row may ever contribute to `target` (it should not — a skipped session is not a plan commitment).<br>213.4 Check the same shape on 08-13 and 08-15, where superseded rows *are* correctly `is_active=false`, so Z2/Sweet-Spot targets are currently right by luck rather than by rule.<br>213.5 Tests: the real 08-11 three-row chain must yield `target=1, done=1`; a skip-then-reinstate lifecycle asserted end to end; a pure-reducer case with duplicate rows per bucket. | Mark is never shown a weekly target his plan does not contain, and never marked partially compliant for a session he completed. | The 08-11 fixture yields `target=1, done=1`; the supersede path leaves exactly one active row per session slot; the reducer is proved robust to duplicates even when the data is wrong. **Shipped via PR #243 / squash `1d3cac6`.** Both spec facts (the exact 08-11 row shape; 08-13/08-15 already correct) were re-verified against live production before building and held exactly as written. The supersede gap was `swap_day`'s target-date lookup via `_active_workout_on`, which deliberately excludes `skipped` rows and so never deactivated one left behind — fixed with a new unfiltered `_active_occupants_on` query so `swap_day` now deactivates every active occupant on both dates, not just the chosen swap partners. `summarize_weekly_mix` dedupes to one session per `workout_date` and a skipped-only date contributes nothing to `target`. | ~~Batch 214 — A 12-second sprint is graded on evidence that exists~~ | 🔴 High | Shipped | **Corrected 2026-08-19 at `/batch-start`, before any code.** Every phase below was re-verified by replaying the real 08-13 ride through the real `segment_ride_intervals`; the replay reproduces the stored packet's 24 intervals value-for-value. Two premises were wrong and one root cause was missing entirely. Original wording kept in git history.<br>214.1 **Mark's point (2026-08-13) — verified true, and independently checkable from data the app already stored.** The app reported his 6 sprints at 54–65% FTP against a 185% target. Garmin's own activity summary records `max_power_watts` = **519 W** for that ride, and the trace holds six clean bursts peaking at 515 / 512 / 519 / 509 / 510 / 514 W — 182–185% of his 280 W FTP. He delivered all six.<br>214.0 **The root cause, absent from the original spec: a pause desynchronises the segmentation clock.** `_sample_time` (`ride_intervals.py:619`) reads `elapsed_sec` — Garmin's `sumElapsedDuration`, which *includes* paused time — while `_planned_windows` (`:288`) builds a cumulative clock from the IR's step durations, which is timer time. The 08-13 trace has exactly one gap: at sample 755, `elapsed_sec` jumps 1508 → 1618 (**110 s**) while `moving_duration_sec` advances 1507 → 1508. Every planned window after that pause is 110 s ahead of the executed content. The Z2 block hides it (65% either side of every boundary); the 12 s sprints are the one place where the target changes faster than the offset, which is why this surfaced there and only there. **Blast radius: 31 of 57 rides since 1 May carry a pause > 30 s** (mean 135 s, max 501 s), including *every* structured ride in August — 08-19 (104 s), 08-16 (105 s), 08-15 (84 s), 08-13 (109 s), 08-09 (200 s). Every one is currently graded against shifted windows. `moving_duration_sec` is populated on 100% of samples on all of them (`garmin_sync.py:664`, `sumMovingDuration`), and its max for 08-13 is 3479 s against the IR's 3480 s total — an exact match. Segment on the pause-excluding clock. **Coupled change:** `_actual_lap_windows` (`:310`) prefers each lap's `elapsedDuration` over `duration`; that preference pairs with the elapsed sample clock and must flip with it.<br>214.2 **Add the missing metric — stands, but not for the stated reason.** `_build_interval` (`:238-239`) computes `avgPowerWatts` → `pctFtp` → `adherence` and there is no `maxPowerWatts`, though `maxHeartRateBpm` exists (`:256`) — confirmed. A 515 W peak is unrepresentable, so Mark's argument cannot be answered from the data model. Peak is also the physically right statistic: a 12 s sprint's *mean* includes its acceleration ramp, and at the true bursts the mean is 461–494 W against peaks of 509–519 W. Measured on the fixed clock, peak over each sprint window reads 178–182% (nominal window) and **182–185% once the window carries a small boundary slack** — flat from ±6 s to ±30 s, so it is a plateau, not a tuned number.<br>214.3 **Premise wrong; reframed.** The claim was that the app "lacked the resolution to measure". It did not: 6 samples at ~2 s each over a 12 s sprint is ample, and on the corrected clock those same 6 samples read 500+ W. **A sample-count floor would suppress a grade the app can now get exactly right.** The real evidentiary gap is *boundary confidence*: even on the fixed clock a residual ~8 s offset remains against a 12 s effort, so a planned-clock window cannot be trusted to sit on a short effort. Guard on that — boundary source plus the ratio of boundary uncertainty to step duration — not on sample count. Confirmed true as written: `_adherence` (`:557`) has no floor while `_fade`/`_hr_drift_pct` correctly refuse below `FADE_MIN_DURATION_SEC` (`:570`), and `TRACE_ALIGNMENT_BIN_SEC = 5` is coarser than half a sprint. **Also confirmed: the existing DTW rescue cannot save this ride.** `_trace_aligned_windows` bails at `:348` because the planned clock's RMSE is 6.85 %FTP, under the 8.0 threshold — a ride that is 97% steady-state looks well-fitted in aggregate while being catastrophically wrong on the 2% that matters. Forcing the DTW path past the bail-out was measured: it declines on both clocks. This is not a threshold-tuning problem.<br>214.4 **Stands, and is the best line in the spec — verified both ways.** A work interval recording below its adjacent recovery is a segmentation failure, not a performance result. On the real packet the guard fires on **6 of 6** sprints (54.1–54.6% against recoveries at 63.5–68.6%); on the pause-corrected clock it fires on **0 of 6**. It is a live detector, not a permanent suppressor. Suppress the grade and say so.<br>214.5 **Premise wrong — the app already knows, and knowing did not help.** `prescribedErgMode` is *prescribed* ERG mode, set only by `vo2_progression` to `"off"` for surge protocols; it was `null` because this session came from `today_card_swap`. But Mark did **not** have to supply ERG himself: `profile.athleteProfile.indoorTrainerMode` is fully populated in the KB (`mode: "erg"`, `prescribedPowerIsDelivered: true`, `neverSuggestErgOff: true`, explicitly covering his sprint sessions), and the prompt already instructs the model to treat prescribed %FTP as delivered. **The model overrode all of it because the packet handed it six intervals hard-stamped `adherence: "under"`** — a deterministic grade beats a prompt instruction. Worse, it *confabulated a mechanism* to justify the number: "the ERG trainer held the locked Z2 base load during the sprint windows", "ERG hadn't released before the boundary", "confirming ERG held base load". None of that happened. The same output contradicted itself two sections later with "2.1% Z6 (sprint peaks)", computed from the whole trace and therefore immune to the bug. **The lesson replaces the phase, and the phase is dropped:** do not add another advisory field for the model to weigh — stop emitting the false fact. Populating `prescribedErgMode` for this session would be actively harmful, not merely useless: the value `"off"` triggers the prompt's `single_honest_note_when_erg_ridden_on_erg_off_surge_protocol` rule, which would have the coach tell Mark ERG smoothed his surges — false for this ride, where the prescribed 515 W was delivered on all six. Deliberately unchanged; recorded here so the next session does not re-spec it.<br>214.6 Tests: the real 08-13 trace, as a committed fixture, must not grade the six sprints "under"; a pause-desynchronised trace regression that fails on the old clock; peak power present and peak-graded for a short sprint; the work-below-recovery control fires on the pre-fix data and not on the post-fix data; a long steady interval still grades on mean as today; lap-window selection asserted against the flipped duration preference. | The app never tells Mark he under-performed an effort it lacked the resolution to measure — and when it cannot measure, it says so. | The recorded 08-13 sprints stop grading "under"; segmentation survives a mid-ride pause; peak power is present; the grade refuses when boundary uncertainty is comparable to the effort's length; the work-below-recovery guard fires on the real pre-fix data and clears on the fixed data. **This and Batch 213 are the same failure class:** the coach asserting a standard Mark was never set and then marking him down against it. **Batch 214 adds a third: the model will invent a physical mechanism to rationalise a wrong deterministic fact, so a false fact in the packet is worse than a missing one** — which is the strongest argument yet for Batch 217. **Shipped via PR #244 / squash `f92d6b7`.** All sixteen PR checks and post-merge `main` CI green (**1237 passed, 0 skipped** against a 1224 baseline; locally 873 / 364 expected PostgreSQL skips). Production verified on the exact merge SHA `f92d6b74d7de63066aca4fbc02dfa8951e4416e8` — Railway direct and Vercel same-origin health both served it, readiness `db: ok`, web `/` 200, protected daily-loop 401 direct and proxied. **The batch smoke is two-sided.** A read-only introspection of the running container drives the *deployed* segmentation with the real 08-13 shape: `clockSource=moving_duration_sec`, `pausedSec=110`, all six sprints `on` at 183.9% on 515 W peaks; the wall-clock control (same trace, moving column removed) returns six withheld grades with `ungradedCount=6 / underCount=0`, proving the guard is live rather than merely present. On the frontend the baseline was taken **before** the merge and could see the old state (`Interval execution` 1, all three new strings 0); after deploy both hashes changed (`index-BU_lH9pf` → `index-D3-IK_yV`, `DashboardPage-DtPP2tqU` → `DashboardPage-C3dUEEEZ`) and "could not place this window on the effort", "Not measured" and "% peak" each read 1. **No production repair** (Craig's call at `/batch-start`, on Batch 212's principle that a stored analysis records what the coach actually said). Decision #292. |

The only Red substitution ever *pushed* (08-01, 29 min) was a ride carrying 185% FTP work — the case 215.4 protects.<br>215.2 **Verified exactly as written.** `RED_DURATION_SCALE = 0.5` (`verdict_scaling.py:38`) with `ease=None` (`:159`) is a blanket half-duration cut plus a `RECOVERY_CAP_PCT = 60` cap; Amber routes through `ease_amber_power_pct` with `ENDURANCE_CEILING_PCT = 75` so a Z2 ride keeps its intensity and only loses duration; Red has no equivalent. **The measured harm on 08-08:** the stored packet reads `plannedWorkPowerPct: 62` → `adjustedWorkPowerPct: 60`, `plannedDurationMin: 45` → `adjustedDurationMin: 22` — an already-Zone-2 ride halved *and* pushed below Zone 2, with `intensityHeldAtEndurance` hardcoded to Amber so the read could never say the intensity survived. **The module docstring states the current behaviour is deliberate**, so this reverses half of Decision #61: **append a new DECISIONS entry, do not silently edit the old one.**<br>215.3 **Premise wrong; the defect is real but sits somewhere else.** The row said "all three 08-08 rows have no `origin`/`adjustment`, so the canonical transform never ran". Those are `planned_workouts` rows, whose `structured_workout` never carries either key on *any* day — they are IR-level annotations on proposals. **The canonical transform did run:** proposal `54100516…`, created 07:17:23 on 08-08, `origin: red_substitution`, `adjustment: {verdict: Red, durationScalePct: 50, powerCapPct: 60}`, 45 → 22 min, with its `red-regen` audit row — and the brief quoted it exactly ("*cut to 22 minutes at 60% FTP (50% duration scale)*"). It was never approved or pushed. `adjust_ir_for_verdict` is *already* the Red path at `executable_coaching.py:239` and `:574`, so building this phase as written would have rewired a path that is correct. **The real fragmentation is the interval editor.** `scale_block` is verdict-blind — always `AMBER_DURATION_SCALE` and `ease_amber_power_pct`, applied to the primary block only — and the router returns it as `changeTo` while the UI pre-selects it (`useState('scale')`). On a Red morning the editor therefore *opened pre-filled with the Amber adjustment*: 2100 s × 0.75 = 1575 s, and with the read-only 5+5 ramps absorbing none of the cut, 45 min → 36. **Mark approved the number the app offered him.** Make the editor's pre-filled block today's own verdict adjustment, landing on the transform's *total* so both quote one figure.<br>215.4 **Verified and untouched.** `blocks_red_vo2`, the `never_vo2_on_red` policy floor and the intensity ceilings are unchanged: intensity-aware *duration* is the change. A ride carrying anything above the Zone-2 ceiling can never be "already endurance", so it always takes the recovery substitution.<br>215.5 **Gate on combined load, per the coach's own caveat to Mark** — and the case is live: 08-08 also held a 15-minute strength session. With another session on the day the endurance allowance is withdrawn and Red reverts to the recovery substitution. The fact is resolved by the callers, which already load the day's plan; the transform stays pure.<br>215.6 Tests: the 08-08 case as a regression fixture; an already-endurance ride on Red keeps its intensity and takes a materially lighter duration cut; a VO2 ride on Red is still substituted and still blocked; brief-quoted and delivered durations asserted equal on every branch; the editor's pre-filled block asserted equal to the transform's total. | On a Red morning the hard work is still cut, the easy work that helps Mark sleep is not gutted, and the brief and the ride finally agree on one number. | An endurance ride on Red keeps intensity with a light duration change; VO2-on-Red is unchanged and still blocked; `summarize_verdict_adjustment`, the delivered row and the editor's pre-filled change quote the same figure; a new DECISIONS entry records the reversal and why. **Three shapes were Craig's call at `/batch-start`:** `RED_ENDURANCE_DURATION_SCALE = 0.85` (45 → 38 min, so Red still says materially "less" without deleting the sleep benefit) over holding full duration or reusing Amber's ×0.75; a verdict-aware editor *default* (with "Scale down" kept as an explicit generic preset) over adding a second button; and resolving the combined-load fact at the call sites over widening the pure transform's signature. **Shipped via PR #245 / squash `a651a96`.** All sixteen PR checks and post-merge `main` CI green (**1258 passed, 0 skipped** against a 1237 baseline; locally 891 / 367 expected PostgreSQL skips). **215.1/215.2/215.4 were re-verified and held exactly; 215.3's central premise did not, and the row above was corrected before a line of code was written** — the canonical Red transform *had* run on 08-08 and the brief quoted it exactly, so the fix moved to the interval editor, whose verdict-blind preset the UI pre-selects and whose 36-minute default Mark approved. Red now splits on the ride: already-Zone-2 holds its intensity and takes `RED_ENDURANCE_DURATION_SCALE = 0.85` (45 → 38 min); anything harder, or any day carrying another session, keeps the original half-duration/60%-cap substitution unchanged. The deployed editor returns 2295 s on Red, equal to the transform's total, and nothing at all on an unknown verdict. Both new frontend strings are present in `DashboardPage-BD78A-qr.js`, with the grep's reach validated by the pre-existing `Scale down`/`Keep current` labels resolving to the same chunk. **No production repair** — the 08-08 read records what the coach actually said (Batch 212's principle); every Red from here is adjusted correctly.
